# Inbox

The Sammelbecken: every topic and idea that still needs handling. Low friction — drop a row,
refine later. Status flow: `inbox → refining → refined → planned → in-progress → done`.
When a topic is refined, its ideas move into durable state under [`features/`](features/),
[`crosscutting/`](crosscutting/), [`decisions/`](decisions/) (+ `contracts/`) and the row's status advances.

> **A row at `inbox` has no prior work behind it — only a title.** These rows were once pointers into a
> previous implementation that has since been deleted; what they pointed at is gone. Several are large
> (the product vision, goals & stakeholders, legal, search, journal). Refining one means **deciding it
> fresh, with the user** — not recovering it.

> Single-file by choice. It is already large; once it needs scrolling to navigate, split by area (one file
> per area) rather than let it rot.

## V1 — the line

Planning lives here by convention ([docs/README](README.md) § use-case), so the release scope does too. The
*system's* goals and quality bars are not this — they are [architecture](architecture/README.md) §1 and §10,
and this section is downstream of them.

**V1 is self-hosted.** No public instance operated by us. The audience is **one person who hosts for themselves
and their circle** — technical enough for `docker compose` **or for `helm install` on a cluster they already
run** (decided 2026-09-09: Kubernetes **gates** V1, it is not post-V1 scope —
[ADR 0064](decisions/0064-kubernetes-helm-deployment-target.md), whose "alongside — not instead of" describes
the two targets and was never a statement about timing) — and the same human as the operator. Share-link
recipients are *guests*, not audience: they must not be overwhelmed, but nothing is built to win them over.

That single decision settles most of the scope below: with no central operator there is nobody to moderate for,
nobody to verify email addresses against, and nobody to sign a service promise.

**In V1** — each row keeps its own status where it lives; this list only draws the line.

| Row | Why it is in |
|---|---|
| ~~**#113** key recovery~~ **DONE** | Half of the durability bar in [architecture](architecture/README.md) §10. The `RECOVERY` wrap was written at account birth and withheld server-side with **no release path** — unreachable, while the published wiki promised it. The wrap now has its path; the other half of that bar (#26/#30) does not. |
| **#26** deployment + operations (**#30** dissolved into it 2026-08-06) | Under self-hosting these *are* the product, and the other half of the durability bar: a backup nobody documented is not a backup. **Backup/restore is now built** — [ADR 0056](decisions/0056-instance-durable-state-and-cold-logical-backup.md) + [uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance.md) decided it; `scripts/instance_backup.py`, [infra/README](../infra/README.md) § Backup & restore, `infra/docker-compose.prod.yml` (leg (c) on a named volume, guarded by `scripts/validate_config_secret_persistence.py`) and two grouped web e2e rehearsals now carry it. **One act is all this still owes — and the two other items below stopped being debts without this row noticing (corrected 2026-08-18, measured against `main` @ `4e954ea`).** (1) *Publish the repository.* The flip it used to gate is done: on 2026-09-24 [ADR 0040](decisions/0040-coverage-gaps-and-http-not-a-surface.md)'s note on which rows a run has admitted counted `web-e2e` as a row a pre-import run had executed, so the two rehearsals are cited on its declared run and architecture §10 reads *held*; the use-case stays `planned` only for the criteria no surface reaches. What publishing still gates is the first run of `admin-web`'s and `packages/crypto`'s jobs, whose suites wait under `gaps`. It is the owner's call, which is why it is a row here and not a CR phase. (2) An **upgrade runbook** — ADR 0056 §6 makes the backup its precondition, not its author. **No longer blocked:** a release now names itself from the tree, `v<major>.<minor>` is created by the publish, and the manifest records the release a backup came from, so an upgrade finally has both a target and a *stateable* rollback target ([ADR 0057](decisions/0057-release-identity-and-deployment-recipe.md), built 2026-08-11). **The runbook itself is BUILT** — [infra/README](../infra/README.md) § *An upgrade, step by step*, seven steps, and `wiki/en/running-your-instance.md` announces it. What is still owed is the **first publish**, which is item (1) — not a second debt: the runbook's own text records that only step 2 has ever executed, and that condition is the missing remote. (3) The **unreferenced-blob sweep** — **not owed; it stays here as the decision, not as a debt.** The *other* half of [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-blob-storage-presigned-direct-to-store.md) §3's GC sentence, **deferred on a measurement, not on effort**. The set it would act on is *empty by construction*: blob rows are created only by `MediaStore.persistBlob`, reached only from `attach` and `commitCover`, both `@Transactional` and both writing the reference in the same transaction — and no delete or update path removes a reference while leaving the blob (`commitCover`/`clearCover` each delete the old blob row before reassigning). The job as promised would therefore ship with a Definition of done of *"finds nothing"*, carrying its whole blast radius on the one reference arm no foreign key protects. So the clauses promising it in ADR 0013 §3 and in `en.memory.media` E3 are **deleted, not deferred**: both sentences are about *abandoned uploads*, an abandoned upload never becomes a blob row, and `UploadSessionPurge` satisfies them alone. **Its precondition is now a guard rather than a memory** — `scripts/validate_blob_reference_arity.py` pins which arms exist and which of them the one live predicate over them already enumerates; read its docstring before designing this, and do not re-derive the arity by hand. **The one constraint that guard cannot carry:** the writers leave no window, but a *reader* collecting the reference sets **before** it reads `veyq_blob` sees a row whose reference committed in between. Read `veyq_blob` first, the reference sets after — a read-ordering rule, **not** a retention window, and no grace period substitutes for it. **Two neighbouring leaks are not this sweep's job and never will be:** #131 (orphaned media rows — still legitimately referenced, so this predicate correctly skips them) and #130 (orphaned store objects — no row at all, so no predicate over `veyq_blob` can see them). |
| ~~**#111** device & session manager~~ **DONE** | A lost device can be cut off from the device still in hand — all four slices built 2026-08-12: the session list + remote revoke (A), the sealed device register that names them (B), the device↔wrap join behind "sign out and remove this device's saved key" (C), and E3's locked entry point on the sign-in form. The last one is what makes the row's promise reachable where it matters: on a fresh or foreign device the sign-in form will not submit without a Secret Key, so the screen built for a lost laptop was unreachable from the device you would actually be holding. |
| ~~**#12** device linking~~ **DONE** | The E2EE-native answer to "sign in on my other device" — it carries auth *and* unlock. **Refined 2026-08-12** into [ADR 0059](decisions/0059-device-linking.md) + [uc.account.onboard-device](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.onboard-device.md). **Slice 1 — the server half — is BUILT 2026-08-13** (`cr-device-pairing-channel-and-session-delegation`, deleted): five `POST /api/pairing/*` endpoints on the auth spec, `backend/.../features/account/pairing/`, `V46__device_pairing.sql`, and `SessionService.issueDelegated` for ADR 0004's second origin. Three shapes it decided that the web slices inherit rather than revisit: the channel is addressed by a client-minted **handle** that is deliberately **not** a public key (the server stores only its SHA-256); **`delegate` is its own verb before `seal`**, because the grant is sealed client-side and its plaintext must be in the trusted device's hand first; and **the collect that returns the sealed grant spends the channel whoever calls it**, so a trusted device that keeps polling after it seals consumes the ceremony before the new device arrives. `scripts/validate_pairing_shapes.py` now carries §4 permanently. **Slice 2 (*add a device*, web) — REFINED and BUILT 2026-08-13** (`cr-pairing-add-a-device-web`, deleted), and the refine answered the four questions this row had left open. **(a)** In *add a device* the displayed code carries a `veyqpair:` prefix and the **handle**, nothing else. **Slice 3 answered the QR-sign-in half, and the answer is the same payload under a SECOND marker (`veyqsignin:`), still no key.** The use-case had said this direction *"displays the public key as a code"*; it cannot, and the reason is size — a serialized transport bundle is 1260 bytes, i.e. a **version 28 / 129-module** QR nobody scans off a laptop screen and one sitting past the version-23 bound `web/CLAUDE.md`'s jsQR exception rests on, against **version 4** for marker-plus-handle. Nothing is lost by relaying the key instead: in *that* direction the scan authenticates nothing anyway (the attacker authors the QR, therefore the key), so the out-of-band channel buys no key authenticity — the transcribed code does, and the answer binds them. The second marker is not cosmetic: the payloads are byte-identical, so without it the sign-in card's scan entry would read one as the other, post a bare transport key, and **burn** the write-once answer slot before any screen looked wrong. **(b)** Answered, and it settled the phase order: **no `contracts/crypto` vector, so slice 2 is a pure web slice** — but *conditionally*, on the CR's first decision, that the new device's ephemeral transport key is a full `Identity` bundle. That makes `fingerprint()` apply unchanged and leaves the check number a display rule over an already-pinned digest; a bare hybrid keypair would instead owe both a new wire serialization and a new digest input, and the order would be contracts → web. The general claim that a new `asymWrap` context needs a vector is **false in this repo**, measured from two angles: `content-key`, `media-meta`, `node-meta`, `scope-epoch` and `share-key-recovery` are live in `web/src` and appear **0×** in `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json` — the vectors accreted per-CR with no stated rule, which is a small durable-state gap of its own. **(c)/(d)** Both decided in the CR: one scanner entry on the sign-in form dispatching on the payload prefix, and the trusted device stops polling the instant it has the answer and never polls again — a collect after `seal` would spend its own grant. **Three findings the refine raised that outlive the CR:** the coverage criterion `the-locked-entry-adds-no-shell-route` is written about the *QR-sign-in* entry, yet slice 2 puts the **first** new option on that same sign-in card, so either the criterion covers both entries or slice 2's has none; **ADR 0059 §7's deliberate slide has nothing to build on** — measured, no slide/swipe-to-confirm exists anywhere in `web/src` and PrimeVue's imported set carries none, so it is a primitive built from pointer events, not a wrapper around an existing control; and `jsqr@1.4.0` (cozmo/jsQR) becomes load-bearing for a second, security-critical flow while it is **de facto abandoned** (measured 2026-08-13). **Settled 2026-08-13, and it left durable state rather than a note:** jsQR is kept as a deliberate, granted exception to the root `CLAUDE.md` no-abandoned-dependency rule, because every alternative is worse *here* — and the exception, its measurement and the three triggers that reopen it now live in [`web/CLAUDE.md`](../web/CLAUDE.md) § Conventions, which is where someone about to "fix" the stale version will actually read it. `KitScanner.vue` had **no test at all**, so the reuse refactor landed with that component's first harness — `web/src/features/account/emergencyKit/KitScanner.test.ts` exists (measured 2026-08-18). **Slice 2 may land alone on the condition that the `wiki/` section is assembled only after slice 3** — measured, `wiki/` carries nothing on device linking today; its only QR mentions are ADR 0028's safety-number comparison. **Slice 2's trusted half is BUILT 2026-08-13** — the settings entry, the on-screen code, the answer poll, the compared number, the deliberate slide, and `delegate` → `seal` (`web/src/features/account/pairing/`, plus the shared `SlideToConfirm` primitive ADR 0059 §7 had nothing to build on). **Its new-device half did not land with it, and the reason was a slice-1 shape nobody had followed to its consumer:** `/api/pairing/delegate` returns the refresh token in the response **body**, and the web client cannot keep a body token — `boot()` revives a session only from the httpOnly cookie, and `TokenResource` sets a cookie only for a request that already carried one. An onboarded device would therefore be signed in for **exactly one page load**, holding a `DEVICE_KEY` wrap it can never reach again and no password to fall back to. **That transport correction is BUILT 2026-08-13** (`cr-delegated-session-cookie-transport`, deleted): `POST /api/token/adopt` on the auth spec, a second method on `TokenResource` inheriting its class-level `@PermitAll` + `@RateLimit(IP)`, and `TokenAdoptSliceTest`. The refine that produced it began by re-deriving the finding instead of inheriting it — which is what caught that **one third of it was false**. The two mechanical claims hold verbatim (`boot()` refreshes with an empty body; `TokenResource.kt:36` is `if (fromCookie != null)`). The rejection reason did not: keeping the token in JS was called *"the first multi-tab-unsafe session in the client"*, and it would not have been the first — measured, **nothing** in `web/src` serializes refresh, `boot()` refreshes in every tab, and the loser of a concurrent refresh reads `SUPERSEDED` and revokes the family, so ADR 0004's clone case is reachable **today, with cookies**. That is now **#123**, its own row, because it predates device linking and touches every web session. The cookie survives as the answer for the reason ADR 0004 § Context already gave (a body token is one XSS away) plus one the CR adds: a cookie is self-updating across tabs where a script-held copy goes stale silently. **Four decisions the refine settled, all now in durable state rather than in the CR:** adoption is a **new verb** (`POST /api/token/adopt`, second method on `TokenResource`), because `/api/token/refresh` deliberately cannot change a lifetime and a cookie-less refresh has none to inherit — a `sessionScope` there would let every refresh re-decide its own; it is **not narrowed** to delegated families, because the family row records no origin *by decision* (ADR 0004 § Session family) and the narrower channel-keyed variant is impossible — `collect` marks the channel terminal, so nothing survives to check against; the **lifetime is chosen on the new device**, by the same single "stay signed in" question login asks, which also drives the `DEVICE_KEY` choice (E5, ADR 0005 §22 now carries the password-less carve-out); and the response is **`SessionResponse`, not `TokenPair`**, because of a second inherited-value miss the refine found — write moment 1's device↔family join is keyed by family id, `PairingDelegateResult` carries only a token, and without the id the onboarded device is never *named* in the sessions list, which the use-case's own postcondition demands. **Slice 2 is COMPLETE 2026-08-13.** Its new-device half and the shared tail landed in the order ADR 0059 §2 forces — adopt *before* unlock, register and enrol: `onboardTail.ts` (which slice 3 reuses rather than rebuilds), the routeless join card the sign-in form's ONE scan entry dispatches to on the payload prefix, a sibling grant poll whose stop condition is the inverse of the trusted device's, and a Master-Key unlock export so `openVault` stays the single choke point into the vault. The label the ceremony chose is threaded into write moment 1 rather than written afterwards, because a write after the unlock would be a fourth write moment. **Slice 3 (*sign in with a QR code*, web) is BUILT 2026-08-14** (`cr-pairing-qr-sign-in-web`, deleted), and the `wiki/` device-linking page landed with it in EN+DE as planned. It reused `runOnboardTail` verbatim rather than rebuilding the tail, added no route on either device, and changed **no wire shape** — the whole slice is web plus a prose correction. **Three things it settled that outlive it.** The ADR's *"three attempts"* was **not buildable and is deleted, not reworded**: `PairingStore.recordAnswer` updates only a row still `PENDING`, so the answer slot is write-once, and `PairingChannel` is deliberately status-free — there is no back-channel on which the trusted device could say *"wrong, try again"*, so a retry would need a field this relay must not have. One attempt is therefore structural, the transcription is editable until it is sent, and the new device learns a refusal only as the channel going quiet, which its copy says. The **deliberate slide moved to LAST**, after the proof holds: that is what the built *add a device* direction already does, a gesture spent before the evidence exists cannot be about the evidence, and taken last the refusal costs the user nothing — with the slide first, a wrong code spends the whole ceremony and hands back nothing, which reads as *"it glitched, try again"*, the exact reflex ADR 0059 §7 is written against. And that leaves **two gates in series** (proof verified AND slide completed), either of which alone satisfies the criterion's *no envelope is ever posted* — so they are tested with one bite each, because a suite driving only a wrong code cannot say which one held. **What remained on this row was the live obligation, not a slice — and it is now discharged:** master-key + identity rotation was refined 2026-08-14 into [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) and **built 2026-08-16** as row **125**. A phished grant is therefore *stoppable* — never reversible, and no surface of the flow may imply otherwise. Two directions ship together (*add a device* / *sign in with a QR code*), the grant carries the Master Key **and** a session so no password is typed on the new device, and each direction earns a different binding step — compare a code where both screens are in front of you, transcribe one back where they are not. **The sequencing reason was half wrong and the correction is load-bearing:** #111 does not make a phished grant reversible — it makes it *containable and visible*. Rotation, refined into [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) (row **125**), does not make it reversible either: it ends the exposure going forward and retracts nothing. **CLOSED on this line 2026-09-21, measured against the whole branch, not against this row's own text.** All three slices are in the tree (`ls web/src/features/account/pairing/` → 16 files; `V46__device_pairing.sql`; `scripts/validate_pairing_shapes.py` green), the EN+DE wiki page landed as conditioned, and the live obligation this row kept open — rotation — is row **125**, `DONE 2026-08-16`. What remains of #12 is **passkey and only passkey**, which this section lists as *out of V1*; its home row has said so since 2026-08-18 while this line kept the topic open. `uc.account.onboard-device`'s coverage carries its refs since 2026-09-24 (ADR 0040's note on which rows a run has admitted). |

Zoom is parked with it only because the two share one surface and nobody wants that file opened twice. **Consequence for this line, stated plainly: with #89/#90 parked, no V1 row is unbuilt work any more.** What is left is the first publish (#102's last surface, the server log, was built 2026-10-01). |

This also closed **#113**'s one open item — a status code, not a capability flag. |

| ~~**#139** favorites per item + gallery filter~~ **DONE** | Owner's call 2026-09-20: the gallery is V1's primary viewing surface, and a favorite you cannot put on the *photo* is a favorite on the wrong object. **Named here with its tension stated, not hidden:** it is net-new surface (contracts → backend read shape → web) on a line otherwise made of durability and operations debts, and its *"everyone's favorites"* half **decides a group-privacy default** the enabler deliberately left open ([ADR 0014](decisions/0014-group-scopes-and-governance.md)). That decision was the gate, and it **fell before the build**: [ADR 0068](decisions/0068-favorites-two-confidentiality-levels.md) settles the two confidentiality levels, and `web/src/app/favoritesPrivacy.test.ts` pins the default. **DONE 2026-09-20** (`cr-favorites-two-objects`, deleted) — contracts, `V49__item_favorites.sql`, the backend slice, the web marks + gallery filter and its coverage all landed. This line went on calling the decision an open gate for a day after its own home row recorded it as done; it is corrected here rather than annotated, because a V1 line that disagrees with the row it points at is worse than either version alone. |
| ~~**cr-change-password-cold-credential-gate**~~ **DONE 2026-09-20** | Built in five phases (contracts → backend → web → docs → coverage); the CR is deleted, and what it decided lives in [uc.account.change-password](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.change-password.md) § *One entry point* + [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) Decision 2. `attestationSignature` is required, the handler's `if (present)` branch is gone, and `proveColdCredential` moved out of rotation into `web/src/features/account/coldCredential.ts` so **both** screens that mint a cold credential run one implementation of the proof. The forgotten-password entry point is deleted, not gated — the owner's call on the one population that loses a self-service route (mail-less instance, forgotten password, signed in via `DEVICE_KEY`) is recorded in the use-case rather than here. Three bite tests carried it: the backend applied the full rewrite with no attestation (red at `PasswordChangeSliceTest.kt:87` on the untouched tree), the web client posted a wrap the account could never open when handed a foreign Secret Key, and forcing the proof's Master-Key comparison to `true` reddened change-password **and** rotation together. The finding itself is not restated here: [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) Decision 2 now carries it, which is where somebody about to build a third cold-credential screen will read it. |
| **The six open web CRs** — ~~`cr-gallery-one-order-for-every-tile`~~ **DONE 2026-09-20**, ~~`cr-upload-prepare-visibly-and-decode-once`~~ **DONE 2026-09-20 (3 of 4 phases)**, ~~`cr-sidebar-favorites-show-their-cover`~~ **DONE 2026-09-20**, ~~`cr-devices-and-sessions-one-rubric`~~ **DONE 2026-09-20**, ~~`cr-shell-preferences-into-profile-menu`~~ **DONE 2026-09-20**, ~~`cr-web-own-confirm-dialog`~~ **DONE 2026-09-20** | Owner's call 2026-09-20: all of them ship in V1. Three are defects against something already promised — an in-flight upload tile sorts by capture time instead of pinning to the front of [uc.memory.gallery](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.gallery.md), and [uc.memory.cover](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.cover.md)'s cover is unkept in the sidebar's Favorites section, where the CSS for it was written and never wired (#140). and picking a large batch looks like the app has hung, because the pre-upload phase names none of its stages ([uc.memory.upload](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.upload.md) — every optimistic tile says *"Uploading"* while nothing has left the device yet). Three are interface work with no defect behind them: one rubric for what *this device* is, one door for theme + the settings gear, and the app's own confirmation dialog in place of `window.confirm`. None of the six touches `contracts/`. `cr-upload-prepare-visibly-and-decode-once` was opened **after** the call that widened this line and is carried by it, not by a second decision. **The gallery one is built and its CR deleted:** both surfaces now sort every tile — in flight or listed — through one comparator keyed on the capture time the file will be sealed with, so finishing an upload changes a tile's content and never its place. The rule itself lives in [uc.memory.gallery](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.gallery.md) § *One order for every tile*; what the CR taught about predicting which assertion a bite reddens on moved to [changes/README](../changes/README.md). **The upload one shipped three of its four phases and closed the fourth unbuilt, which its own text allowed:** the tile now names the stage it is in (queued / preparing / uploading, visibly and in both aria-labels), the Safari re-read window no longer gates the first enqueue, and `addMedia` decodes a picked original once instead of three times. Phase 3 — overlapping a file's on-device stage with the previous file's PUTs — was closed on the owner's call against the measurement that phase's entry condition demanded: only **7%** of a batch is spent on the wire, so that is all an overlap could reclaim. The numbers and the two levers that DO reach the other 93% are row **#141**; the contributor-side Safari gap it found is row **#142**. **The preferences one is built and its CR deleted:** theme joined language as a submenu of the sidebar profile menu and the top bar lost both the theme dropdown and its gear, so the shell now offers exactly one route to `/settings` — a property that is now an acceptance criterion with a test behind it, not a convention. [ADR 0022](decisions/0022-settings-information-architecture.md) records the move and, as a rejected alternative, the category-error argument that recommended keeping the gear; what the CR taught about keying a locator per retired thing moved to [changes/README](../changes/README.md). Its one cited number did not survive contact: the compact top bar is **3rem**, not the 2.1rem the CR asserted. |
| ~~**#146** dead web CSS — the deletion, not the guard (**#136** + **#137** + **#140** fold in)~~ **DONE** | Owner's call 2026-09-20, **restated the same day once #140 was measured and the price changed**. The original entry said #136 and #137 *"cost minutes"* and that #140 owed a decision about a guard. Both halves moved: #140's decision is **no guard** — not because the exceptions resist derivation (they don't) but because **461 of 648 classes in `web/src/styles/` have no user at all**, so the predicate is red on the day it is born. The two minute-sized rows are grains of that pile, and taking them while leaving it would be incoherent — so what V1 owes here is **#146, the deletion pass itself**. **The cost is honestly larger than the entry it replaces**, and its risk is the one thing this repo cannot check: `npm run check` sees types, lint and units, and **nothing sees a visual regression**. Safety therefore comes from live hand-checking, which is why the pass is **staged by risk** rather than run as one act — provably dead first (a whole vendor block for a dependency `web/` does not have), views that never existed here second, anything that reads like live app chrome last and one group at a time. **The guard is not dropped, it is re-ordered:** after the pass the same predicate is near zero and becomes a **ratchet** that holds the cleanup, which is what a guard is for — it holds a state, it does not create one. **DONE 2026-09-21** (`cr-delete-the-inherited-stylesheets`, deleted) — and the re-ordering held: the deletion pass ran first (`3549ea4`..`3845648`, 11209 → 3626 lines across both packages) and the ratchet this row scheduled *after* it is in the tree too (`50b09e2`, `scripts/validate_stylesheet_anchors.py` → green, 634 anchor compounds). #136 and #137 are struck through with it. **One residue, and it is verification rather than deletion:** `admin-web`'s tenant list and detail were never looked at live — the same parked *"two eyes on the admin side"* #136 had. |

Under self-hosting the trust chain is the whole quality evidence, so a suite that is **not hermetic** undercuts it twice: it manufactures the exact false alarm #148 already paid a diagnosis pass for, and on any machine where something listens on 3000 the 14 refused requests become 14 answered ones with nothing reporting it. Cheap to close, and it is the kind of thing that is never worth doing later. |

The missing SPA fallback measured as suspected (2026-09-23): recovery, invite and share links 404 in production on both targets. **The line widened on 2026-09-20, and what that costs is recorded here rather than discovered later.**
Everything raised that day went in on one owner's call — #136–#140 and the seven open CRs. Only one of them gates
a release: `cr-change-password-cold-credential-gate`, a live bypass of a decision already taken. The rest is
interface work and code health, added to a line that until then was made of durability and operations debts
(**#26**, **#12**, **#102**, **#89**/**#90**) — and those four now ship *after* it, not before. Two of the
additions carry an open product question rather than a task: **#139**'s four forks (whose privacy default
*"everyone's favorites"* picks, against the enabler's own stated fix) and **#140**'s predicate. Both are decided
in their refine, before anything is built. The trade was named and taken, not overlooked.

**Standing scope rule for V1 (owner's call 2026-10-03):** everything that concerns veyq's **stability, quality or security** goes on the V1 line — bugs, races, guard and test gaps, honest docs, hardening — while new features stay after V1 unless the owner names them. A new finding is classified against this rule when it is filed; ask only where it is genuinely unclear.

**The line widened again on 2026-09-23 — by a whole review.** The security/reuse review of that day filed
#151–#175 and `cr-inert-store-responses`, and all of it went on the line on one owner's call. The rows with a live
exploit path are what the release most needs, and most of them are small: `cr-inert-store-responses` (stored XSS
on the app origin), #152 (first-run setup takeover), #156 (cross-tenant recovery tokens), #157 (pending invitees,
suspension bypass), #153 (unbounded presigns), #175 (targeted login lockout). Five rows are design work decided
in their refine: #152, #154, #157, #159, #160. The largest single item is #161's protocol half (pairing becomes a
PAKE); it was first ranked behind native clients, because against V1's operator-served web client it protects
nothing, and it went on the line knowingly. #151 is cheapest now: before V1 there is no installed store to
migrate. The trade was named and taken, not overlooked.

**Out of V1** — deferred by decision, not by oversight: journal (**#18**), search (**#21**), passkey (part of
**#12**), people (**#20**), playlists (**#36**), per-Memory lock (**#46**), desktop ingest (**#50**), file
storage (**#52**), trusted web delivery (**#53**), notifications hub (**#84**), live
co-editing (**#6**), on-device agent work (**#10**, **#23**), SaaS and billing (**#38**, **#41**).

Also out, and moved rather than dropped: **email verification (#112)**. It was in V1 only as recovery's second factor, and ADR 0054 shipped that factor as the token mailed at recovery time — address control proven when it is used, not stored from registration. **Back on the pre-V1 list 2026-09-28 (owner's call)**, built 2026-10-01 (`cr-email-address-confirmation`, [ADR 0074](decisions/0074-address-control-is-recorded-where-nothing-else-proves-it.md)): a recorded, non-gating confirmation. *(This paragraph used to say it returns with "#43 open self-registration" — #43 is at-rest PII encryption; no row owns open self-registration.)*

Newly out *because* V1 is self-hosted: public share moderation/quarantine (part of **#19**) — with no central
operator there is no one to quarantine on anyone's behalf.

**Walked back** — shipped or published copy that promised more than V1 will hold. **All four DONE 2026-08-05**
(`36213dea`), and kept here as the standing rule rather than as a backlog: published copy is a claim like any
other, so when scope shrinks the copy is corrected in the same pass, not filed.

- The moderation/quarantine promise in `wiki/{en,de}` — removed; replaced by *"veyq ships no built-in moderation
  tooling today"*, which is what self-hosting actually means.
- The permanently-disabled `⌘K` search box in the topbar — removed. Search is out of V1, so the affordance went
  with it rather than staying as a dead control.
- The trusted-device flow in `wiki/{en,de}` — now carries the caveat the app already showed: some browsers
  cannot remember a device at all, and unlocking there needs the Secret Key every time.
- `memory.detailComing` — deleted; it was dead copy for a shipped feature, referenced nowhere.

| 102 | **Nothing checks that the ciphertext is actually ciphertext — the wire assertion** | security, crypto, operator-blind, e2e, testing, NEW | **DONE 2026-10-01 — all four surfaces live: database (2026-08-03), store + HTTP (2026-09-21, `9d5ce90`), server log (2026-10-01, `cr-wire-assertion-server-logs`, deleted).** The log slice sweeps every retained boot log and every container log of the stack (over the run's window) against the run's canaries through one needle set — raw, JSON-escaped, percent-encoded — armed per boot log (its database's JDBC line, the shutdown line) and per run (a key planted in the store's request log, found percent-encoded); the same needles closed a gap in the HTTP sweep, which matched a canary only raw while the recorder keeps URLs percent-encoded. State: the concern's § Surfaces and § *What a green sweep does not prove*. The successors stay open as #202/#204 and #203. [ADR 0044](decisions/0044-field-visibility-declared-at-the-field.md) checks the **schema**, not the payload: a field carrying `contentEncoding: base64` is *permitted* to be opaque, and nothing observes whether the client actually put ciphertext in it. A client writing plaintext into a field marked opaque leaves `scripts/validate_field_visibility.py` green. So does one that leaks through a path param, `X-Contributor-Token`, a presigned-URL query string, or an error `message` — the surfaces ADR 0044 names as outside its scope and assigns to nobody. **The check:** drive a real flow against a live backend + store and assert the **server-observable bytes** are ciphertext plus opaque metadata and nothing else. The oracle is the wire, not the code, which is the only reason an agent cannot satisfy it by weakening an assertion. **REFINED 2026-07-31 → [ADR 0053](decisions/0053-wire-assertion-canary-sweep.md) + concern [cc.security.wire-assertion](crosscutting/cc.security.wire-assertion.md). SLICE 1 (DATABASE) DONE 2026-08-03 (`94d14779`, CR harvested + deleted): `web/src/test/canaries.ts` derives canaries by unsealing the server's own responses (string leaves kept **by type**, so key material cannot enter); `scripts/run_web_e2e.py` `sweep()` proves their absence in each suite's own `veyq_e2e_<n>` via a data-only `mariadb-dump`, searching only `INSERT` lines with the canary escaped the way the dump escapes literals; `scripts/validate_e2e_canaries.py` pins the completeness half buildless (every discovered suite must import AND call the recorder). Measured: 4/4 suites green with the sweep armed (1/7/10/1 canaries), 18/18 guards green over the committed tree. The live state is the concern's § Surfaces table — that row is now **live**, the other three still *planned*. **Store, log and HTTP opacity stay open as their own CRs**, and the log's *retention* is already paid (ADR 0053 decision 4, `.e2e-out/<stamp>/`), so the log slice starts at the sweep rather than at the plumbing.** The e2e dependency was paid first (`run_web_e2e.py` exists, `web-e2e` green in CI), which also moots #97's unanswered question — the debt was paid for its own sake, not as a precondition. A spike then settled the shape empirically: after `memoryLocation.e2e`, its sealed values appear **0 times** in that instance's DB while `e2e.test` appears 3×, all of it declared plaintext (`pii` / `public`). So the hard part is not finding strings, it is telling deliberate plaintext from a leak — which is what the "do not let a cheap available sensor stand in for it" warning was really about. Decided in the ADR: the oracle is **canary absence** (allowlist conformance is unbuildable — 67 DB columns carry no contract); canaries are **derived by unsealing the server's own responses**, never hand-listed and never hooked into the conformance-pinned crypto (which would record key material); **two sensors on disjoint surfaces** joined by a canary file; **DB first**, per ADR 0053 § Decision 5. **HTTP and store collapse into one slice (2026-08-25 sparring session, not yet a CR):** the e2e suite drives no real browser — `grep -rn playwright web/package.json` → 0 hits; the "live" tests are Vitest in Node calling the app's own client modules over `fetch` (`web/src/features/account/recovery/recovery.e2e.test.ts:14` imports straight from `vitest`). Media's presigned store PUT/GET go through the SAME `fetch` as every backend API call (`web/src/features/memory/media.ts:857,862`), so a single hook on `globalThis.fetch` inside the e2e process — record every request/response body before/after the call — sees backend JSON traffic and store blob bytes alike, run through the same canary sweep the DB slice already has. No proxy process, no port. **Log stays its own surface and is explicitly NOT being built now** — a log leak (a stack trace, a stray debug dump) happens server-side and would never cross a `fetch` this hook can see, so it needs its own sensor later; retention is already paid (ADR 0053 decision 4), so picking it back up costs only the sweep, not the plumbing. **RE-DERIVED 2026-09-21 before the CR, and the 2026-08-25 sparring note needs two corrections — one cosmetic, one structural.** (i) The locator drifted: the presigned store PUT/GET are `web/src/features/memory/media.ts:1029,1034`, not `:857,862`. The claim holds — both are bare `fetch`. (ii) **The environment was never written down, and without it the mechanism reads as refuted.** Row **#149** measured the same day that *"patching `globalThis.fetch` in `src/test/setup.ts` never fires, because the client's request does not pass through the binding visible there"* — true **under happy-dom**, which installs its own `window.fetch`. Measured now: `@vitest-environment happy-dom` appears in **73** files, `node` in 2, and **0 of the 10 `*.e2e.test.ts` suites declare either**, so they all run under vitest's default **`node`**, where `fetch` is the global undici one and the generated runtime resolves it **at call time** (`web/src/api/generated/auth/runtime.ts:205`: `await (this.configuration.fetchApi \|\| fetch)(…)`). The hook therefore works — and the inverse follows: #149's happy-dom `settings.fetch.interceptor` seam is **not available here**, because `globalThis.happyDOM` is undefined in the e2e process and `setup.ts`'s whole interceptor block is skipped. (Which also means that block's `armed` carve-out is belt-and-braces, not the thing that lets e2e traffic out.) Reaching for that seam would have produced a sensor that silently never runs. **(iii) The structural correction: *"a single hook sees backend JSON and store bytes alike"* is true of the wire and WRONG as a sensor for the store.** [ADR 0053](decisions/0053-wire-assertion-canary-sweep.md) §3 splits the surfaces by *who can reach them*, and gives the object store to the **orchestrator**, because only it holds the store credentials. A client-side hook sees what the client **sent**; the operator-blind claim is about what is **stored** — which includes objects the server wrote itself and the **object keys** as the bucket holds them, neither of which crosses that `fetch`. So the combined slice is **two sensors and two oracles, not one of either**: the suite's `fetch` recorder for **HTTP bodies (opacity)**, and an orchestrator-side sweep for the **object store (canary absence, keys AND bytes)**. The second is cheap — `scripts/instance_backup.py`'s `Store.mirror_out()` already mirrors a bucket to a local directory and the orchestrator already calls `_store_for(...)` for `fresh_bucket`. **Owner's call 2026-09-21: both oracles in one CR** — the sensors are separate but the surfaces go live together. **STORE + HTTP BUILT 2026-09-21** (`cr-wire-assertion-store-and-http`, deleted) — two of the four surfaces go live together, with **two** sensors as ADR 0053 §3 requires rather than the one the 2026-08-25 note imagined. `web/src/test/wireRecorder.ts` records the suite's own requests and responses; `run_web_e2e.py` gained `sweep_http()` (opacity **and** canary absence over URLs, headers and bodies) and `sweep_store()` (canary absence over object **keys and bytes**, mirrored with the operator tool's own rclone plumbing). **Only the server log is left**, and it is still deliberately parked with its retention already paid. **The one design correction the build forced, and it is the reusable part:** the opacity oracle needs **two** tests, not one. Readability alone needs a length floor against noise, and the floor is exactly where short plaintext hides — the bite put an unsealed Memory name on the wire at **23 bytes**, one under a 24-byte floor, and the check never looked at it. Below the floor, *shortness itself* is now the evidence: nothing legitimate on this wire is short (measured minimum 32 bytes across every declared-opaque field a real suite sends; an AEAD value cannot be under 28). The general form is harvested to [`changes/README`](../changes/README.md) § *a threshold added against false positives is where the false negatives hide*. **A suite that stores nothing is skipped out loud**, discriminated by its own `veyq_blob` count rather than by a list of which suites upload. And `scripts/validate_opaque_names_disjoint.py` now pins the invariant the HTTP oracle rests on — the opaque and plaintext property names must stay disjoint (38 against 126). **Verified:** `media.e2e` green on all three sensors (1 canary, 21 requests, 2 objects); B1 red naming `encryptedName` on both directions; B2 red on a canary in bytes and a canary in a key; B3 red on all four zero-population cases. |

Mutation as the assertion-strength link `validate_coverage.py` can't see — per surface, on the unit layer, gated at break 95 (PR + main): **web** StrykerJS over the file-pure crypto tier (`v1`, the spaces ring, `naming`, `accountBirth`, `nodeMeta`; scope + per-file evidence in `web/stryker.mutate.json`), **backend** PIT over the pure policy tier (rotate/consent, removal cascade, rate-limit, share-access `canView`/`canUpload`, last-owner; scope in `backend/core/pitest.scope.json`). The ADR owns the rules (§2 one-liner-vs-capability-matrix, §3 file-pure-not-layer-wholesale); the manifest `_comment`s own the per-file/per-policy reasoning — not restated here. **Lower-prio tail, left out by decision** (correctness not security, mostly wiring — below the gate's bar): `memoryLocation`/`memoryDate`/`mediaDeriver` validation/sort gaps. |

| 15 | Keys (grants, unlock wraps, rotation) | feature, crypto | inbox |
| 125 | **Key rotation after a compromise (master key + identity)** | feature, account, crypto, security | **DONE 2026-08-16** (`cc3740b` + `0625834`; `cr-account-key-rotation-after-compromise` deleted). [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) + [uc.account.rotate-keys](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.rotate-keys.md) are the durable home. `POST /api/account/rotation` replaces the Master Key and the identity keypair **together** in one transaction — both cold wraps rewritten under fresh salts (the Emergency Kit survives because the *Secret Key* is unchanged), every other wrap deleted as a **whitelist of survivors** so a method added later fails safe, live pairing channels superseded, every other session cut — and refuses whole if a cold wrap is missing. The rotator is excluded from their own scope rotation by a decline row written at origination, **whether or not that call created the job**, because a coalescing origination would otherwise leave a scope's live rotation claimable by the account it locks out. `POST /api/tasks/declines/clear` makes [ADR 0028](decisions/0028-identity-authenticity-tofu-verification.md) (2)'s *“the wrap then proceeds”* true and cannot reach that exclusion, because a scope-rotation job has no delivery target for a membership id to resolve to. **Three things it settled that the refine had wrong.** The gate is a **live proof**, not the recorded unlock origin: rewriting the cold wraps needs password + Secret Key as *material* and no KEK survives an unlock, so every path supplies them and checking them against the stored floor proves possession **now** — ADR 0060 Decision 2 and the use-case's §2/E1 say the built shape, and a device-opened session upgrades **in place** rather than being turned away. The decline exclusion is per **key-holder**, not per device (the claim query keys on `userId`); seven of eight sites were corrected and the eighth is a comment in an applied Flyway migration that must stay wrong — editing it fails startup on every instance that already ran it. And `scripts/validate_rotation_shape.py` pins the one-mode decision, which is a **negative** nothing else can hold: the property set, that every field is required (an optional one is a discriminator spelled by omission), and that a rewritten wrap carries no `method`. **What is deliberately not covered:** there is no live-suite proof that a rotated account is re-admitted by a second, real key-holder — that needs two accounts against a live backend, and the coverage file says so rather than letting the slice tests read as more. |
| 126 | **Accepting a changed identity key never clears the declining worker's job exclusion** | bug, account, crypto, security, tasks | **DONE 2026-08-16** (`0625834`, with row 125). `POST /api/tasks/declines/clear` makes [ADR 0028](decisions/0028-identity-authenticity-tofu-verification.md) (2)'s *“the wrap then proceeds”* true: `acceptKeyChange` re-pins **and** drops this key-holder's own declines against that contact, so the ordinary next claim delivers. **Narrow in three ways, each load-bearing** — the caller's own rows only (a blind server must not generalise one human's decision to a key-holder who has not looked), `grant-delivery` jobs only, and that contact only. The third is what keeps it away from the rotation exclusion that shares its table: a `scope-rotation` job's `resourceRef` is a **scopeId**, so no membership id can ever resolve to one — the invariant holds by the shape of the query rather than by a special case in it, and `TasksSliceTest.clearingDeclinesNeverFreesARotationExclusion` asserts both halves in one run. The clear's failure is deliberately **not swallowed**: reporting an accept that delivers nothing re-creates the exact stranding this closed, and re-accepting is idempotent. |
| 127 | **The vault keeps no record of which wrap opened it** | account, crypto, security, client | **DONE 2026-08-16** (`0625834`, with row 125) — **and its one prospective consumer turned out to need it for something else than the row assumed.** `vault.ts` now carries `UnlockOrigin`, three-valued (`cold` / `device` / `none`) because the session-restore and pairing-grant paths open **no wrap at all** and `none` is their only honest entry; a boolean would have to label one of them with a method it never used. Every unlock path states its own at `openVault`, which cannot infer it. **What changed against the row's premise:** it was written expecting the record to *be* the cold gate. It is not — rewriting the two cold wraps needs password + Secret Key as *material* and no KEK survives an unlock, so every path supplies them anyway and checking them against the stored floor is both free and stronger (it proves possession **now**). The record earns the sentence on the screen — why the Secret Key is asked for here — while a device-opened session upgrades **in place** rather than being refused, which is what [uc.account.rotate-keys](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.rotate-keys.md) §E1 had said all along. |

| 24 | Platform (rate limiting, app shell, web views) | feature, platform | **DONE 2026-07-27 (cr-ratelimit-posture)** → [ADR 0051](decisions/0051-declarative-rate-limit-posture.md); the rate-limit-**core** unification spun off → **#115** (also done). Prior state — rate-limiting *was* ad-hoc per-surface: per-IP `AuthRateLimitFilter`/`@RateLimited` (auth + invite-accept), handler-level `ShareRateLimiter`/`SharePasswordAttemptLimiter`, and now `InviteMailRateLimiter` (cr-email-invite-send-hardening — per-(tenant,recipient)+per-tenant on the member-invite mint/reissue paths). **Follow-up refine (owner direction 2026-07-24):** a **declarative auth+rate-limit posture** — an annotation that can also express **per-owner/per-recipient** keying (not just per-IP), plus a `validate_*` guard that forces **every endpoint** to declare its auth + rate-limit posture, with an explicit, justified opt-out for the few that need none (the trust-chain philosophy applied to endpoints). What surfaced it: the per-IP `@RateLimited` shares **one global window across all annotated endpoints**, so it can't serve as a per-endpoint baseline for authenticated actions — marking the member-invite endpoints coupled owner traffic to the *login* budget and broke `AuthRateLimitTest`, so it was reverted; the anchor belongs to this future annotation instead. **What landed:** ADR 0051 + `@RateLimit(key)`/`@RateLimitExempt(reason)` on **every** endpoint; `IpRateLimitFilter` + `UserRateLimitFilter` (the `USER` key needed its own post-authorization filter — `quarkus.http.auth.proactive=false` leaves no principal at AUTHENTICATION priority, where the per-IP one sits); `RateLimitPostureSweepTest` enumerates the surface **twice on different keys** (compiled main classes vs. live CDI `@Path` beans) and is **bite-proven on four injections**. Security payload landed as a **new per-share identity budget** on `createShareIdentity` (the one guest call that writes a row before any upload exists) + `completeShareMedia` on the existing upload budget; `deleteShareMedia` is exempt (single-row ownership already caps it). **Owner call 2026-07-27:** the three anonymous guest **reads** and `MediaResource`/`TasksResource` are `@RateLimitExempt` with the residual gap written down — `cc.security.rate-limit` § Unbounded by decision — rather than capped on an axis that would punish legitimate traffic. Blind spots named, not hidden: the sweep proves **declaration**, never that a domain-keyed limiter **fires**, and being a JVM test it cannot be pinned by `validate_guard_gaps.py` (trust-chain skill gap-map). No-Restate finding: the **auth** half is already done — ADR 0008 default-deny + `DefaultDenyTest` force an auth statement on every endpoint, runtime-fail-closed — so only the **rate-limit** half is new. CR adds `@RateLimit(key=…)`/`@RateLimitExempt(reason=…)` + a reflection **sweep** (fail-closed like default-deny, **fork-free** — the OpenAPI-template-generation path stays rejected per ADR 0008, whose `jaxrs-spec` templates carry no operation-level annotation anyway) forcing **every** endpoint to declare posture-or-exempt. Enum keys: `IP`/`USER` (one generalized boundary filter — pre-/post-auth) + `SHARE`/`RECIPIENT`/`OWNER`/`TENANT` (handler-markers → the honest **declaration≠enforcement** limit goes to the gap-map). Values stay in `VeyqConfig` (self-hostable); ADR 0051 extends 0008. The sweep also flushes out today's un-throttled `@PermitAll` **writes** (`createShareIdentity`/`completeShareMedia`/`deleteShareMedia`) — the security payload. The rate-limit-**core** unification (3 Fixed-Window impls → 1, Token-Bucket) spun off → **#115**. |
| 116 | **`mutationThreshold` is an aggregate across all five scoped classes, so a weak new member passes on its siblings' backs** — measured 2026-07-27 while grounding `cr-token-bucket-rate-limit-core`: the first `./gradlew pitest` after the new core landed scored **56/58 = 97 %** and the build was **green** against the calibrated threshold of 95, while the class the gate had just been re-scoped onto — `TokenBucket` — was **23/25 = 92 %** on its own, with both survivors in it. So the one class whose assertion strength the change was about was *under* the bar and nothing said so. Same defect class as #114 and [ADR 0045](decisions/0045-mutation-testing-trust-chain-unit-layer.md) §4's reason for `mutation_meta_check_backend.py` (a whole-scope signal misses a single member) — except the existing per-glob meta-check asserts only **> 0 mutations**, never a per-glob **score**. Two shapes to weigh at refine: (a) extend `mutation_meta_check_backend.py` to a per-glob score floor read from the same manifest (cheap, the report already carries per-class status; needs a decision on whether the floor equals the global 95 or is per-class); (b) leave it and accept that the aggregate is the gate, documenting it where the manifest is read. **Only found by running the gate rather than reasoning about it** — the structural check (glob matches, files exist) was green the whole time.  **DONE 2026-07-30 (cr-mutation-gate-completeness).** Shape (a) was taken but **not** as this row framed it: a per-glob *percentage* floor is unusable at these sizes — measured **3/6/9/15/25** mutants, so **one** survivor scores 66.7/83.3/88.9/93.3/96.0 %, and a per-glob 95 would silently mean **100 % for four of the five classes**, abolishing the exact headroom [`build.gradle.kts`](../backend/build.gradle.kts) documents the 95 for (one future *equivalent* mutant). Shipped instead as a size-independent **survivor budget**: per glob **≤ 1 undetected mutant** *and* **≥ 1 killed** — the second closes the degenerate case the budget alone passes (a 1-mutant glob whose only mutant survived is *within* budget at 0 % killed). Survivor = PIT's own `detected != "true"`, grounded against `DetectionStatus.isDetected()` in pitest 1.19.4 rather than a hand-listed status set: KILLED/TIMED_OUT/**NON_VIABLE**/MEMORY_ERROR/RUN_ERROR are detected, SURVIVED/NO_COVERAGE/NOT_STARTED/STARTED are not — so a non-viable mutant is excluded for free and an *uncovered* one correctly counts against the budget. Three new `mutation_meta_check_selftest.py` cases (10 total) pin it, the first **replaying this row's own incident**: 56/58 = 96.6 % total, above the 95 gate, with both survivors in one glob → red. The row's premise held; only its proposed remedy needed re-deriving. | tooling, ci, mutation, guard, NEW | **DONE 2026-07-30 (cr-mutation-gate-completeness) → per-glob survivor budget** |
| 115 | Unify the rate-limit core (3 Fixed-Window impls → 1, optionally Token-Bucket) | platform, security, tech-debt | **DONE 2026-07-27 (cr-token-bucket-rate-limit-core).** One pure `TokenBucket` + one `TokenBucketCounter` shell now carry **every** bound — the two boundary filters, both share budgets, the share-password attempt bound and both invite axes; `RateLimitWindow`/`FixedWindowCounter` and the two hand-rolled maps are gone, and the mutation gate moved with the class (`pitest.scope.json` + **both** `paths:` blocks in `mutation-backend.yml`). **[ADR 0052](decisions/0052-token-bucket-rate-limit-core.md)** records the decision, taken on design grounds against the refine's recommendation, and both directions of the gradual-refill trade (boundary burst gone; the share-password wall gone too — throughput unchanged, deterrence marginally softer). Bucket **derived** from today's config, so no key added or renamed and no operator retunes; separate burst/sustained dials stay a later additive key. `2 → 0` proven permanently, not tallied: **`scripts/validate_ratelimit_core.py`** (bite-proven on three injections — a re-introduced map, a renamed-away core, an emptied shell) plus its filename-grep limit pinned as open gap **9** in `validate_guard_gaps.py`. `./gradlew check` **469** green; all 15 `validate_*.py` green; the annotation claim re-measured against **HEAD** (`grep -rnE '^[[:space:]]*@RateLimit\(key' backend/src/main`, marker not paraphrase): **12** annotation lines, byte-identical before/after — ADR 0051's `key` indirection collected on. (An earlier report said *19*: that grep's comment filter let indented KDoc mentions through. Byte-identity held; the count did not.) **Gate actually run, not asserted:** `./gradlew pitest` → **58/58 mutants killed, 100 %**, of which `TokenBucket` **25/25** (`scripts/mutation_meta_check_backend.py`: the `me.raddatz.veyq.core.ratelimit.TokenBucket` glob matches 25). The first run was 56/58 — **both survivors sat in the new class** (`refill`'s saturation test: `>=`→`>` and `capacity - tokens`→`capacity + tokens`, both invisible from an empty bucket and an exact division), closed by `TokenBucketTest.theSaturationBoundaryIsExactFromAPartlyFilledBucket`. Refine trail: **refined 2026-07-27 → `cr-token-bucket-rate-limit-core`.** Owner picked **Token-Bucket** over the refine's recommendation (first-attempt anchoring); recorded as a decision on design grounds — no incident in this repo is attributable to the window model. **Two of the three costs the refine priced in dissolved on scoping:** the bucket is *derived* from today's config (`capacity = max-per-window`, `refill = max/window`), so sustained rate and burst size are unchanged, **no key is renamed** (a rename would leave a tightened limit silently on defaults — SmallRye ignores unknown keys), and the existing 429 tests keep asserting what they assert (`measured`: only `InviteMailRateLimiterTest:25` reads a `Retry-After` value, a `1..3600` range check that still holds). The separate burst/sustained dials — the bucket's actual capability — land later as additive optional config; this CR ships the algorithm plus the removal of the window-boundary burst. Also finishes the consolidation with a **permanent guard** (no rate-limit map outside `shared/ratelimit/`). Spun off from #24 / `cr-ratelimit-posture` refine (2026-07-27). **Re-measured 2026-07-27 after cr-ratelimit-posture — the premise shrank, it did not dissolve.** `AuthRateLimitFilter` is **gone** (replaced by `IpRateLimitFilter`/`UserRateLimitFilter` over the new shared `FixedWindowCounter`), and `ShareRateLimiter` was **moved onto** that counter, so both now sit on the mutation-verified `RateLimitWindow` ([ADR 0045](decisions/0045-mutation-testing-trust-chain-unit-layer.md) §2). **Two hand-rolled map holders remain** (`grep -rln ConcurrentHashMap backend/core/src/main/kotlin/me/raddatz/veyq/core/{ratelimit,sharing,admin/tenant}`): `SharePasswordAttemptLimiter` (own `Window` data class + inline arithmetic — the one true duplicate left) and `InviteMailRateLimiter` (already calls `RateLimitWindow`, but keeps **two** of its own maps for the per-recipient and per-tenant axes — a `FixedWindowCounter` per axis would absorb it). So the remaining work is 2 → 0, not 3 → 1. Consolidate onto **one** core; **Token-Bucket** is the candidate model — it separates burst capacity from sustained refill, which Fixed-Window can't (and Fixed-Window has the window-boundary burst weakness). The `key` indirection from #24 lets this land **without touching a single `@RateLimit` annotation** (the algorithm sits behind the posture). New core must be **re-mutation-verified** (it replaces `RateLimitWindow`); values stay in `VeyqConfig` (self-hostable). |
| 46 | Per-memory extra lock (password / biometric on a single Memory) | feature, memory, crypto, keys, NEW | **inbox (grounded 2026-07-15, not refined).** The blocker is structural, not mechanical: **every layer of the key model is disjunctive** — "one key, many wraps" means each wrap is another way *in* (ADR 0012 §2, en.account.key-model). A lock is **conjunctive** (hold `K_SC` **and** the password), so it cannot be added as a wrap; it must **replace** the `symWrap(K_SC, K_node)`. That is the first AND-construction in the system, and it cascades: (a) **recovery dies for that Memory** — Emergency Kit, recovery code and Master Key all restore `K_SC` and still won't open it, against the explicit promise that no route back is ever lost; (b) **a share bypasses it entirely** — `symWrap(K_share, K_node)` hands out `K_node` with no password (ADR 0029), so a share of a locked Memory is a plaintext export; (c) **the name still leaks** — names key off `K_SC`, not `K_node`, so a locked Memory keeps its title and tree position; (d) if the threat is **duress**, a visible locked-state names the one Memory to demand — deniability needs a hidden-node primitive that does not exist. **Owner decides first:** is this crypto or a UI gate? (ADR 0037 chose a server gate for share passwords, but that shape does **not** port — a member already holds `K_SC` and the server ships `wrappedContentKey` on every `listNodes`.) Primitives are all present (Argon2id KEK, kdfParams, symWrap contexts) — the code is a day; the product question is the work. **Also: 'biometric' in this title is a different feature** (device-local, so per-device = OR again) and should be split off. |

Later: **on-device coarse names**. **Pinned (refinement):** medoid = the true medoid, **min summed Haversine distance** (robust to outliers, never the mean); `derivedMode` default **auto**, flips to **fixed** on a manual pin move; (2a) **rename** the `wrappedCapturedAt` tier as it grows to also carry GPS (`gpsLat`/`gpsLng`), **no GPS override**; map = **Leaflet**, tiles a self-host/opt-in seam → its **own ADR at slice 3** (mirrors ADR 0016); the opt-in **settings** surface scheduled later (timing TBD); **2b UI kept minimal** (start simple, no map yet). use-case **split into `uc.memory.date.md` (done) + `uc.memory.place.md` (web-complete, cr-0069)**; name manual **or** geocoded); sealed in the node `wrappedMeta` beside the date (read-modify-write so a location save can't clobber the date). **Re-slice:** geocoding (was slice 4) folds into set-location; Coverage filled (8 criteria, real CI refs); all gates green. | 89 | Lightbox landscape image doesn't rescale on device rotation (fullscreen) | feature, memory, media, lightbox, ux, bug | **dropped 2026-09-28 (owner's call).** **inbox — but the relevance check is now DONE (measured 2026-07-30):** a case-insensitive `grep -niE` for `zoom`, `orientation`, `resize`, `scale`, `innerWidth` and `matchMedia` over `web/src/features/memory/components/MediaLightbox.vue` returns **0 hits in 649 lines** — the lightbox reacts to no viewport or orientation change at all. So the symptom is a real never-built gap, not a regression, and it shares one fix surface with **#90**. Symptom: open a landscape photo in fullscreen, then rotate the device → the image does not scale to the new viewport and is shown cropped/incomplete. Relates to Memory gallery (#16). **GROUNDED 2026-09-21 — and the relevance check this row rests on does NOT prove what it claims. PARKED: do not build against it.** The 2026-07-30 measurement was a `grep -niE` for `zoom`, `orientation`, `resize`, `scale`, `innerWidth`, `matchMedia` returning 0 hits, read as *"the lightbox reacts to no viewport or orientation change at all"*. It does not follow. The image is sized in **pure CSS** — `.media-lightbox-stage img { max-width: 100%; max-height: 100%; width: auto; height: auto; object-fit: contain }` (`web/src/styles/media.css:138-151`) — which reflows on rotation without a line of JavaScript. The grep proved the **absence of JS**, not the presence of a defect. And `object-fit: contain` cannot crop, so the word *cropped* in this row is not explained by anything in the file. **Provenance, which this row never recorded and which changes what it is:** the symptom is a **second-hand report** — the owner's father, on his own device — and **nobody here has reproduced it** (owner, 2026-09-21). So this is an unreproduced third-party report standing on a refuted mechanism, which is the weakest state a row can be in, and the reason it is parked rather than refined. **Two candidates, measured, for whoever picks it up — neither yet tied to the symptom:** (a) the breakpoint is **width-only and sits in the rotation path** — `media.css` has exactly one (`@media (max-width: 900px)`, `:301`), while a phone in landscape is 844–932 CSS px wide depending on the device, so rotating can flip the lightbox from the bottom-sheet layout into the **desktop** one with a fixed `clamp(20rem, 28vw, 22.5rem)` side column on a viewport now ~390 px tall; (b) the chrome reserves are **pixels sized for portrait** — stage `inset: 0 56px 92px` on mobile, topbar 64–72 px, i.e. ~42 % of a 390 px-tall viewport. **What must happen before any work:** reproduce it, on a named device, with the viewport size written down — because the two candidates predict a *small* image, not a cropped one, and a fix aimed at the wrong one is unfalsifiable. **One adjacent finding that is NOT this row's** and should not be smuggled into it: `dvh`/`svh`/`lvh` appear **0×** in all of `web/src` while `layout.css` uses `100vh` three times (`:2`, `:26`, `:40`) — the mobile address-bar trap, live for the app shell. The lightbox itself is `position: fixed; inset: 0` and immune. Own row if anyone wants it. |

Relates to Memory gallery (#16). **PARKED 2026-09-21 with #89, and for a different reason than #89's.** Nothing here is refuted — there genuinely is no zoom affordance, and this row is a feature request, not a defect.
