| 111 | **Device & session manager (see + revoke all my devices/sessions remotely)** | feature, account, security, crypto, sessions | **done — Slices A, B, C and E3's entry point are all BUILT (2026-08-12): [uc.account.sessions](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.sessions.md) written, ADR 0004 edited to match, ADR 0058 added, and the contract + backend + web landed for each.** Goal: a user sees **every** device that can skip full unlock + **every** active session, and can revoke any of them **remotely** ("sign out everywhere"). **The 2026-07-22 grounding was wrong in three ways** (measured against `main` @ `e725069`, clean tree): **(a)** this is **not** the superset of the "This device" panel — `DeviceTrustPanel.vue:34-38` makes **no API call at all**, it only reads IndexedDB, and the one consumer of `GET /api/account/key-material` is `unlock.ts:17`, which uses it as `find()` lookups by `method`+`id`, never as a list. The manager is a **new server-backed surface**, not an extension. **(b)** The sessions half was **not ADR-sized — it was already decided** by [ADR 0004](decisions/0004-session-tokens.md), which needed no new ADR, only an **edit**: its earlier design stored the client-built name as ciphertext *on the family* (`PUT …/sessions/{id}/name`) and paid for it with a standing obligation to re-encrypt per login, or the operator could re-link a device's logins. Since E1/E2 put the name in the sealed device register anyway, that mechanism was **removed rather than guarded** — no per-family ciphertext, nothing to correlate, and one naming mechanism instead of two. Missing now is only the contract and the backend. **(c)** The genuinely ADR-shaped gap is a different one: **there is no join between a wrap and a session family.** A wrap carries `id, method, wrappedKey, kdfParams` and its table has no timestamps at all; a family carries `id, created_at, last_used_at` (`V5__login_sessions.sql:13-20`); nothing links them — so "revoke *this device* everywhere" is not implementable today. **Cheaper than the row claimed:** `SessionService.revokeAllForUser()` already exists (`SessionService.kt:97-100`, reachable only from recovery at `RecoveryHandler.kt:120`), so "log out everywhere" needs only an endpoint; and `last_used_at` is rewritten on every refresh (`SessionService.kt:62`). **Decisions taken 2026-08-11.** **(E1) the per-device label lives in a separate Master-Key-sealed blob**, modelled on the built `GET/PUT /api/account/identity-pins` (`account.openapi.yaml:197-267`, versioned, `409 stale_version`) — no new field on `MasterKeyWrap`, no per-wrap metadata server-side. **(E2) the wrap↔family join lives client-side, in that same blob.** The operator genuinely cannot tell devices apart today — all wraps are served in one response (`AccountResource.kt:40`) and **no endpoint reads or uses a single wrap**, so unwrapping is client-only; a server-side link would newly turn N anonymous families into per-device clusters, against `0004:65-67`. Three consequences bind the design: the **server stays the authoritative set and the blob is only an annotation layer** — anything it does not know (pre-feature devices, a failed write) is still listed anonymously and still revocable, which is the same degradation ADR 0004:97-100 already accepts for unnamed families; the blob needs a **stable client-side `deviceId`**, because keying on `wrapId` breaks on a trust-level switch, which swaps the convenience wrap ([ADR 0005](decisions/0005-unlock-methods-and-device-trust.md):99-101) — and no per-device identifier exists in `web/src` today; and a revoke **kills the family first, then the wrap**, since auth ≠ unlock (`vault.ts:1-4`) and a wrap alone authenticates nothing, so the family is the cut that counts. Cost: one PUT per **login** (the family is stable across refreshes, `0004:37-38`) plus the `stale_version` retry. **(E3) this row runs before device linking** (#12, now [ADR 0059](decisions/0059-device-linking.md)): linking's own sharpest risk is a phished QR approval handing a stranger the Master Key, and today there is **no remote revoke** to undo it — shipping linking first ships the attack without its remediation. **The second half of that reasoning was wrong and the refine corrected it:** a revoke does *not* undo such a grant — and rotation, refined later into [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) (row **125**), does not undo one either — so this row bought linking **containment and discovery**, never reversal — and linking's ceremony is therefore heavier, not lighter, for it. **Slices, in order:** **A — sessions** (list + revoke over the existing families, in `auth.openapi.yaml`; no naming, no join) — **BUILT 2026-08-12** (`GET /api/sessions`, `DELETE /api/sessions/{id}`, `POST /api/sessions/revoke-others`, and Settings → Personal → Sessions). **It left one gap, found in implementation and then found to be worse than first written:** the use-case's E3 promises a **locked** device can still cut off a lost one, and the panel honours that — it takes no vault dependency and disables nothing when locked. The settings hub *route* is `requiresUnlock` (`web/src/router.ts:34`), so a locked caller cannot reach the panel. **But the real blocker is one step earlier, and it is not a route:** the Secret-Key field is `required` (`LoginView.vue:262`) whenever this device carries no password DEVICE wrap for the entered email (`LoginView.vue:69` — the predicate is device-local trust, not "no open Master Key"), so on a **fresh or foreign device — the lost-laptop case — email + password cannot even be submitted** without a Secret Key. The two ways to reach "session live, vault locked" today are both failure paths: a stale device wrap that reveals the field after login (`LoginView.vue:126-131`), or a wrong Secret Key. So the state E3 is written for is not reachable where it matters. **The two obvious fixes are both wrong.** Dropping `requiresUnlock` from `/settings/:section?` exposes every other settings panel to a locked vault; a locked-reachable *route* for the panel would introduce the first session-gated-but-not-unlock-gated shell route — a new security class in the router — to serve an action that needs no panel at all. **Because without names, exactly one action is fully meaningful locked: "sign out everywhere else."** It identifies nothing and needs to identify nothing; per-row revoke on an unnamed list is guessing by date, and the list itself is four anonymous rows. **The decided shape (2026-08-12), therefore, is on the login form, not behind it:** a secondary action beside the submit, shown only when `needsSecretKey` — *"I don't have my Secret Key with me"*, a `type="button"` so it bypasses `required` — which performs **login only, no unlock attempt** (so no red "unlock failed" on a path where not unlocking is the intent), and renders a distinct locked state whose one offered action is `POST /api/sessions/revoke-others`, beside the sign-out button already there (`LoginView.vue:329-336`). **Security posture is unchanged, and that is measured, not assumed:** `login()` sets the tokens *before* any unlock (`login.ts:37`) and the endpoint is `@Authenticated`, so a password-only holder can already call it directly; what changes is reachability in the UI, which is the entire point. The residual is a nuisance — whoever holds the password can repeatedly eject the account's other sessions — and it exists today via the API either way. **Cost, stated honestly because the first estimate was wrong:** this is not "one button". It is a second entry action, a distinct locked state with its own EN+DE copy, and its tests — well under a route + panel, not free. **The standing alternative is to drop E3 outright**; if that is chosen, `uc.account.sessions`'s plain-language promise *"You don't need your Secret Key for any of this"* must go with it, and it must go **before** that section is assembled into `wiki/` (nothing about sessions is published there yet, so the window is open). **Sequenced last (decided 2026-08-12): E3 is kept, and the login-form entry point runs AFTER B and C.** The reason it can wait is also the reason it never gets better: the locked ceiling **does not rise** with either increment — names and the device↔family join both live in the Master-Key-sealed register, so a locked caller never reads them, and the locked action stays session-level by construction. Deferring therefore costs nothing in locked *capability*; what it buys is that the unlocked panel stops being anonymous rows first. What it costs is time in which E3 is promised and not delivered, which is why the obligation now lives in the use-case's E3 rather than only here, and why the plain-language section stays out of `wiki/` until the entry point lands. Do not fold this into B — B is about naming, this is about reachability; **B — the sealed device register** — **BUILT 2026-08-12** (`3eeda27`): [en.account.device-register](features/account/enablers/en.account.device-register.md) is the durable design, and `GET`/`PUT /api/account/device-register` + `veyq_device_register` + the web register/rename/reconcile all landed, with `scripts/validate_session_family_shape.py` promoted to keep a name off `SessionFamily` permanently. Refinement corrected this row's own slicing: B carries the device↔**family** join too, because without it not one name renders — the slice is label + `deviceId` + family-join, exactly as `uc.account.sessions` § Out of scope already listed it, and **C is the device↔wrap join**. Three further corrections, all measured: "one PUT per **login**" is not buildable (the blob is Master-Key-sealed, and at login there is no Master Key — the write hangs off the **unlock**, and `session.familyId` already carries the family id from the login response, so no contract change is needed); the union-merge worry dissolves because every removal here is *derived from the server's authoritative set*, not a user's intent — **provided** an external removal is applied as a frozen id-delta and an internal one (eviction) is recomputed on the merged map, which is why B offers no manual "forget this entry"; and device entries are **not** pruned when their families die (that would silently revert a rename), growth being bounded by least-recently-seen eviction instead. **C — the device↔wrap join** and "cut this device off completely" — **BUILT 2026-08-12** (`cr-device-wrap-join-and-remote-cutoff`): the durable truth is [ADR 0058](decisions/0058-revoke-never-takes-key-material.md) (a revoke never takes key material; removing a wrap is a **named escalation**, family-first, and no "unrecognised wraps" list may be offered) plus the `wraps` map, the **three** write moments and § *A user-intended removal* in [en.account.device-register](features/account/enablers/en.account.device-register.md); the surface is [uc.account.sessions](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.sessions.md) § Devices with a saved key + E5. **The open question this row carried is answered, and not the way it guessed:** C's removal *is* user-intended, and it is safe under union-merge anyway — because the intent is executed against the **server-authoritative set first** (the `DELETE` on the wrap endpoint) and the register edit only follows from **re-reading** that set. So the collision with anti-withholding never happens: the join is on no unlock path (a device finds its own wrap by the id in its *local* record), so pruning a join row can never make a client lose access, and a withheld wrap devalues an annotation instead of destroying key material. Nothing server-side was missing — no contracts phase, no backend phase. The structural invariant is now a guard, `scripts/validate_device_shapes.py` (`DeviceRegister`/`DeviceRegisterUpdate` = `{blob, version}`, `MasterKeyWrap` = `{id, method, wrappedKey, kdfParams}`), because the shortcut it fences — a cleartext `wrapIds` list or a `deviceId` on the wrap — would hand a blind operator the device↔wrap correlation while everything kept working. **Two defects it carried:** the DELETE contract said only `DEVICE` was removable while the implementation whitelists `DEVICE` **and** `DEVICE_KEY` — **fixed with B** (`3eeda27`), the contract was the stale side. And `boot.ts` calls `clearDeviceKey(false)`, dropping the local key but **orphaning the server `DEVICE_KEY` wrap** — `boot.ts` is *correct* to skip the server delete (no session to authorise it), and the orphan is afterwards unidentifiable, since the wrap table has **no timestamps** and the client deliberately refuses to clean up on a missing wrap. Its handle is therefore **not** a heuristic but the exclusion-shaped bulk action *"remove every saved key except this device's"* (ADR 0058 §5), whose exclusion is read from this device's own local record. Note also that `RECOVERY` wraps are filtered out of key-material (`KeyMaterialHandler.kt:39-40`), so this list is never "all your unlock methods"; and `MAX_WRAPS_PER_METHOD = 20` is a **per-method** cap, so orphaned `DEVICE_KEY` wraps compete only with other `DEVICE_KEY` wraps. **E3's login-form entry point — BUILT 2026-08-12** (cr-locked-sign-out-entry-point), and the locked ceiling did not rise with C either (both joins are sealed), exactly as this row predicted, so the locked list stays anonymous and the locked action stays session-level. Its durable home is [uc.account.sessions](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.sessions.md) § E3, which carries all five decisions; the two that decided the form: **no locked list** (the counter-argument — an anonymous list with dates is not nothing to someone who signed in on exactly one machine yesterday — was attacked rather than left standing, and loses because the one input that would justify sparing a row lives in the sealed register's `wraps` map and is therefore unreadable in exactly this state); and **after the action succeeds this device signs out too**, so "I cut everything off" means everything. The structural invariant the whole shape rests on — no route is session-gated but not unlock-gated — is now the permanent guard `scripts/validate_shell_route_gate.py` rather than the prose it had been. Two corrections the refine made to its own scoping note, both measured: the dead copy was **nine** keys in both locales, not two (the seven `unlock.*` leftovers of the login/unlock merge, now deleted), and **`LoginView.vue` had no test at all** — E3 is a 100 % LoginView change, so it stood up that view's first harness, which is the cost the "not one button" estimate above never measured. **Relates to:** #12 (account & auth — "sessions" already pending there; device linking), #46 (key model), `uc.account.trust-device` + `uc.account.remember-this-device`, ADR 0004/0005/[0026](decisions/0026-client-trust-tiers.md)/[0044](decisions/0044-field-visibility-declared-at-the-field.md). |
| 84 | Central notifications hub (topbar) replacing the profile-menu "Gruppeneinladungen" | feature, platform, ui, notifications | **refined 2026-07-22 → scoped to a minimal invitations attention-badge; the multi-source hub deferred.** v1 surfaces today's pending **group invitations** (`uc.spaces.consent-group-add`, `GroupInvitationsView`) as an **appears-on-demand top-bar badge** beside the upload-queue tray, retiring the badge-less sidebar `ProfileMenu` entry — a **web-only** presentation refinement (no contracts/backend, no new endpoint). **Operator-blind: settled, no ADR** — the count derives client-side from the existing `listMyGroupInvitations`; the blind server already holds the `pendingConsent` rows, so surfacing them leaks nothing new and no server-visible notification table is added. **Read-state: none** — the signals are self-clearing (accept/decline drops the row); server-persisted read-state was the one path that would reopen operator-blindness (engagement metadata → an ADR) and is explicitly deferred. **Terms stays a hard router gate** (`uc.account.accept-terms`, cr-0114), not folded in. Surfacing + these decisions are recorded in `uc.spaces.consent-group-add`. **Minimal slice BUILT 2026-07-22** (`InvitationsBadge.vue` + module-singleton `useMyInvitations()`; badge in the top bar routing to `/invitations`, sidebar `ProfileMenu` entry retired; refresh on scope entry + after accept/decline; `npm run check` + trust-chain green; CR harvested + deleted). #84 stays open as the **parked hub** topic. Deferred: the true multi-source hub (awaiting-access/grant-delivery, terms re-accept #13, future sources), any read-state, and a background poll (v1 refreshes on shell/scope entry; a poll is polish, cf. #48). Relates to platform/app-shell (#24), spaces group-add/consent (#14), the upload-queue tray (cr-0058). |
| 112 | **Email verification (address-control proof)** | feature, account, email, security, DONE | **done 2026-10-01** (`cr-email-address-confirmation`, deleted) → [ADR 0074](decisions/0074-address-control-is-recorded-where-nothing-else-proves-it.md) (Accepted) + [uc.account.confirm-email-address](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.confirm-email-address.md), built on backend + web. `veyq_user.email_confirmed_at` is set by a redeemed member invite, a completed reset, or a confirmation link opened with the account's own session, bound to the address mailed; it is shown in the Security settings and offered once after a sendable mail-settings save, and it gates nothing — recovery still proves the mailbox at use time. Correcting a wrong address stays with #197; the self-registration gate ADR 0074 Decision 3 writes down stays with #198. |
| 138 | **The terms editor shows every language whether it has terms or not, and "Insert template" asks nothing** | feature, admin, terms, ui, web | **DONE 2026-09-20 (`cr-terms-editor-lists-the-languages-that-have-terms`, web-only, CR deleted). Refined the same day: lift, do not copy.** `LocaleMarkdownEditor` has no runtime coupling to the memory feature (one type-only import of a bare `Record<string,string>`) and already reads its language labels from the **`terms.*`** namespace — it is not share-owned, only share-stored. Counter-argument on the record: [docs/ai/README](ai/README.md) says this repo's clean shared abstractions came from **five** usages, not two, so the props surface stays minimal. **Two things the refine found that the row did not name, and both are the actual work:** the panel holds `de`/`en` as two flat refs where `''` and *absent* are indistinguishable — that indistinguishability IS the defect, and a CR that moves the file without moving the data shape has fixed nothing; and with two supported locales the *add a language* dropdown holds one option or zero, so a `Select` is a control for a choice that does not exist. **inbox (raised 2026-09-20, with the UX decided in the same exchange).** [`TenantTermsPanel.vue`](../web/src/features/tenant/TenantTermsPanel.vue) renders both the DE and the EN textarea unconditionally, and `insertTemplate()` drops the whole 11-clause blob into *every empty* locale with no dialog and no choice. **Wanted:** the panel lists only the languages that actually carry terms, a row opens a markdown editor dialog, and a missing language is added explicitly — which is the shape [`LocaleMarkdownEditor.vue`](../web/src/shared/ui/LocaleMarkdownEditor.vue) (cr-0116, share notes) already has, so the refine decides *lifting it to shared* vs. a second copy, never the copy by default. "Insert template" becomes a dialog that picks the language. **Decided 2026-09-20: the template goes in whole — no per-clause toggling** — which **supersedes this use-case's own main flow step 2** ([uc.admin.tenant-terms](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.tenant-terms.md), *"They pick/toggle clauses from the template"*): that line is now the stale side and the refine corrects it. The decision also disposes of a trap it would otherwise have owned — the clause numbers are literal in the template strings (`## 1.` … `## 11.`), so any subset would have printed 1, 2, 4, … and the numbering would have had to be generated at insert time. **Overwrite is a question, not a silent skip:** if the chosen language already holds text that *differs* from the template, ask before replacing it (identical → no-op, empty → insert). **Two traps measured against [`TenantTermsHandler.kt`](../backend/core/src/main/kotlin/me/raddatz/veyq/core/admin/tenant/TenantTermsHandler.kt):** `normalizeOne` drops a blank locale and an all-blank update is a **silent no-op**, so a language added but left empty simply vanishes on reload — the UI must not offer that state; and deleting a locale *does* work (the handler rewrites the whole map) but **bumps `terms_version` and re-gates every member**, so a delete needs a confirm that says exactly that. **Scope: web-only** — `LocalizedMarkdown` already carries both locales optional ([_common.openapi.yaml](../contracts/api/_common.openapi.yaml)), so there was no contracts and no backend phase. **Shipped:** the editor is **lifted** to [`shared/ui/LocaleMarkdownEditor.vue`](../web/src/shared/ui/LocaleMarkdownEditor.vue) with its first test at all (it had none, and its only caller stubbed it out) and a neutral `localeEditor.*` namespace; the panel holds a **`LocaleMap`**, which is what makes "the languages that carry terms" expressible. The refine's dropdown count was off by one case — with an **empty** map *both* languages are addable — so the control is **one button per addable language**, naming it: the shape the refine wanted, correct for two. Both `TenantTermsHandler` traps are discharged in the UI: the blank-locale drop is stated while the empty language sits there (rather than forbidding the row, which is a legitimate intermediate state), and the delete confirm names the re-gate. **One deliberate divergence from this row:** the insert asks in **both** directions, not only on overwrite — a control whose silence depends on state the owner cannot see is the defect this row exists to remove. The structural invariant is now a guard, [`validate_locale_editor_single_home.py`](../scripts/validate_locale_editor_single_home.py), measured **red** on the pre-CR tree; the CR's own one-shot `grep` proof did **not** work, and its lesson is in [changes/README](../changes/README.md) § *A proof about WHERE a thing lives*. |
| 113 | **Recovery — no-escrow key recovery flow** | feature, account, recovery | **DONE 2026-08-05 (cr-account-recovery, deleted)** → [ADR 0054](decisions/0054-password-recovery-two-factor-release.md) + [uc.account.recover-access](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.recover-access.md). The grounding finding reshaped it: veyq has **no separate recovery code** and never had one — `kekRecovery` takes the Secret Key and no password, so the recovery factor *is* the Emergency Kit, and the wiki had been describing a single-use code that issues a fresh kit. So this is a **password reset**: a mailed one-time token authorizes releasing the withheld `RECOVERY` wrap, and the Secret Key is the second factor — proven since 2026-09-27 by a signature of the account's Secret-Key proof key (#159 (3)), which replaced the stored Master-Key hash the flow first shipped with. Neither factor alone suffices. Built across contracts (auth spec, unauthenticated by necessity), backend (V44 token table, atomic reset that also drops **`DEVICE_KEY`** wraps — they carry no password and would otherwise survive a reset someone triggered *because* a device was lost), and web (`/recover`, `recoveryKekAsync`). Proven by a live e2e over real SMTP that also carries the wire assertion: a Memory created before the reset still opens after it, and its name appears nowhere in the database. **Its one open item is closed** by [ADR 0055](decisions/0055-mail-is-a-precondition-not-a-capability.md) via #118: `POST /api/recovery/request` answers `503 mail_unavailable` instead of a silent `202`, so no capability flag was needed. The client half — `/recover` rendering that `503` instead of silently redrawing the form — landed with `cr-email-member-invite-mail-only`, so nothing here is open. |
| 139 | **Favorites stop at the Memory — the ask is a favorite per *item*, plus a gallery filter "everyone's / only mine"** | feature, memory, media, client, contracts, crypto, privacy | **DONE 2026-09-20.** Refined → [ADR 0068](decisions/0068-favorites-two-confidentiality-levels.md)** + [en.memory.favorites](features/memory/enablers/en.memory.favorites.md) + [uc.memory.gallery](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.gallery.md). All four forks this row raised are decided there, together with the rejected alternatives and the measured ceiling — **this row does not restate them**. The shape, in one line: favorites become **two objects with two audiences** — a private **master-key**-sealed Memory pin (which discharges [ADR 0014](decisions/0014-group-scopes-and-governance.md) §7 and closes a live leak, since a group's shared `K_SC` made every member's pins readable) and a shared `K_SC`-sealed **per-item** mark read back as the scope's per-member blobs. The typed single envelope this row proposed is **rejected**: one blob has one key and therefore one audience. Item marks are partitioned **per node**, chosen because the server has already observed that id, so unlike blinded per-item rows or keyed-hash buckets it costs no new metadata. Presentation is **aggregate-only — a presentation choice, not a confidentiality guarantee**, which is why the leak above was fixed rather than deferred. **Built** (`cr-favorites-two-objects`, deleted): contracts (a per-node `item-favorites` resource whose entries carry a `self` flag and **no member id**, so "never show who marked what" is a property of the data rather than a UI rule), backend (`V49`, the slice, and three reclaim paths — scope purge, node purge, grant revocation), web (the master-key pin seal + its rotation re-seal, the item store, the gallery filter and the per-tile aggregate), coverage. **Two things it taught that outlived it:** a contracts phase on a **new tag** ends green everywhere — the unconditional "cannot end green" in `changes/README.md` was about adding an operation to an *existing* tag, and is now qualified there; and `loadFavorites` swallowing a failed unwrap makes any bite phrased over it unreddenable, a trap now recorded on that `catch`. |

| 122 | **A `uc.`/`en.` id cited in a CODE comment is a reference nothing checks** | docs, trust-chain, guards | **done** (cr-doc-citations-resolve-from-code, 2026-10-01) → `validate_adr_citations.py` became `scripts/validate_doc_citations.py`: a `uc.`/`en.`/`cc.` id cited from code, a spec or an ADR body must name a doc that exists, anchored on the feature directories and `cc.` stems it reads from `docs/`; the code scan roots come from the root `package.json` workspaces, which brought `admin-web/src` and `packages/crypto/src` in. The 12 sites are repointed; the anchor's blind spot (an id under a feature that does not exist) is pinned open as `validate_guard_gaps.py` case 30, the closed hole as 29. Refine measured (2026-09-28, `faf9ef4`): still 7 dangling ids, now at 12 sites; the new one is `contracts/api/scopes.openapi.yaml:263`. Anchored on a feature directory, the predicate had 0 false positives over 413 citing files. Also found: that guard's hard-coded scan misses `admin-web/src` and `packages/crypto/src`, 129 `ADR NNNN` citations nothing checks (all resolve today). Found 2026-08-12 — `validate_doc_links.py` enforces that a reference resolves *inside* `docs/`; **nothing enforces it for the doc ids that live in code comments**, and veyq's code comments cite their governing use-case/enabler in almost every file. Measured over `web/src` + `backend/src/main` + `scripts`: **61 cited ids, 7 dangle** (a grep for `\b(uc|en)\.[a-z0-9-]+\.[a-z0-9-]+` against the stems under `docs/features/`). Three failure modes, and they are different bugs: **wrong prefix** — `en.memory.cover`, `en.memory.date`, `en.spaces.add-group-member`, `uc.spaces.personal-scope` all name a real doc under the *other* kind; **the doc was split since** — `uc.memory.date-place` is cited by 4 files and is today `uc.memory.date` + `uc.memory.place`; **it never existed** — `uc.platform.app-shell` (cited by `boot.ts:1` and `features/account/instance/instance.ts:1`) and `uc.account.profile`. Found by the doc↔code pass of the graphify graph, i.e. by joining the two layers — two independent subagents hit it while reading, a grep then measured the class. **The guard question, both ways.** For: this is not a new invariant, it is the *stated* rule of `validate_doc_links.py` applied to the one surface it cannot see, and 12% drift is not noise. Against: nobody noticed in the whole life of the repo, which is the standing evidence-of-cost test in `docs/ai/README.md` (*'no guard unless a human would notice the defect'*). **A trap for whoever builds it:** the naive predicate is wrong — my first sweep read `.value.trim()` and `.parent.parent` as doc ids. Anchor on a known feature name or on comment context, or the guard cries wolf and gets switched off. Fixing the 7 is cheap and independent of the guard decision. |
| 25 | Architecture & runtime topology | infra | inbox |
| 26 | **Deployment + operations — one topic, #30 dissolved into it.** Under self-hosting "wie deploye ich das" and "wie betreibe ich das" are the same question; the real seam is **artefact vs. procedure** (what the operator *starts* — image, prod compose, config — against what the operator *does*), and [`features/admin/_index.md`](features/admin/_index.md) had already drawn it: the use-case owns goal/failure-modes/acceptance, `infra/` owns the commands. **V1 scope cut (2026-08-06):** ~~helm dropped~~ — **reversed 2026-09-07: Kubernetes is back in scope as a first-class second deployment target (Helm chart) — [ADR 0064](decisions/0064-kubernetes-helm-deployment-target.md); and reversed one step further 2026-09-09 — it does not merely rejoin the roadmap, it GATES V1** (asked directly at refinement; § V1 above carries the same correction, and 0064's Context is amended in place — its "alongside — not instead of" was a statement about the two targets that everyone read as one about timing). **Slice 4 — the chart itself: REFINED 2026-09-09, then deliberately PAUSED before any CR was written, on a question the refinement raised rather than answered (→ #135).** What the refine measured, all of it against the tree and a running store rather than against 0064's prose: (a) the projector has **no sink seam at all** — `TenantIngressProjector.regenerate()` calls a private `writeAtomically(Path.of(path), render(routable))`, and the class KDoc's claim that a K8s sink "reuses the DB-facing half below, not this file writer" describes a seam that does not exist, so extracting one is real work and its own phase; (b) the backend has **zero** Kubernetes dependency (only the `kubernetes-client-bom` *pom* is in the Gradle cache, dragged in by the Quarkus BOM) — decided: `quarkus-kubernetes-client`, chosen for its `@WithKubernetesTestServer` seam as much as for the client, since without it the sink is testable only against a real cluster; (c) tenants keep **their own domains** on Kubernetes (the wildcard-cert shortcut that would delete the whole sink was re-offered and re-rejected, consistent with ADR 0063); (d) **Garage's CORS cannot express a projected origin set** — the measured table now lives in [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-blob-storage-presigned-direct-to-store.md) §4, and its consequence is that `*` is the only working policy on a multi-tenant instance, which was accepted knowingly; (e) the CORS branch additionally needs a **second signing mode**, because `endpointFor` swaps `public-endpoint`'s host for the tenant's — right for compose's path route, wrong for a store on its own host, and 0064 had not said so; (f) `garage-init`'s image is **`build:`-only and unpublished**, so a chart cannot pull it — dissolved by measurement rather than by a third release artifact (Garage's Admin API does every step over plain JSON; a bucket was created and deleted through it); (g) a **PVC does not seed ownership from the image the way a Docker named volume does**, so `/var/lib/veyq` would come up root-owned and reproduce, on the chart's first-ever install, exactly the `AccessDeniedException` restart-loop this row already paid for once — `scripts/validate_app_volume_ownership.py` says in its own limit that it cannot see this. The intended cut was five CRs (ingress sink · blob-on-its-own-host · store bootstrap · the chart · the chart's OCI release). **Unblocked 2026-09-09:** #135 resolved to [ADR 0067](decisions/0067-store-provisioning-from-a-declared-type.md), which deletes `garage-init` and its unpublished image, so the chart has nothing left it cannot pull; and the store bootstrap is no longer a CR of this slice's own — it is ADR 0067's. Two of the five also collapse into one mechanism: ADR 0067 Decision 5 makes "the store has its own address" named signing behaviour with the Kubernetes branch as one caller, so blob-on-its-own-host is not a separate design. The chart's OCI release is now built too, its publish unobserved (status column) — **the chart is BUILT (2026-09-14, `cr-veyq-helm-chart`, deleted): `charts/veyq`, the same five services, every pod `restricted`-compliant (the admin image made unprivileged for it, held by `scripts/validate_images_run_unprivileged.py`), TLS settled as a passthrough with three named modes (ADR 0064 § Decision), the chart generating nothing, `fsGroup` for the KEK's claim (finding (g) below, closed), one app pod under `Recreate`; held on the rendered manifests by `scripts/chart_meta_check.py`, outside the buildless glob because it needs `helm`; the OCI release was the last open sub-slice because its only proof is a CI run** — and **the ingress sink is BUILT (2026-09-11, `cr-tenant-ingress-kubernetes-sink`, deleted)**, which closes measurements (a) and (b) of this row: `TenantIngressSink` is a real seam (`TenantIngressProjector` keeps the DB-facing half; `TraefikFileIngressSink` and `KubernetesIngressSink` are the two implementations, named by `veyq.tenant.ingress.sink` rather than inferred), and `quarkus-kubernetes-client` is the backend's first Kubernetes dependency, chosen for `@WithKubernetesTestServer` — an in-JVM fabric8 control plane, which is the only proof a repo with no cluster can offer. The two properties a file sink never needed are the ones held by test: the reconcile **deletes** a withdrawn domain's `Ingress`, and it lists **by** its `app.kubernetes.io/managed-by: veyq-tenant-ingress` label, so an operator's own object in the same namespace is never a deletion candidate. What the sink left the chart, beyond the RBAC 0064 already names, was TLS: it emitted **no TLS block**, and cert-manager is driven by an annotation on an object the backend writes at runtime — closed 2026-09-14 by the passthrough: the sink stamps `spec.tls` per named mode plus the operator's annotation map, verbatim, and configures nothing (0064 § Decision carries it). Still owed from this row: **blob-on-its-own-host is written**: ADR 0067 Decision 5 is `changes/cr-store-own-address-signing-mode.md` (2026-09-10), beside `changes/cr-store-provisioning-from-a-declared-type.md` for the bootstrap. `garage-init` goes from every stack, so the chart has nothing left it cannot pull and development runs the production shape. The 2026-08-06 reasoning ("zero k8s artefacts in the tree and the §10 self-hostability bar is literally one `docker compose up` + a JVM") held for a single-host, single-tenant story; it stopped holding once multi-domain, multi-tenant ingress ([ADR 0063](decisions/0063-database-driven-tenant-ingress.md)) became a real capability docker-compose alone can't fully serve, and the operator wants to offer the chart in-repo. The reversal also pulls in an admin-UI/CLI extraction into its own container ([ADR 0065](decisions/0065-admin-ui-cli-separate-service.md)), shipped on both deployment targets. **Slice 3 — the admin-surface extraction: BUILT 2026-09-08 (`cr-admin-web-extraction`, web half) and 2026-09-09 (`cr-admin-cli`, the CLI half); both deleted.** `admin-web/` is its own package and its own image (`ghcr.io/<owner>/veyq-admin`), built and cosign-signed by the same publish as the app so the two pin together; that image also ships **`veyq-admin`**, a Node CLI over the same generated client the UI uses (eight tenant verbs + `release`; setup is deliberately not a command, it mints key material and shows the Emergency Kit once) — fresh login per invocation from `VEYQ_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`VEYQ_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, nothing on disk, ~2s of Argon2id per call, measured in the built image; nginx serves it and reverse-proxies `/api/*` from its **own** origin, which is what keeps the Server-Owner refresh cookie same-origin and — the point — makes first-run setup independent of DNS and TLS. Two exposure modes, one image, one variable: `ADMIN_DOMAIN` unset creates no Traefik router at all and binds `127.0.0.1:${ADMIN_PORT}`; set, it gets its own router + certificate. **The sequencing claim was RUN, not asserted:** whole prod stack up with `VEYQ_DOMAIN` pointed at an NXDOMAIN name and `ADMIN_DOMAIN` unset → setup, Emergency Kit, sign-in, console, tenant create (invite copy-link intact) and the release panel, all over `http://localhost:<admin-port>`. `shared/crypto/v1` + `accountBirth` + `base64` moved into `packages/crypto` (`@veyq/crypto`), the repo's first npm workspace and the ONLY shared package — a one-character change to its Argon2id params reddens both surfaces' account-birth suites, which is the one-copy proof. **Two things the ADR itself had measured wrong, both amended in place:** per-tenant mail settings and terms authoring are **tenant-OWNER** surfaces (`403 not_tenant_owner`, mounted off `activeSpace.tenantId`) and stayed in `web/` — moving them would have taken a tenant's own configuration away from every owner who is not also the Server Owner; and the generated-client scope needed `auth.openapi.yaml`, without which nothing there can obtain a token. **One defect it found and FIXED immediately after (2026-09-08):** a first-ever `up` of the prod stack could not start the app at all. `/etc/traefik/dynamic` — the ADR 0063 ingress projection's mount point — did not exist in either runtime image, so Docker had nothing to seed the named volume from and it came up `root:root`; the app runs as uid 10001 and `TenantIngressProjector.onStartup` died with `AccessDeniedException`, restart-looping. The **sibling** mount on `/var/lib/veyq` was correct and the `Dockerfile` comment above it spells the mechanism out in six lines — the rule was written down and the volume added a year later simply did not inherit it, which is the argument for `scripts/validate_app_volume_ownership.py`: it derives the set from the compose file, so a third volume enrols itself. Five injections bite, one of which caught the guard's own first predicate silently passing (`\s` matched newlines, so `mkdir` chewed into the next `chown`'s arguments). Proven by a first-ever `up` on fresh volumes with **no** manual chown: app started in 2.5 s, `tenants.yml` written, `GET /api/setup` 200. **Re-proving that on 2026-09-09 (`b6f4c88`) turned up two more on the same path, both fixed there.** `TenantIngressProjector.writeAtomically` could still kill startup: its catch opened one line too late, with `createDirectories` and `createTempFile` above it — so the one failure ever observed was the one it did not cover. Fixing the ownership removed the cause for compose; this removes the coupling, which matters because the guard reads compose files and says nothing about [ADR 0064](decisions/0064-kubernetes-helm-deployment-target.md)'s chart. And Traefik **rejected the projection on every fresh instance**: it refuses a section that is present but empty, so the `routers: {}` the empty branch emitted logged `"routers cannot be a standalone element"` on every reload — measured against the pinned v3.6, along with the shapes it does accept. No routing was lost, but every freshly deployed instance has zero tenants, so every new operator met a proxy insisting their routing was broken while debugging their first deploy. Both proven by a second first-ever `up` built from the fixed tree: 0 `standalone element` errors against the 2 the same stack produced before. **The second defect it found is CLOSED too (2026-09-08, `5669914`):** `web/`'s password floor + breach warning had exactly one caller, `SetupView`, which moved out — so the three remaining password-set points were left enforcing no minimum length and showing no breach warning. `passwordPolicy.ts` / `pwned.ts` / `passwordWarning.ts` are back and wired at `ChangePasswordPanel` (new password only), `TenantJoinView` (register branch only) and `RecoverView`. The client is the WHOLE control, not a convenience over a server check: under Model B ([ADR 0003](decisions/0003-operator-blind-password-auth.md)) the backend never receives an account password, so the obvious later 'hardening' is one the operator-blind boundary forbids — said in place at every site. Where it must NOT apply is pinned as negative-control cases (`TenantJoinView`'s sign-in and re-auth branches, `ChangePasswordPanel`'s CURRENT-password field), because gating those would lock an owner whose password predates the policy out of accepting their own invite. En route: a disabled submit button was not the gate — Enter submitted a five-character password past it. Also closed en route: **nothing anywhere asserted the production Argon2id cost** — `t: 3 → t: 4` in `v1.ARGON` left all 1062 tests green, and Stryker has no numeric-literal mutator, so a silent downgrade was invisible to every gate; now pinned against the contract's own `kdf_params` vector. **Channels reduced to the one already built** (`container.yml`: ghcr, multi-arch, cosign, `latest` + `N.x`), no stable/beta/edge matrix; **updates kept**, but its actual content is that `quarkus.flyway.migrate-at-start=true` with no `%prod` gate *is* today's undecided update mechanism. **Slice 1 — backup/restore: refined → [ADR 0056](decisions/0056-instance-durable-state-and-cold-logical-backup.md) + [uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance.md) + coverage (`status: planned`).** Decided: three legs not two (DB + store objects + the `config-secret.key` file, whose loss boots cleanly and then refuses invites and resets); the capture is **cold in one precise sense — stop the app, leave the stores up**, which makes "row present, object absent" structurally impossible and deletes the whole ordering argument; the store leg is captured **logically via `rclone`** (verifiable against the blob rows, vendor- and version-neutral, restore target recreated by `garage-init.sh`) with volume-copy kept only as the same-host fast path; **same domain only**; the fingerprint check is the restore's final step. Measured en route: `garage meta snapshot` exists and works on the pinned v2.0.0 but covers **only** metadata — `block` has no snapshot, so even the volume path is not fully served by the store's own tooling. **Slice 2 — versioning: refined 2026-08-10 → [ADR 0057](decisions/0057-release-identity-and-deployment-recipe.md) + [uc.admin.identify-instance-release](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.identify-instance-release.md); BUILT 2026-08-11.** Decided: the version comes from a `VERSION` file in the **tree**, not from a registry count — which is what makes it checkable locally, the only kind of proof a repository with no remote can offer; a publish onto an existing tag **fails** instead of replacing it (the old registry query's `|| printf '[]'` made a transient API error indistinguishable from "nothing published yet", and both ended at `${major}.1`); exactly one git ref `v<major>.<minor>` per release; and the production compose file **travels inside the signed image**, so the recipe cannot drift from the bits it describes and inherits the image's cosign signature instead of needing a second trust path. Also decided: **two version notions that never merge** — the pre-auth `major`/`build` is the *client's* compatibility gate (and stays, because a native client is the only surface that can ever drift from its server), the release version is the *operator's* rollback target and is Server-Owner-only, because a precise release number is what maps an instance to a CVE list. Measured en route: `scripts/instance_backup.py:442` writes `app.version` from the **capture process's host environment**, where `VEYQ_VERSION` is never set — so every manifest to date records `null` in the one field that would name a rollback target. **What slice 2 shipped (2026-08-11):** a `VERSION` file (`1.0`); `container.yml` derives the version from it with **no network call at all** and refuses a republish (image tag *and* `v<major>.<minor>` ref), creating that ref itself — so a **pushed tag is no longer a trigger**, which answers the open question below: a push to `main` is the release, a tag is its consequence. The prod compose + `.env.example` ride in **both** runtime targets at `/opt/veyq/deploy/` (proven by extracting them from a built image and running `docker compose config -q` on the extract). Server-Owner-only `GET /api/instance/release` (new `contracts/api/instance.openapi.yaml`) + a Settings → Server → Release panel; `GET /api/setup` unchanged. The manifest's `app.version` now reads the app **container** (`docker inspect`, so a *stopped* app — the cold-capture state — still answers) and records which absence it is instead of a structural `null`. Held by `scripts/validate_release_identity.py`, which **executes** the workflow's version step offline; 11 injections bite. **Closed since this row was written:** the prod-compose KEK defect (`infra/docker-compose.prod.yml` mounts `veyq-config-secret` and `scripts/validate_config_secret_persistence.py` holds three files to that path); whether the first container run publishes or only builds. **Still open:** the upgrade procedure (→ #121, now unblocked); the in-image recipe is the two files only, so `garage.toml` still comes from the repo and an extracted recipe is not yet self-sufficient — **narrowed 2026-09-10**, since ADR 0067 deleted `garage-init.sh` and `garage-init.Dockerfile` along with the image that ran them, leaving `garage.toml` as the whole residual dependency; narrowed again 2026-09-14, since the chart carries its own copy as a `ConfigMap` — the compose recipe still reads it from the repo. **The chart's ACME opt-in is BUILT (2026-09-15, `cr-chart-acme-issuer`, deleted):** measured against the compose recipe, ADR 0064's reference/configure cut runs between the *sink* and the *recipe*, not between the backend and the whole deployment — so the chart, as the recipe's counterpart, templates one namespaced cert-manager `Issuer` `veyq-acme` on opt-in (`ingress.tls.acme.*`, HTTP-01, e-mail required, Let's Encrypt production by default like `VEYQ_ACME_CASERVER`) and merges `cert-manager.io/issuer: veyq-acme` into the one annotation map both the chart's own `Ingress` metadata and the backend's properties file read, while the sink keeps stamping references and the backend's `Role` is unchanged; `values.schema.json` refuses the opt-in beside any mode but `per-domain`, without an e-mail, or beside an issuer annotation of the operator's own. Held by `scripts/chart_meta_check.py`, which now renders every `charts/veyq/ci/*.yaml` and asserts both annotation consumers apart — now run by `container.yml`'s test job, which no run has executed yet. En route the rate-limit prose on both targets was corrected: 5 failed validations lock a hostname for an **hour**; the week is a cap of 50 **issued** certificates per registered domain. | infra, deploy, ops | **refining** — slice 1 refined 2026-08-06, slice 2 **built 2026-08-11** **2026-10-14: the chart's OCI release is BUILT, publish unobserved (`cr-release-pipeline-and-chart-oci`, deleted)**, together with the publish it rides on: a push only builds; a publish is dispatched by hand as a snapshot (default) or a release, the minor a commit count, major `0` pre-release (ADR 0057 §1-2); the chart is packaged with the release version, pushed to `ghcr.io/<owner>/charts/veyq`, signed, and refused on a republish (ADR 0064), all held by `scripts/validate_release_identity.py`. What stays open is the owner's staged first run, in the order infra/README § Cutting a release lists — no remote, so nothing has run. |

The mechanism is done, not merely "refined" — measured 2026-07-22. |
| 100 | **The agent dev-loop has no sandbox** | infra, security, supply-chain, tooling, NEW | **inbox (grounded 2026-07-15).** The lethal trifecta is fully assembled here: untrusted input (repo content, dependencies, docs) + private data (S3 creds, `~/.claude.json`, the live local instance) + network. Claude Code's built-in Bash sandbox **does not cover** Read/Edit/WebFetch, MCP servers or hooks — its own docs say those run unconstrained on the host. The threat is not the agent going rogue: in the nx/s1ngularity attack (Aug 2025) the payload wrote **no recon logic at all** — it invoked the already-installed, already-authenticated AI CLIs using their own documented flags (`--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--yolo`, `--trust-all-tools`) and harvested 1,000+ valid GitHub tokens. The agent was the confused deputy, and it shipped the switch for its own guardrails. Candidates: `@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime` (wraps the whole process — every tool, hook and MCP server — default-deny write + network) or a devcontainer with a default-deny egress firewall. Scope note: this repo's **CI is not the exposed surface** — no `pull_request_target`/`issue_comment`/`workflow_run` triggers and no agent actions (verified 2026-07-15), so CVE-2026-47751 and the Clinejection class do not apply. The gap is the local loop only, where the sole control today is the allow-list in the un-checked-in `.claude/settings.local.json`. To settle at refine: whether the friction is worth it for a solo local loop, and whether a sandbox that must allow-list the project dir + S3 + the local instance still constrains anything meaningful. |
| 101 | **Two dead pieces of tooling — one of them not marked dead** | ci, infra, tooling, hygiene, NEW | **DONE 2026-07-21 — all three items; nothing open.** Grounded 2026-07-15: `pages.yml` triggers on `site/**` + `docs/api/**` — **neither path exists** — and copies `docs/api/*.openapi.yaml`, but the contracts live under `contracts/api/`. It can never fire on push, remains triggerable via `workflow_dispatch`, and would then fail; it holds `pages: write` + `id-token: write`. The contrast **is** the finding: `wiki-sync.yml` sits in the identical state and is cleanly disabled with its staleness explained in place (`:28-31`, naming both causes and the fact that fixing means rewriting the paths, not restoring) — the debt was declared where someone looked, and the neighbour was missed. Same shape as `web-e2e`'s honest `if: false`. **`.claude/launch.json` DONE (2026-07-21):** removed 3 stale dev-server configs — `veyq-legacy-smoke` (the only one that truly dangled: `cd .../frontend`, gone since `7a1c4e9f`; not transferable — `veyq-web-smoke` already covers web smoke) plus `veyq-web-cr54`/`veyq-web-cr110` (this row's original claim that these pointed at `frontend/` was wrong — both already `cd .../web`; they were stale per-done-CR scratch configs, cr-0054/cr-0110 long DONE, so removed as scaffolding-deleted-when-done). Kept the 3 generically-named ones (`veyq-web`, `veyq-web-smoke`, `veyq-web-gallery`). **DONE 2026-07-21:** `pages.yml` **deleted** — it never functioned (dead `site/**`+`docs/api/**` triggers, copied from a non-existent `docs/api/`), so there was nothing correct to preserve; deleting also removed its dangling `pages: write` + `id-token: write`. `wiki-sync.yml` **revived** — retargeted `docs/wiki`→`wiki/`, dropped the gone PlantUML render step (Mermaid renders natively on the Wiki), clone into `wiki-remote` so the target doesn't collide with the repo's own `wiki/`, `if: false` removed. `architecture §11`'s wiki-sync debt row removed (no longer debt). |
| 121 | **A `curl \| bash` server-management script — start, stop, backup, restore.** | infra, ops, self-hosting | **refined (2026-08-10); UNBLOCKED 2026-08-11 — #26 slice 2 shipped the release identity, so an image can now be built, named and pinned.** The archived repo has one on `main`: `scripts/veyq-server.sh`, **1181 lines of bash**, advertised exactly this way. Read it before designing — it is a model for the *shell*, not the *content*, and the three commands wanted here are the three it never had. It carries `install setup upgrade auto-upgrade status doctor backup`: no `start`, no `stop`, no `restore`, only a `compose up -d` inside `install`. Its `backup` is a `.env` copy plus a `mariadb-dump` plus a `tar czf` of the media directory — **two legs, not three** (the config-secret KEK did not exist yet), in exactly the opaque volume-copy form [ADR 0056](decisions/0056-instance-durable-state-and-cold-logical-backup.md) §3 rejected, and with nothing that reads it back. It also carries dead weight: instance-wide `MAIL_*` (SMTP is per-tenant since ADR 0048 and its password is sealed under the KEK), a WebAuthn key, a Journal collaboration sidecar, a reset profile, CIFS media volumes. **The real argument for building it** is none of that convenience: `scripts/instance_backup.py` states in its own docstring that it cannot check the one precondition the procedure has — *cold means the writer is stopped* — because it cannot know what "the app" is on this host. A shell that owns the compose file **can** know, so it can stop the app, capture, and start it again instead of trusting the operator's discipline. **Refined 2026-08-10 — the topic split, and the half that was blocking everything went first.** The row's own strongest argument (the tool cannot check that the app is stopped) is a **correctness** hole, not a distribution one, and needs no `curl` at all. The real blocker turned out to be deeper than the missing raw URL: **no image exists and none can be built** — `container.yml` dies on a missing `VERSION` — so bootstrap has nothing to install and upgrade has no target. That half became #26 slice 2 and is **built**; this row is what remains. **Decided here, to be picked up by the tool CR.** (1) Bash vs. *Python, bash only for thin glue*: 1181 lines violates it outright — a thin bash bootstrap, everything else Python, `start`/`stop` as genuine glue, `backup`/`restore` **calling** `instance_backup.py` rather than reimplementing a second, worse backup. (2) The `curl … \| bash` posture is answered by [ADR 0057](decisions/0057-release-identity-and-deployment-recipe.md): the recipe rides **inside the cosign-signed image**, so the only unsigned artifact in the chain is the one-liner itself, and the bootstrap's real job includes `cosign verify` — offer the pipe, document the verify-then-run form beside it. (3) **The env contract is not a list to maintain — it is `docker compose config -q`** against *that version's* compose file. Of the 9 required variables only **two** are operator knowledge (`VEYQ_DOMAIN`, `VEYQ_ACME_EMAIL`); five are pure randomness the bootstrap should generate. The check must run **before** the backup and the stop: compose reports missing variables one at a time at `up`, which is the worst possible moment. **Two measured defects that were this row's, both SHIPPED 2026-08-17 — it inherits neither, and the trap they left is worth keeping.** (a) `scripts/instance_backup.py`'s `docker compose … exec` emits no `-p`, and no compose file carried a top-level `name:`, so Compose derived the project from the *directory* and `capture --compose-file infra/docker-compose.prod.yml` resolved project `infra`. On a host that also runs the dev stack — which is every developer's, and the one any rehearsal runs on — that is **worse than a miss**: it succeeds, captures the dev instance, and reports a backup, so nothing tells the operator they hold the wrong one. (b) The remedy was one line and not a tool: `docker-compose.prod.yml` now declares `name: veyq`, retiring the `-p veyq` rule at every site. It is held by `scripts/validate_release_identity.py` (the project is a property of the file, and no tracked file instructs the flag) and rehearsed live by `scripts/run_web_e2e.py`, which runs the documented capture twice one flag apart. **What still belongs here:** the network a procedure has to name is `<project>_default`, so a runbook prints `veyq_default` for prod and `infra_default` for dev — a tool that owns the compose file can *derive* that instead of printing it, which is the same argument as (3) above about the env contract. |

| 32 | **Crypto conformance vectors — no stated rule for what earns one, and it runs in both directions** | contracts, crypto, meta | **dissolved into #160 (2026-09-23).** [ADR 0072](decisions/0072-a-seal-binds-its-object.md) Decision 6 is the rule this row asked for: a live seal purpose earns exactly one vector, a vector with no live purpose is deleted (`invite-temp-key`, `session-token`), and a conformance test compares the typed seal union with the vector set in both directions — so the count stops being a measurement anyone has to repeat. `cr-seal-binds-its-object` builds it. The measuring trap this row recorded — each of three predicates missed a different subset (literal contexts, non-exported constants, `sym_`/`asym_` prefix stripping) — is why that CR's locator runs from two angles. |
| 103 | **Closed sets the contract does not declare** | contracts, api, codegen | **DONE 2026-07-21 (cr-contract-closed-set-enums).** The four bare-`string` closed sets now declare enums: `role` → `[OWNER, MEMBER]` on `MyTenant`/`ScopeMember`/`ScopeSummary` (inline, mirroring the sibling `SetMemberRoleRequest`; a named `$ref` would break `validate_scope_model_vocabulary.py`'s anchor), `JobStatus.status` → `[pending, failed]` (the fail/decline **response** union, not the 4-value column lifecycle). Decision resolved **yes-close**: unlike the deliberately-open error `code`, both are bounded — `role` by ADR 0007/0014 + the vocab guard, `status` by fail/decline semantics. Both drift/vocab guards stayed green with zero guard edits. Fallout measured by the compiler (locator, not tally): 5 backend `.valueOf` sites, 2 web test fixtures — the "~19 test refs" estimate over-counted. |

| 41 | SaaS: **Subscription** as a billing layer between the deployment (veyq.cloud) and Spaces | saas, billing, scopes, NEW | inbox  **Gained a second half 2026-09-09, out of [ADR 0067](decisions/0067-store-provisioning-from-a-declared-type.md)'s refinement: who owns a tenant's object store, and on what terms.** The owner's SaaS model has a customer either buying storage through veyq or bringing their own bucket (e.g. straight from AWS) and pointing their tenant at it — which makes the store connection per-tenant, and that is a **billing** shape as much as a technical one: a tenant on their own bucket pays their own storage bill, so veyq bills for the service and not the bytes. **The decision this owes is a trust one, and it is not ADR 0067's:** SigV4 signs under the secret, so a *central* operator presigning against a customer's own bucket holds that customer's infrastructure credentials with read, write and delete on it. [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-blob-storage-presigned-direct-to-store.md)'s reason that backend-held store credentials are unproblematic is literally *"End users do **not** bring their own S3"* — true today, false in this model, so that sentence is a justification to be brought up to date when this is decided, not a prohibition to be worked around. Two standard answers with different costs: a credential **scoped to the one bucket**, or an **assumed role** (AWS STS `AssumeRole` + `ExternalId`, an added module of the SDK already in use) so no long-lived secret is held at all. Content stays E2EE either way — this is about the customer's infrastructure, never their data. The shape it needs is already preserved (ADR 0067: the type sits on the connection; own-address signing is named behaviour), and the one trap is the reclaim tombstone carrying no tenant. **The storage quota is keyed to this question**: [ADR 0073](decisions/0073-storage-quota-belongs-to-whoever-pays-for-the-store.md) §1 gives the ceiling to whoever pays for the store connection, so a tenant on its own bucket sets its own, and this row decides who may write the quota on the tenant row once a connection can belong to a tenant.|
| 324 | **veyq becomes a platform; Memories is its first application** | platform, architecture, applications, NEW | **refined 2026-10-03** → [ADR 0079](decisions/0079-veyq-is-a-platform-memories-is-its-first-application.md) + [docs/apps/](apps/README.md). Decided with the owner. **Phase 1 is done 2026-10-04** (backend boundaries inside the single Gradle module, the core → application import guard). **The web side is done 2026-10-04** (`web/src/features/memory` is the application; core imports it only through `web/src/applications.ts`; the shell asks a build-time registry; `validate_web_core_imports_no_applications.py` has no allowlist; what it taught lives in [`web/CLAUDE.md`](../web/CLAUDE.md) § Core and application). **Phase 2 is done 2026-10-05** (cr-platform-gradle-modules, closed): the backend is the Gradle modules `:core` and `:apps:memories` composed into one fast-jar, every package named for its owner, the generic blob upload and quota and the `FOLDER` placement in core with Memories behind fail-closed seams (`UploadCompletion`, `UploadAdmission`, `NodeKindRules`), the contracts and generated API split by owner, the schema two pinned baselines under one timestamp version scheme, each module's tests in that module (`:core`'s with no application); the direction is the build's plus `validate_backend_module_graph.py` (no application → application). What it taught lives in [ADR 0079](decisions/0079-veyq-is-a-platform-memories-is-its-first-application.md) Consequences, [`backend/CLAUDE.md`](../backend/CLAUDE.md) and at the code. **Left:** node URLs are built (#328, 2026-10-11: the fragment path with a folder slash and each application's slug; open only the real-browser click-through) (the cover/revision generalisation is done, #341, and core reads no `veyq.media` key, #342); on the web the `features/memory` name (the single-claim slots are gone: dialogs and sections are per kind, `cr-files-application` P4); `wiki/` (EN+DE) not updated; and three things only the owner's machine or a remote can show — whether Dependabot's `gradle` entry (`directory: "/backend"`) follows the included subprojects (a `directories:` list if not), `quarkusDev` hot reload across the workspace modules with the IntelliJ `Run Backend` configuration, and the **[LIVE]** proofs the split's last step could not run here: `docker build .` and `scripts/run_web_e2e.py` against the final fast-jar. |

| 328 | **Node URLs: the path in the fragment, a folder with a slash, an application with its slug** | platform, web, routing, DONE | **built 2026-10-11, not yet clicked through in a real browser** — decided with the owner 2026-10-11, which **supersedes** the first decision of 2026-10-04 (history-mode `/<app-slug>/:nodeId`, no name in any URL) and its assessment (a contradiction with [ADR 0015](decisions/0015-operator-blind-readable-urls.md), three open gaps). **The final design:** the content path stays in the `#fragment` (ADR 0015: operator-blind, readable, resolved client-side) and the scope prefixes are unchanged (token-less tenant, `~username`, `@group`). After the token: a **folder** is its slug path with a trailing slash (`#foo/memory/`); a **Depot** node is its plain slug (`#foo/memory`, Depot declares it has no segment, `claimNoRouteSegment`); every **other application's** node carries the application's route slug before its name (`#foo/memories/memory`, chapters extend it, sub-pages are the application's), claimed once in the registry (`claimRouteSlug`, a second claim throws, platform words refused). A claimed slug is **reserved** among folder and file names (a folder `Memories` is `memories-2`); folders, files and each application's nodes are **numbered apart** (file `a.b` and folder `a-b` need no `-2`; equal names within a class keep the creation-order numbering, `anna`/`anna-2`); without a trailing slash a path means the file if one has the slug, else the folder, with it only the folder, and `…/memory/bar` always reads a folder. The row's three gaps vanish with the design: no scope lookup (the scope is the prefix), FOLDER's slug is the slash, `~username` stays; no history path, so no node id reaches the operator's logs, and **no backend or contract change**. **Built:** ADR 0015 amended (the grammar, the dropped idea), the registry's `claimRouteSlug`/`claimNoRouteSegment`/`routeOf` (`registry.ts`), `location.ts` (`parseHash` keeps the slash, `buildHash`/`hashForNode` emit it, `segmentsOf` numbers per class and around reserved slugs, `resolveSlugPath` with the application segment and a backtracking file-before-folder reading), the host (`MemoryHomeView.vue`) and guard `validate_web_fragment_paths_via_location.py` (only `location.ts` builds or splits a fragment path). The service worker and the share link build no content path (a share link is `/s/:id#k=…`), and the deep-link restore after login keeps the whole fullPath. **Consequences, stated:** an old segment-less Memory bookmark (`#foo/rom`) falls back to the scope root, and a client that drops a folder's trailing slash opens the file when one shares the slug. **Open:** a real-browser click-through (the e2e suites were not run), and the wiki. |
| 329 | **`PublicShare` carries the root node's kind** | platform, sharing, contracts, web, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-05 — `PublicShare.rootKind` is served for every unlocked role, `WRITE_ONLY` included, and omitted on a locked response and while the root is in the trash; the public share page dispatches on it, and `DROP_SHARE` is retired** (cr-platform-contract-split slice 5). The backend reads the kind off the share's root row past the password gate (`ShareRootKindSliceTest`, raw JSON); the property carries `JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` because the backend's Jackson otherwise writes `"rootKind": null` (measured). The web head reads `share.rootKind` instead of deriving it from `nodes`, share bodies are keyed by the kind token, and Memories claims `MEMORY` only. |

## Code health & audit findings (2026-07-11)

Three platform-wide audits (security · code-quality · tests) run 2026-07-11 via fan-out subagents. Each row
is a **thematic CR bundle** grouping related findings. Order = *guardrails first*: **P0** security fix, **P1**
regression guardrails, **P2** real bugs + hardening, **P3** structural consolidation ("missing shared
primitives"), **P4** god-components. **planned** rows are direct-CR-able (bug/cleanup/tooling on existing
state); **inbox (refine)** rows need a sparring pass (± an ADR) before a CR, per "refined state before the CR".

| id | topic | tags | source | status |
|----|-------|------|--------|--------|
| 110 | **`validate_coverage.py` hardened for reference *substance* — the axis #62 was not** — #62 (P1b) was reference **resolution** (does the ref point into the void): class-scoped resolution (cr-0158), the `coverage`/`gaps` overlap ban + `http`-not-a-surface ([ADR 0040](decisions/0040-coverage-gaps-and-http-not-a-surface.md), #81+#82). **All done** — nothing of #62 was open. What *was* open is a **different axis, same filename**: reference **substance** (does the resolved target carry weight), pinned as documented gaps by `validate_guard_gaps.py` (cr-0180). Filing that under "#62" is the trap — resolution ≠ substance. **Closed (`measured`: `for f in scripts/validate_*.py; do python3 $f; done` → all green; fallout **0** across 37 coverage YAMLs):** gap 1 — a web ref must now exist **and** match `*.test`/`*.spec` (`WEB_TEST_RE`; the only non-test hit corpus-wide was `share.ts` **in a comment**); gap 2 — a backend ref's resolved method body is now read, an empty `{}` block rejected while a Kotlin **expression body** `= expr` still counts (`_kotlin_funs`; the edge that made naive `{}`-detection wrong — 2/227 refs delegate via `= assertWriteGate(...)`). The two `validate_guard_gaps` cases were **flipped, not deleted** (`expect_pass True→False`) — the meta-table is now the history of *closed* holes, not just open ones. **Gap 4b left open by decision** (lying `status: planned`): the obvious rule "planned ⇒ no refs" is *false* — `uc.spaces.verify-identity` is legitimately `planned` yet carries a real ref (a partially-built UC); the narrow "every criterion covered ⇒ contradiction" rule shuts only the full, never the partial evasion. Counter-example recorded in `validate_guard_gaps.py`'s 4b docstring so nobody re-derives the false rule. **Substance ≠ weight:** a `*.test` file that asserts nothing still passes — oracle correctness stays [ADR 0045](decisions/0045-mutation-testing-trust-chain-unit-layer.md)'s job (edge #1), never this guard's. | tooling, ci, guard, trust-chain, NEW | **DONE 2026-07-21 (#62 resolution-axis done since ADR 0040; this closed the substance-axis holes cr-0180 pinned); no CR — record lives in the guard docstrings** |

| 93 | **`ScopesStore.myTenants` can report the wrong role** — it does `MembershipEntity.find(“userId”, userId).groupBy { it.tenantId }` then `memberships.first().role` (`ScopesStore.kt:390`). Every scope birth mints its creator an `OWNER` row carrying that `tenantId` — personal-scope auto-birth alone guarantees one — so an ordinary member holds ≥2 memberships with the tenant's id (`MEMBER` on the root scope, `OWNER` on their personal scope), and `.first()` over an unordered `find` may return the personal row → **`MyTenant.role` = `OWNER` for a plain member**. Same shape as cr-0163 (a role read keyed on `tenantId` rather than the root `scopeId`) in a spot that sweep did not reach. **Not** a privilege escalation — `myTenants` is a display field, not a gate (`TenantOwnerGateTest` pins the gates) — so the damage is a wrong badge/affordance in the switcher. Untested: `ScopesSliceTest.kt:479` only ever seeds a real owner. Fix = select the root-scope membership (`scopeId == tenantId`). **Merges into the #58 icon CR** if that lands first: selecting the root-scope row fixes `role` *and* yields the `wrappedGrant` the widened `my-tenants` needs, in one edit. **DONE 2026-07-21 (cr-tenant-role-and-v24-comment):** `myTenants` role now keys on the root scope (`scopeId == tenantId`), mirroring cr-0163, with a `?: MEMBER` fallback so a belonged-to tenant (belonging is by `tenantId`, `MembershipStore.kt:30`) is never dropped from the switcher. Slice test seeds a member with a personal-scope OWNER row (same `tenantId`) → asserts `MEMBER`; bite-proven (reverting to `.first().role` reds it). The `wrappedGrant` widening stays for the space-icon CR. | backend, bug, authorization-adjacent, NEW | #58 refine (2026-07-15) | **DONE 2026-07-21** |
| 94 | **`@Size`/`maxLength` caps exceeding MariaDB's `TEXT` byte-limit, unguarded — and H2 hid it.** Full inventory measured: **5 columns** over-declared, not 2. Four are base64 (char == byte): `identity_pins.sealed_blob` (1 MiB), `scope_favorites.ciphertext` + `job.sealed_body` + `job.sealed_result` (each 65 536 = 64 KiB, one byte over). The fifth is the byte-vs-char sleeper the grounding surfaced — `tenant.terms_markdown_by_locale`, **free-text** (not base64): a per-locale JSON map of two 20 000-char markdown fields under utf8mb4 (≤4 B/char) → ~160 KB worst case, no single cap over `TEXT` yet the column overflows. **Damage corrected from the inbox's premise:** under the shipped `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES` it is a hard `ERROR 1406` → 500 on a contract-valid body (measured on real MariaDB 11.4.12), *not* silent truncation — that's the strict-off fallback. Every cap is a deliberate ceiling (`65 535` isn't even a valid base64 length — a multiple of 4 is), so the fix widened the **columns**, never the caps. **DONE 2026-07-21 (cr-maxlength-fits-column):** V41 `MODIFY COLUMN … MEDIUMTEXT` ×5 + entity `columnDefinition` sync; guard `validate_maxlength_storage.py` (base64 cap: multiple of 4, ≤ 65 532 unless on a self-pinning MEDIUMTEXT allowlist verified against the migration) + `validate_guard_gaps.py` cases 7/8 (oversize base64 → rejected; free-text overflow → still passes, the documented open gap — both directions run for real). **The migration mechanism was the real fork:** there is **no** type-change DDL in the H2(default) ∩ MariaDB subset (measured both engines: MariaDB takes only `MODIFY COLUMN`, default-H2 only `ALTER COLUMN … SET DATA TYPE`, each rejects the other) → the test datasource now runs H2 with **`MODE=MariaDB`**, a **parity** decision that shrinks the divergence class #94 came from (recorded in `backend/CLAUDE.md`); it does **not** enforce the byte ceiling — the guard does. Proven: `./gradlew check` green under the mode, and a baseline-vs-mode diff showed **392 tests, identical outcomes, 0 behavior changes**. The #58 icon (`sealedIcon`, 21 884) is provably not among the offenders. | backend, contracts, bug, prod-only, NEW | #58 refine (2026-07-15); done cr-maxlength-fits-column | **DONE 2026-07-21 → 5 columns MEDIUMTEXT; `validate_maxlength_storage.py` + H2 `MODE=MariaDB` parity** |
