| 114 | **`mutation-backend.yml`'s `paths:` filter is narrower than the PIT scope it gates** — measured 2026-07-27: [`backend/core/pitest.scope.json`](../backend/core/pitest.scope.json) mutates **five** policy classes (`RotationPolicy`, `RemovalPolicy`, `TokenBucket`, **`ShareAccessPolicy`**, **`MembershipPolicy`**), but [`mutation-backend.yml`](../.github/workflows/mutation-backend.yml) lists only **three** `.kt` source/test pairs in each of its two `paths:` blocks (`:24-29`, `:42-47`) — `ShareAccessPolicy.kt`/`MembershipPolicy.kt` and their tests are absent. So a PR that edits **only** one of those two never triggers the backend mutation gate: the assertion-strength check silently does not run on exactly the change it exists for. The workflow's own comment claims coverage — *"A new policy enters via a `pitest.scope.json` edit, which is listed here, so adding one always triggers the gate"* — which holds for the **adding** commit and is false for every later edit; that stale-by-construction comment is the real defect, and it is the same class as [ADR 0045](decisions/0045-mutation-testing-trust-chain-unit-layer.md)'s reason for `mutation_meta_check_backend.py` (a per-glob check exists precisely because a *whole-scope* signal misses a single member). Two shapes to weigh at refine: (a) keep the manual list and add the two missing pairs — cheapest, but the drift recurs on policy #6; (b) widen the filter to the directories the scope globs name and let `failWhenNoMutations` + the per-glob meta-check carry precision — no manual list to drift, at the cost of some no-op runs. **Not a guard-glob candidate**: a `validate_*.py` cross-checking `pitest.scope.json` against a workflow `paths:` list would be buildless and cheap, but per [`ai/README.md`](ai/README.md) rule 3 it needs a *noticed* defect first — this one was found by reading, not by being bitten, so measure the cost before building it. Found while mapping the verification layer into [`ai/README.md`](ai/README.md) § the verification layer (commit `54c1e28e`).  **DONE 2026-07-30 (cr-mutation-gate-completeness).** Neither (a) nor (b) alone: the two missing pairs were added **and** the mechanism replaced, because the drift is measurable rather than theoretical — `a8cc4ce4` (2026-07-19) created the workflow correct at three classes; `b15807a7` (2026-07-20) added `ShareAccessPolicy` + `MembershipPolicy` to the manifest and **did not touch the workflow** (`git show --stat b15807a7 | grep -c mutation-backend.yml` → 0); `1fed462c` (#115) then maintained both blocks. The manual list broke on the first policy added after it existed, **one day later**. Option (b) measured and rejected: the four scope directories hold **33 main + 37 test** files against the **10** in scope — ~70 files' worth of no-op mutation runs. Shipped: **`scripts/validate_mutation_trigger_paths.py`** derives each path from `pitest.scope.json` (trailing `*` stripped, package→dir, class→`.kt`) and asserts it exists **and** is listed in **both** `paths:` blocks *separately* — hand-duplicated lists drift apart, and a backstop that quietly stopped covering a class is worse than none. **Its first run was its own bite test**: red on the live defect, naming all 8 missing entries, before a line of the fix was written; two further injections confirm it (dropping `TokenBucket.kt` from the **push** block only reds naming that path and that block while leaving `pull_request` unflagged — the union-vs-separate discrimination; a renamed manifest glob reds on both the missing file and the missing trigger). The *"not a guard-glob candidate"* call is superseded: [`ai/README`](ai/README.md) § when to add a tool is *pain before tool*, and `b15807a7` is the pain. The stale workflow comment claiming completeness is replaced by a pointer to the guard. **Limit written down, not closed:** a path filter cannot follow imports (`MembershipPolicy`→`MembershipEntity`, `RemovalPolicy`→`ScopeEntity`+`MembershipEntity`), so editing a transitive dependency still moves a score untriggered — pinned as open gap **10** in `validate_guard_gaps.py`. | tooling, ci, mutation, guard, NEW | measured 2026-07-27 (harness doc pass) | **DONE 2026-07-30 (cr-mutation-gate-completeness) → `validate_mutation_trigger_paths.py` + both `paths:` blocks completed** |
| 117 | **Two Argon2id-heavy unit suites time out under full-suite CPU load — a real flake, and CI is the slower machine** — surfaced 2026-08-03 while closing cr-wire-assertion-db-sweep, in files that CR never touched. `web/` sets no `testTimeout`, so vitest's **5000 ms default** applies (`grep -rn testTimeout web/vite.config.ts web/package.json` → no match), and the Model B derivation tests sit just under it: **measured**, two consecutive `npm run check` runs failed *different* tests — run 1 `3 failed` (`login.test.ts` "signs the nonce" **5088 ms**; `setupServer.test.ts` "derives a deterministic salt + keypair" **5148 ms** + "rejects when the server rejects the registration" **5349 ms**), run 2 `1 failed` (`setupServer` "derives a deterministic salt + keypair" **5135 ms**) — while `npx vitest run src/features/account/login/login.test.ts src/features/admin/setupServer/setupServer.test.ts` in isolation is **11 passed**. So the failure is contention, not logic: Argon2id is deliberately expensive (ADR 0003), the full suite runs it concurrently with the build, and a few hundred ms of scheduler pressure crosses the line. **Why it matters beyond a local annoyance:** a GH runner is slower than this Mac, so the same margin is thinner in CI, and the failing test is *non-deterministic across runs* — the shape that gets re-run until green and then stops being read. **Not** in the e2e path, which is immune by construction: `scripts/run_web_e2e.py` passes `--testTimeout` from `TEST_MS` (default **120000**, `:86`/`:428`). Shapes to weigh at refine: (a) a per-suite `testTimeout` on the two derivation files — smallest, but a magic number that rots as params change; (b) a global `test.testTimeout` in `vite.config.ts` — one knob, hides genuinely-hung tests longer; (c) isolate the crypto-derivation tests into their own non-concurrent project — most correct, most machinery. Cost so far is **two re-runs in one session**; per [`ai/README.md`](ai/README.md) rule 3 that is the pain to weigh, and it is currently small — but it is a *flake*, not a defect, so the cost is paid in trust rather than in time. | web, tests, flake, ci, NEW | measured 2026-08-03 (cr-wire-assertion-db-sweep close) | **DONE 2026-08-05** (`f3f6f3eb`). **None of the three shapes above was chosen** — the framing was wrong. All three ask *which tests get more time*; the fix asks **who owns the derivation**. Where the test builds the fixture *and* computes the expected value, it picks cheap Argon2id params on both sides (`TEST_ARGON`, t=1/m=64KiB) and still runs real Argon2id over the real code path — the cost params are a public argument of every v1 derivation and travel in each wrap's `kdfParams`, so this is crypto agility, not a test affordance; those suites drop from 1.1–3.7 s to 0–45 ms. Where **production** mints the wrap, the test cannot choose the params without production growing an argument for a test's benefit — so those keep the real cost and state the real ceiling (`PRODUCTION_ARGON_TIMEOUT_MS`). Shape (b) was rejected outright: the other ~770 tests run under 5 ms each, and their 5000 ms default is a live canary for a hung promise or a missing mock — a global bump buys a handful of tests headroom by spending every other test's fastest failure signal. |
| 118 | **A one-time link must not pass through hands it was not minted for** — retire the member-invite copy-link, make a configured mailer a precondition | security, email, invite, account, NEW | **DONE 2026-08-06** as [ADR 0055](decisions/0055-mail-is-a-precondition-not-a-capability.md), built across `cr-email-refuse-without-mailer` (wire + backend + the member dialogs) and `cr-email-member-invite-mail-only` (the e2e harness on Mailpit, `/recover`'s refusal, and the wiki) — both CRs deleted. The rule, from [ADR 0054](decisions/0054-password-recovery-two-factor-release.md) § Decision 4: sort one-time links by *whose thing they open*. A **share** link is made to be passed on. An **invite** token creates an account that does not exist yet. A **recovery** token opens an existing account with its content — and today's member-invite token, though it creates, is still minted **for a named other person** and handed to whoever is looking at the owner's screen. The weak argument is "they could not open it anyway" (true, and not the point); the real one is that a secret addressed to someone else should not be routed through a third party at all. **The work:** (a) `InviteMemberDialog` + `TenantMembersPanel` stop surfacing `bootstrapSecret` — mail becomes the only channel for a member invite; (b) inviting is **refused with a clear error when the tenant has no working mailer**, rather than minting invitations that reach nobody; (c) `POST /api/recovery/request` answers **`503 mail_unavailable`** in the same situation, so the sign-in screen's reset link fails loudly instead of returning its usual silent `202`. **NOT a capability flag (revised 2026-08-05).** The first cut of this row proposed one; that was wrong. A capability is an operator off-switch for a feature that is *optional per instance* ([uc.platform.capability-discovery](features/platform/use-cases/uc.platform.capability-discovery.md) §4), so advertising "can send mail" would **adopt a mailer-less instance as a supported configuration** — the opposite of the decision. Mail is a base function: every instance is expected to have it, and only tenant creation happens before one can exist. The precedent is the blob store, which is likewise not optional and likewise has **no client-facing flag** — `BlobStoreStartupCheck` simply refuses to start (*"media is not gracefully degraded"*, `VeyqConfig`). SMTP is per-tenant so a startup check cannot reach it, hence the per-action refusal above. **And it needs no oracle:** non-enumerability protects *"does this account exist"*; *"can this instance send mail"* is a property of the instance, so answering it plainly leaks nothing about any address. The client therefore asks nothing in advance and the reset link may always be shown. **The single surviving copy-link: the ServerOwner's invite to a tenant's first owner** (`CreateTenantDialog`, `TenantDetailPanel`) — a `PENDING` tenant has no mail settings by construction, so no channel can exist yet; chosen over a server-wide SMTP config, which stays a possible later simplification (it would remove the last copy-link entirely). Relates to #13 (email theme), #112 (email verification, out of V1 — it returns with #43), #113 (done — this closes its one open item, now a status code rather than a flag through three layers). |
| 119 | **Change your password while signed in — the case a live session cannot serve today** | feature, account, crypto, done | **refined 2026-08-25 → [uc.account.change-password](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.change-password.md) + `cr-authenticated-password-change`, DONE 2026-08-25** (raised 2026-08-05 while building recovery; both `[DECISION]` calls answered). **(a) old password:** two entry points over one mechanism, as the row proposed — an optional `attestationSignature` (new auth public key signed by the OLD auth private key) rides the request when the actor knows the current password; absent on the forgotten-but-signed-in path, where the live session + a freshly-typed Secret Key is the re-auth, at the same client-enforced trust level ADR 0060 Decision 2 already accepts for rotation. **(b) DEVICE_KEY wraps:** yes, dropped on both paths (mirrors recovery), but **not** blindly — the acting device's own session is spared from the session-revoke (`SessionService.revokeOtherFamilies`, the exact mechanism `RotationHandler` already uses to avoid stranding the caller mid-flow) and may immediately re-enrol its own wrap via the existing `POST /api/account/wraps`, so the person changing their password is not logged out by their own change. **Ruled out during refine:** reusing `POST /api/account/rotation` (ADR 0060) — its request shape mandates a new identity keypair + cascades into scope re-keying, and its own schema comment says *"no `authPublicKey` — the password does not change"*; wrong tool, kept as a cited constraint in the CR rather than rediscovered at implement time. **Built**: `POST /api/account/password` (contracts → backend `PasswordChangeStore`/`Handler`/`Resource` → web `changePassword/` settings section), coverage filled with zero gaps, full trust-chain green, live-browser-verified for both entry points including a login round-trip on the new password. |
| 120 | **Nothing ties a published wiki page to the internal doc it is supposed to speak for** | docs, wiki, guard, drift, NEW | **done** (cr-wiki-pages-name-their-source, 2026-10-01) → the docs no longer call the wiki generated (`docs/features/README.md`, `docs/README.md`, `docs/ai/README.md`, `validate_feature_format.py` E); line 1 of every `wiki/{en,de}` page declares `speaks-for` + `pair`, each declared `docs/` source links its English page back, and `scripts/validate_wiki_sources.py` holds both directions (`validate_guard_gaps.py` 31/32 rejected; 33 pins the limit: a page contradicting its doc stays green). `wiki/` joined `validate_doc_links.py`. Reading the pages against their docs for the headers found 12 claim disagreements on all six pages, handed to the wave coordinator to file as their own rows. Refine measured (2026-09-28, `faf9ef4`): 6 EN + 5 DE pages. EN/DE file names are translated, so the pair must be declared. The operator page is deliberately EN-only. No page names a source. Also found: `validate_doc_links.py` does not read `wiki/` at all (36 relative links, all resolving today). Found 2026-08-05, the hard way. `wiki/{en,de}/account-keys-and-recovery.md` told users *"your password is sent to the server to log in"* — the exact opposite of Model B, where the client derives an Ed25519 keypair and hands over only the public half ([ADR 0003](decisions/0003-operator-blind-password-auth.md)). `en.account.key-model.md:61` had always said it correctly (*"Human secret, never sent to the server"*), so the **published** half had drifted from the internal half, which is the worse direction: the wrong claim was the one strangers read. It survived because a **user asked**, not because anything checked. The same sweep found three more leftovers of a "recovery code" that does not exist — same class, same reason. **The cited sentence itself is fixed** (re-grounded 2026-08-25: both `wiki/{en,de}/account-keys-and-recovery.md` now correctly say the password never leaves the device) — someone corrected the prose without this row learning about it. The guard this row actually asks for — tying a wiki page to the doc it speaks for — is still unbuilt, so the row stays open on that, not on the example that first found it. **Why no guard sees it (measured):** `wiki-sync.yml` **rsyncs** `wiki/**` to the GitHub Wiki; nothing generates it. `validate_feature_format.py` asserts a use-case carries an EN **and** a DE block and calls that *"the single source the wiki is derived from"* — but it never checks that any wiki page came from one, and no wiki page names its source. `docs/README.md`'s *"Wiki source: yes"* column is a convention with no mechanism behind it. The two drifted pages have **no source use-case at all**: they predate `uc.account.recover-access`, and their subject matter actually belongs to `en.account.key-model` + `crosscutting/security-model.md`. **The buildable half:** every `wiki/**/*.md` names the doc it speaks for, and a guard checks that (a) the target resolves, (b) the EN/DE pair both exist and both point at the same one. **The principled limit, stated up front so nobody mistakes green for agreement:** no guard can decide whether two pieces of prose *claim the same thing* — this one would not have caught the sentence above. What it buys is an **anchor**: a use-case or enabler change lands with a visible list of published pages that speak for it, so the drift has an address instead of waiting for a stranger to notice. Relates to #87 (the doc-vs-code drift review), #108/ADR 0046 (claims marked measured vs. remembered). |
| 123 | ~~**Nothing in the web client serializes refresh — and a dropped refresh response revokes the family**~~ | bug, security, account, web, session, DONE | **REFINED 2026-09-21 → [ADR 0004](decisions/0004-session-tokens.md) § *the one-generation grace*.** Originally **inbox (found 2026-08-13 while refining the delegated-session transport correction; facts measured on `main` @ `28d4591`).** Two concurrent presentations of the *same* refresh token are ADR 0004's clone case, and the client produces them by ordinary means. Measured, server side: `SessionService.refresh` (`backend/.../login/SessionService.kt:93-108`) takes a `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` on the token row, so the two are serialized — and the loser then reads `status == SUPERSEDED` and sets `family.status = REVOKED`. Measured, client side: `web/src` contains **zero** occurrences of `navigator.locks`, `BroadcastChannel`, `SharedWorker`, a mutex, or an in-flight-promise cache; `refreshSession` (`web/src/features/account/login/login.ts:44-58`) is a plain async function with no module state; `withRefresh` (`:84-96`) is a bare try/catch used at **97 invocation sites across 25 files** (re-measured 2026-09-21 — the original *"~25 call sites"* was the file count read as the call count); and `boot()` (`web/src/app/boot.ts:43`) fires a refresh **unconditionally in every tab** on every load. **Five** visibility-driven poll loops re-fire together on `visibilitychange` (`grantDeliveryPolling`, `scopeRotationPolling`, `mediaRevisionPolling`, `pairingAnswerPolling` — and `grantEnvelopePolling`, which the original four missed and which contributes nothing, running by its own header comment *"with neither a session nor a vault"*), so a tab regaining focus after the 15-minute access TTL is the routine trigger; two tabs restored together by the browser is the simplest one. **What is and is not proven:** the mechanism is measured in code, the failure has **not** been reproduced at runtime — that reproduction is this row's first job, and it is cheap, because the idiom already exists one file over (`LoginSliceTest.concurrentVerifyConsumesTheChallengeOnce` uses a `CyclicBarrier`; measured, that file has exactly **one**, and reuse detection is otherwise tested only sequentially via `replayedSupersededRefreshRevokesFamily`). **Why it surfaced here and why it is its own row:** `cr-pairing-add-a-device-web` rejected a script-held token because it "would be the first multi-tab-unsafe session in this client". The conclusion was right for a different reason (ADR 0004 § Context: a body token is one XSS away; and a cookie is self-updating across tabs where a script-held copy goes stale) — but the premise was false, and its falseness is this defect. It predates device linking and touches every web session, so it must not be smuggled into the pairing correction. **The likely shapes, none decided:** single-flight the refresh behind one shared promise per tab, coordinate across tabs, or make the server tolerate a narrow re-presentation window of the *immediately* superseded generation — the last would soften reuse detection and needs ADR 0004 to rule on it, not an implementer. **GROUNDED 2026-09-21 — reproduced at runtime on `main` @ `4379b37`, and the reproduction found a SECOND failure mode this row did not have.** The row's own first job is therefore discharged: the mechanism it measured in code is confirmed in the running system, against the dev backend on **MariaDB 11.4** (a harness that synthesises its own user + family + refresh token directly in the DB — `token_hash` is the hex SHA-256 of the token, `Tokens.hash` — then fires N concurrent `POST /api/token/refresh` carrying the *same* token; it must pace itself under the IP-keyed **60/min** auth bound, which is the only reason this is slow to measure). **At 6 concurrent, every single trial revokes its own family:** across two runs (25 and 40 trials, 390 requests) exactly one **200** per trial and **310 × 401** — 65/65. **The new fact: the loser does not only get a 401. With measurable frequency it gets a 500** — `jakarta.persistence.PessimisticLockException`, MariaDB **1213 / SQLState 40001**, on the `select … from veyq_refresh_token … for update` itself. **The cycle is NOT a lock-ordering cycle, and that rules out one of the two obvious fixes.** From InnoDB's own dump (`innodb_print_all_deadlocks` switched ON for the measurement and back OFF after — re-runnable: `SET GLOBAL innodb_print_all_deadlocks=ON` as root with the dev credentials `infra/docker-compose.yml` already carries, then `docker logs veyq-mariadb \| grep -c 'insert intention waiting'` against `grep -c 'Transactions deadlock detected'` — **12 against 12**), **12 of 12** deadlocks are one shape: an **insert-intention gap lock in `ux_refresh_token_hash`**. Transaction A holds `lock_mode X` on the presented token's index record (its `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) and then inserts the successor, whose **random** hash sorts into the gap *immediately before* that same record — `lock_mode X locks gap before rec insert intention waiting` — while transaction B's `X waiting` request on that record is already queued ahead of it. The victim is always the `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` (0 undo entries, so the lightest transaction), which is why the 500 always lands on the *second* caller. There is no ordering to canonicalise here: the conflict is a gap/FIFO artefact inside **one** unique index over **random** material. So of the shapes named above, the server-side one that actually exists is either a retry on 40001/1213 or `READ COMMITTED` (InnoDB takes no gap locks there) — the latter an isolation decision with a radius far beyond this row. **The frequency is a function of table size, and that is the whole reason this reads as a dev-only ghost.** The collision needs the successor's random hash to land in the one gap before the contended record, i.e. P ≈ 1/N. Measured, 6 concurrent, 150 requests per size: **~50 rows → 0**, **~250 → 0**, **~1 050 → 0**, **~50 100 → 0**; and **9/150 (6.0 %)** plus **15/240 (6.2 %)** — two independent runs — with `veyq_refresh_token` essentially **empty**. **Read those zeros correctly:** 0/150 bounds the rate below ~2 % (95 %), it does not prove zero, and nothing here says a populated instance is immune. **Two controls, both 0 in 8 requests:** separate families refreshing concurrently, and a rotation racing a replay of its own SUPERSEDED predecessor. The trigger is exactly *the same token at the same moment* — which is this row and nothing else. **Why the 500 is worse than the 401 it hides among, measured in the client:** `refreshSession` (`web/src/features/account/login/login.ts:54-57`) catches *everything* and calls `clearTokens()`; there is no 5xx branch anywhere in the auth layer (`grep -rn '500\|serverError\|internalServer'` over `web/src/shared/httpError.ts` + `login.ts` → no output; `HttpStatus` names one 5xx, `serviceUnavailable: 503` at `httpError.ts:34`, and it is about a tenant that cannot send mail — nothing reads it on this path). So a 500 kills the in-memory session exactly as a 401 does — but `boot()` (`web/src/app/boot.ts:43-48`) cannot tell a 500 from *"no session"*, and its `else` branch additionally drops the remember-me tiers (`clearSessionVault()`, `clearDeviceKey(false)`). **A transient 500 at load therefore de-enrols the device's password-less unlock**, which the 401 path does not do, orphaning the server wrap rather than deleting it (`deleteServerWrap = false`). `admin-web/src/features/auth/login.ts:41,66` is the same copy with the same gap. **The trap for whoever picks this up:** the `@QuarkusTest` profile is **H2** (`application-test.properties`), and gap locks are an InnoDB construct — a slice test can never go red on this half, however concurrent it is. `LoginSliceTest`'s `CyclicBarrier` idiom still proves the *revocation* half (that reproduces on any engine); the 500 half is only provable against MariaDB. **And the retry is its own small debt independent of whichever client fix wins:** 40001 is the *retryable* class, a rolled-back transaction committed nothing (the token is still `CURRENT`, so a replay is consistent), and the backend currently surfaces it as a **500 from a public endpoint**. But it is not *the* fix and must not be sold as one: the deadlock is a strict subset of the losers, so a retry only converts this 500 into the 401 this row was already about. **THE REFINE, 2026-09-21 — and it re-derived the row's question rather than answering it. Substrate: `main` @ `c0add4e`, clean tree.** The row asks about *two tabs*; measured, that is a special case of something with no concurrency in it at all. A refresh whose **response never reaches the client** has already committed server-side — rows go to `[gen 1 SUPERSEDED, gen 2 CURRENT]` while the browser's cookie still holds gen 1 — so the next page load presents gen 1 and **the family is revoked**. One dropped response permanently ends a session, no attacker and no second tab required. That reframes the fix: **no client-side change can close it**, because the rotation is committed before the client learns anything, so single-flight and cross-tab locks reduce frequency and heal nothing. **The second find is a doc↔code drift sitting on exactly the spot the fix needs.** ADR 0004 claimed the server kept *"current + previous generation hash"* and that anything older was *"merely invalid, not a family-killer"*. Measured: `grep -rn "generation" backend/core/src/main/kotlin backend/apps/memories/src/main/kotlin` returns **three writes and no comparison** (`SessionService.kt:65`, `:114`, `:259`), `refresh` revokes on **any** surviving `SUPERSEDED` row (`:105`), and those survive the full 30-day TTL. The ADR described a retention rule that was never built. **DECIDED (owner, 2026-09-21): the one-generation grace, bounded by generation DISTANCE, not by time.** A presented superseded token exactly one generation behind its family's `CURRENT` rotates normally and supersedes the orphaned successor; any greater distance still revokes. A time window was considered first and **rejected on the measurement**: the benign late case is a person reloading minutes or hours after the dropped response, and any window short enough to keep detection meaningful is far too short for that. Detection is not removed, only deferred by at most one access lifetime — whoever holds the orphaned successor is caught on their next refresh, then at distance ≥ 2. **Bitten before it was written down, four ways, live against MariaDB 11.4:** the lost-response case lands at distance **1**; six concurrent refreshes give `1 × 200 / 5 × 401` with all five losers at distance **1**; a genuine replay after three rotations sits at distance **3** and still revokes; and a chain numbers `1,2,3,4` with exactly one `CURRENT`. So the distance is the exact discriminator between the benign set and the clone set — which is the claim the whole decision rests on. **What those four bites did NOT cover, and it cost a correction:** they measured the distances in the *untouched* tree, i.e. the rule's input, and never the rule's own dynamics once it fires. See below. **The first form of the rule was WRONG and was corrected before a line was built — by tracing it against the concurrency this row measured, not by a test.** As decided it said the grace *"rotates normally, superseding the orphaned successor"*, i.e. incremented. All six concurrent presentations reach the row, so with an increment the second mints generation 3 and **the third is at distance 2 and revokes** — the rule collapses at three concurrent callers, which is the case it exists for. The same increment loses the security half: two parties refreshing alternately sit at distance 1 forever and are never caught. **The fix is that the grace re-mints under the orphaned successor's own number**, so every further presentation of one stale token stays at distance 1 — and whoever holds the orphan lands at **distance 0**, which becomes the detector. Consequence: generations are **not** unique within a family, and the rule does not need them to be; it reads the one `CURRENT` row. The only invariant left standing from the bite is that one `CURRENT` row per family. **No migration:** the distance reads `family_id + status`, an index that already exists, and it makes `generation` a field that is finally *read*. **What is left for the CR, all of it now free of design questions:** (1) the grace rule in `SessionService.refresh` + the `mint` counting change, which turns `LoginSliceTest.replayedSupersededRefreshRevokesFamily` (`:156`) into a case that must present at distance ≥ 2, and wants the first **concurrent refresh** test — the `CyclicBarrier` idiom exists once in the tree (`LoginSliceTest.kt:93`) and is aimed at the login challenge, not at refresh; (2) a shared in-flight refresh per tab, for which the idiom **and its test shape already exist one directory over** (`web/src/features/memory/placeName.ts:51`, `placeName.test.ts:35` *"shares one in-flight request between callers that arrive together"*); (3) the `SQLSTATE 40001` retry. **Two constraints the CR must carry:** `onboardTail.ts:45-52` forbids `withRefresh`, a generic retry and any interceptor on the `adopt` line by boxed comment, so a single-flight must not reach it; and `admin-web`'s own `withRefresh` has **zero callers** (its only refresh is boot), so it is dead code to remove rather than a second site to fix. **Cross-tab coordination (`navigator.locks`) is NOT in that list** — with the grace rule a raced refresh costs a wasted rotation, not a session, and the measurement that would justify the machinery no longer exists. Re-measured and still zero: `navigator.locks`, `BroadcastChannel`, `SharedWorker` all **0** occurrences in both packages. **DONE 2026-09-21** (`cr-refresh-one-generation-grace`, deleted) — four phases: the contract prose, the rule in `SessionService.rotate`, the shared in-flight refresh in `web`, and coverage. **Three things the implementation corrected in the work order itself, recorded because each was a claim nobody had run.** (1) *"`mint` takes the generation to write rather than deriving it"* — it already did; the whole change was at the call site. (2) *"`admin-web`'s `withRefresh` is dead code to remove"* was half right: it had no callers, but the file's own header asserted it was *"the one place that refreshes, around an authed call"*, so the deletion had to take that sentence with it — the header now says the only refresh is at boot, and says to copy `web`'s `refreshSession` **with** its in-flight promise if that ever changes. (3) `refresh` stopped being `@Transactional`: the retry needs to open a transaction per attempt, which an interceptor-managed one cannot give it. **The bite that mattered most was the one on the regression test.** Injecting the rejected incrementing form turns exactly three tests red — the dedicated one, the three-way concurrent one, the orphan one — while the lost-response test stays **green**, which is the discrimination the CR predicted and the reason the dedicated test exists at all. Snapshot restored byte-identical. **Two working lessons are harvested to [`changes/README`](../changes/README.md), not left here:** bite a new rule against its own dynamics and not only against the state it will read (the four bites that approved the wrong rule measured distances the rule had not yet moved); and a harness standing in for a check reports what it **ran**, not only what failed — a local copy of the trust-chain loop without the job's own `count == 0` backstop printed `failures=0` twice for a glob that matched nothing. |
| 124 | **The web's whole cookie transport is absent from the contracts that are supposed to be the source of truth** | docs, contracts, guard, drift, account, DONE | **DONE 2026-08-25 (doc fix only, direct commit — no guard, for the same rule-3 reason #114/#135 were declined).** Found 2026-08-13 while refining `cr-delegated-session-cookie-transport`; re-grounded fresh against `main` @ `a793429` before closing, all four original facts still held: `grep -rn "Set-Cookie" contracts/` hit only `/api/token/adopt`, while `POST /api/login/verify`, `POST /api/token/refresh` and `POST /api/logout` each set/clear the cookie in their Kotlin adapter (`SessionCookie.issue`/`cleared`) without a `headers:` block declaring it. **Shipped:** all four operations in `auth.openapi.yaml` now declare `Set-Cookie` under their success response, each description stating the real conditional shape — cookie-vs-body transport on `verifyLogin`/`refreshSession`, unconditional clearing on `logout` — not just copy-pasted from `/api/token/adopt`. All 28 `scripts/validate_*.py` guards green against the changed tree; per `backend/CLAUDE.md`, a `headers:`-only spec edit (like a `description:`-only one) produces no generated-code delta, so there is no backend phase. **Guard verdict, checked before touching the file, not after:** no. Two reasons, weighed against the one argument that looked strongest — this sits nearer a root `CLAUDE.md` hard invariant (API-first) than #135's test-debt gap did, which would normally tip toward building — but #124's own principled limit undercuts exactly that: OpenAPI cannot express cookie *auth* for a browser client either way (the browser attaches it, no generated code touches it), so the hard invariant about hand-written DTOs was never actually at risk — this was always a documentation-completeness gap, not a codegen-correctness one. With that argument neutralized: **(a)** the trigger table in `ai/README.md` § *when to add a tool* doesn't fire — the invariant-guard trigger is dedup ("a CR removes a duplication or establishes 'X only in Y'"), and this CR fills a missing declaration, consolidates nothing. **(b)** direct precedent, same day: #114 and #135 both declined a structurally identical guard for a defect found by reading during construction, not by an incident, and #114 built its guard only after a real drift incident landed months later. #124 was found the same way — noticed while building `/api/token/adopt`, which is also why that operation already carries the convention correctly. The predicate itself (Kotlin call sites of `.issue`/`.cleared` cross-checked against `headers:` blocks, bounded to the three known login/session resources) stays sketched here for whoever revisits this after a real incident: cheaper than #135's rejected full-delete-enumeration variant, but still a cross-file read, not a one-line grep count — so it waits for the same trigger #114's did. Relates to #120 (published prose drifting from internal docs — same class, one layer out) and to ADR 0004 § Transport, which is where the cookie's semantics are actually written down; neither needed re-deriving here. |
| 131 | **Deleting a Memory/Chapter reclaims its cover blob and leaves every other blob it held** | bug, memory, media, storage, gc | **built 2026-08-18.** The delete is now a **soft-delete of the whole subtree** into a per-space trash, and `NodeSubtreePurge` hard-erases the expired subtree — media, derivative, bucket and blob rows and the store objects, then the node rows. The leak closed because the purge finally owns the whole subtree instead of just its covers; the cover reclaim moved *out* of the delete, since under a soft-delete it would hand a restore back a Memory whose cover is gone. Both are now handled explicitly by the node purge. **Three neighbours were left open and none of them is this:** #130 (store objects with no row at all), #132 (`eraseScope`'s FK to `veyq_job`/`veyq_share`), #133 (the crosscutting doc's per-scope trash wording vs. the built per-Memory *media* trash — this CR settled the grain for **nodes** only). All three are closed since: #130 done 2026-08-18, #132 retired 2026-08-18 (`c8e8135d`, its row removed), #133 done 2026-08-25. |
| 130 | **Every store-object delete is best-effort and silent, so the bytes that survive a failed one are unmeasurable** | bug, media, storage, gc, ops, self-hosting | **done 2026-08-18 → the residual is #134 below, split out rather than buried here.** [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-store-object-reclaim-is-a-durable-work-list.md) owns the decision and the four rejected arms. Built: `veyq_store_reclaim` takes the keys in the **same transaction** as the row delete, so the knowledge of which bytes to delete can no longer die with the row that held it; `BlobStore.delete` returns what it could not delete instead of swallowing it; and `StoreReclaimSweep` is the only caller of it — pinned by `scripts/validate_store_reclaim_arity.py` and by case 13 of `validate_guard_gaps.py`, because calling the store directly is the one-line shortcut every future reclaim path will reach for. What a permanently unreachable store still loses is now `SELECT count(*) FROM veyq_store_reclaim WHERE abandoned_at IS NOT NULL`, which is exactly what this row's title says nothing can answer — so the title is what this row closes. |
| 134 | **Nothing compares the bucket against the database — objects no blob row claims and no reclaim row explains** | media, storage, gc, ops, self-hosting | **done 2026-10-01 (`cr-reclaim-waits-out-the-presign-ttl`) — the veyq-internal producer is closed; the reconcile itself stays parked.** Refining found the premise *"an object that exists is named at every instant"* held at presign time but not at PUT time: a signed URL outlives the row naming its key by up to the presign TTL, and the reclaim row was due on the next tick, so a member could purge a just-completed blob (or replace/clear a cover) and re-PUT its chunks after the sweep — bytes no row and no quota names, 16 MiB per cycle. `StoreReclaimStore.record` now stamps `next_attempt_at` at `veyq.media.upload.retention` after the recording, and `StoreReclaimSweepSliceTest.aRecordedKeyIsNotDeletedWhileAPutSignedForItCanStillLand` pins the floor at the TTL. **Why the reconcile stays parked (owner, 2026-09-28):** after that fix its only producer is a writer outside veyq (a shared bucket, a hand-copied object, a botched restore), so against a stock of zero by construction its only honest Definition of done is still *"finds nothing"*; a future row-drop path that forgets `.record` stays unguarded, deliberately (rule 3, as #114). **If it is ever built**, start from [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-store-object-reclaim-is-a-durable-work-list.md) § Consequences, the reconcile bullet: as an operator tool on `scripts/instance_backup.py`'s rclone plumbing it needs no new `BlobStore` port (`rclone lsf` plus one database snapshot); only a backend reconcile pays for a port and an unpartitionable listing. |
| 135 | **The object-store connection is boot-time environment, and the store's own bootstrap needs a home.** | infra, ops, self-hosting, blob-store, DONE | **DONE 2026-09-10** — both CRs of [ADR 0067](decisions/0067-store-provisioning-from-a-declared-type.md) built and deleted (`74793531` wrote them): `cr-store-provisioning` (`0b4f19dd` the bundled store provisions itself from a declared type, `f9d0e02b` `garage-init` deleted from every stack, `fbf25642` the Server-Owner panel; deleted `9b687e64`) and `cr-store-own-address-signing-mode` (`3c0202be` `veyq.blob-store.signing-mode`; deleted `1f7325d1`). Was: **REFINED 2026-09-09 → [ADR 0067](decisions/0067-store-provisioning-from-a-declared-type.md).** Raised by the owner as *"where you point veyq at an S3 you should also say which KIND it is, and if it is not fully set up, veyq sets it up"*. **Refining it shrank it, which is the result worth recording.** The idea arrived attached to per-tenant stores; measured against V1's own scope — self-hosted, one operator — **one store serves every tenant**, and with that the DB row, the KEK-sealed credential, the Admin API surface, the panel, the CLI verb and the relaxed startup check all fall away. What survives is the half that was actually wanted: veyq provisions its own store, from a declared type, and `infra/garage-init.sh` + its unpublished image are deleted — which is also what unblocks #26 slice 4. **Three measurements did the work.** Garage v2.0.0's Admin API does every bootstrap step over plain JSON (a bucket created and deleted through it), so the custom image only ever owned a shell. A bootstrap does not generalise: `bucket create` and `PutBucketCors` are S3 for anybody, `layout assign`/`apply` are Garage cluster mechanics, and `key import`/`bucket allow` are IAM, which no two stores spell alike — that is the argument for a declared type, against the `provider-neutral` precedent [ADR 0048](decisions/0048-provider-neutral-smtp-mail-transport.md) set for mail (SMTP is one protocol; provisioning is no protocol). And `veyq-admin` signs in as Server Owner per invocation, so it cannot run before setup — ruling out the CLI as the bootstrap's home on sequencing, not on taste. **What the ADR deliberately leaves open, with the shape preserved so it stays cheap:** the type sits on the *connection* rather than on the environment; the "store has its own address" signing mode is named behaviour with two callers (Kubernetes, and a SaaS tenant on their own bucket) rather than a deployment special case; and the per-tenant trap is recorded at the line it bites — `veyq_store_reclaim` carries no tenant **by design** (the tombstone outlives the metadata, `MANDATORY` in the deleting transaction), so N stores would leak bytes the sweep cannot route. The SaaS half went to #41. **CUT INTO TWO CRs 2026-09-10 — `changes/cr-store-provisioning-from-a-declared-type.md` (Decisions 1–4) and `changes/cr-store-own-address-signing-mode.md` (Decision 5, which has ADR 0064's chart as a second caller and touches a different file — genuinely independent work, not a phase).** Grounding the CRs **corrected the ADR in place on four points it had measured wrong**, which is the part worth carrying: `garage-init.sh` writes **no** CORS policy today (`PutBucketCors` is in no code and no `infra/` file), so that step is new work rather than moved work; `garage-init` is deleted from **every** stack rather than the release recipe only — a dev-only sidecar was weighed and rejected for what it hides, since with it present the store is always already provisioned when the backend starts and the **fresh** path would never run outside a unit test; the script's `layout apply --version 1` shortcut is a property of the script, so the provisioner owes idempotence **per step** and against an already-applied layout; and the per-tenant remedy is not the free column it read as — [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-store-object-reclaim-is-a-durable-work-list.md) §6 forbids exactly that shape on exactly that row. **A fifth finding is a consequence rather than a correction, and it only bit once the deletion went everywhere:** now carried as a Consequence there and a phase in the CR: a restore target can no longer be prepared without the app, and the app cannot provision without also migrating (`application.properties:4`, no profile gate), so `scripts/instance_backup.py:660`'s demand for **zero tables** becomes unreachable. *Empty* is redefined as **no data**. That is not a cost 0067 introduces but one it stops hiding — Kubernetes has never had a sidecar, so a cluster restore always faced the same wall, which is why a compose-shaped remedy was rejected in favour of one gate change that serves both targets. The provisioning half still makes **no coverage claim** (no use-case owns "the store sets itself up") and names its proof instead; the CR `satisfies:` only `uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance`, for the A5 half it actually changes. The signing CR satisfies `uc.admin.tenant-reachability`, whose **A5 already states the branch** the code can only half serve. |
| 133 | **The security model promises a per-scope trash; what exists is a per-Memory one, and the gap is a decision nobody made** | docs, crosscutting, media, meta, DONE | **DONE 2026-08-25 (doc fix only, direct commit).** Found 2026-08-17 while refining #131; re-grounded fresh against `main` @ `1e25022` before closing — `security-model.md:339`'s "per-scope trash" wording was untouched since the finding, and `uc.memory.delete-media.md`'s per-Memory trash tab + its explicit "Start light … no cross-scope trash" deviation both still held verbatim. **What #131's build (landed 2026-08-18, after this row was filed) changed about the answer:** the original framing was a binary — move the policy down to per-Memory, or grow the implementation up to per-scope — under the concern that correcting the policy down would *"erase the requirement that #131 needs"*, since #131 was still open and a per-scope node trash was only a candidate design. #131 shipped since, and ADR 0061 Decision 3 built the **node** trash per-scope on its own self-contained reasoning (a trashed Memory has no Memory left to hang a per-Memory trash off of) — it does not lean on `security-model.md`'s wording at all, so that concern no longer applies to either side. **What this closes on instead: the binary was the wrong frame.** There are now two deliberately different, both-built grains — node trash per-scope (`NodesStore.listTrashRoots(scopeId)`, ADR 0061), media trash per-Memory (`MediaStore.listNodeTrashed(scopeId, nodeId)`, `uc.memory.delete-media`'s "Start light") — and neither needed to move. **Shipped:** `security-model.md`'s *Deletion & erasure* section now states both grains by name instead of a single "per-scope" claim, citing ADR 0061 for the node grain and `uc.memory.delete-media` for the media one. No guard: this was prose describing two already-built, already-decided mechanisms — not a repeatable drift shape, and nothing here reduces to a checkable predicate. |

The dead selector was the **only** signal that a shipped feature was missing a surface — the code compiled, every test passed, `uc.memory.cover`'s promise read as kept, and the gap was visible solely as CSS nobody had ever matched. **Deliberately rejected inside that CR rather than built as its tail**, and the reason is the interesting part: the honest predicate needs an exception list — PrimeVue `.p-*` overrides, classes the router applies (`router-link-active`), classes composed inside a `:class` expression instead of written literally — and [`changes/README.md`](../changes/README.md) § *a guard derives its anchor set from the code; it does not list it* is exactly what a hand-maintained exception list violates. **What has to be decided before this is buildable, not discovered while building it:** whether the predicate can be narrowed to a subset that *derives* its exceptions (literal `class="…"` attributes only; only selectors the project itself authors) rather than enumerating them, and what it does about `admin-web/`, which carries its own styles. If no narrowing survives that test, the answer is **no guard** — recorded here as decided, so the next reader weighs the predicate instead of re-deriving that dead CSS was the signal. One occurrence is the whole evidence base so far; a second would be the argument to build it. | tooling, guard, web, css, hygiene, DONE | cr-sidebar-favorites-show-their-cover (2026-09-20) | **DECIDED 2026-09-20 — no guard, and the blocker is the base rate, not the exception list.** Measured on `main` @ `ac7efd6`: `web/src/styles/` defines **648** classes, **461 (71 %)** with no user anywhere in `web/src`; `admin-web/src/styles/` is **152 of 174 (87 %)**. A 12-item hand sample of the narrowed predicate's hits was **12/12 truly dead** — the precision is fine, the corpus is not, and a guard there would be **red on the day it was born**, which [changes/README](../changes/README.md) § *Run a new guard's predicate against the UNTOUCHED tree before you write it down* forbids outright. **The exceptions the row worried about ARE derivable** and that is recorded here so the next reader does not re-construct an exception list: PrimeVue names harvested from `node_modules` (1520, nothing hardcoded), `router-link-active` from vue-router's own defaults, `:class` object keys by also scanning script string literals — which alone accounts for all 12 classes that live only through script. **Why 71 %:** `web/src/style.css` says it itself — the sheets are the design system of the pre-E2EE source (`legacy/frontend-noe2ee/src/styles/`), a directory that is not in this repo, so they carry CSS for views that never existed here; `memory.css` holds 20 `.cm-*` rules for CodeMirror, which is not a dependency of `web/`. **The work therefore moves to #146** and the guard question re-asks itself afterwards as a **ratchet** over a healthy corpus, not as a detector over a landfill. One predicate that does work today and is deliberately **not** this row's answer: a class defined in a `<style scoped>` block and unused in that same file — 7 hits in `web`, 5 truly dead, anchor set fully derived, no vendor list ever needed — but it would have found **neither** actual occurrence of the signal, so it is its own item if anyone wants it. **RE-ASKED 2026-09-21, and the answer flips — measured, not argued.** #146 shipped, and the predicate re-run over the tree it leaves behind returns **0 candidates in `web` and 0 in `admin-web`**. The blocker this row recorded was *the base rate, not the exception list*, and the base rate is gone: a guard written today is **green on arrival**, which is exactly what [changes/README](../changes/README.md) § *Run a new guard's predicate against the UNTOUCHED tree before you write it down* asks for and what this row could not offer in September. **So the entry condition is met and the row is reopened as buildable — but the guard was deliberately NOT built inside `cr-delete-the-inherited-stylesheets`**, because that CR says in its own phase 5 that a guard is not owed by it, and a guard is a shipped artifact with its own bite-test obligations rather than the tail of a deletion. **What the next reader inherits, so none of it is re-derived:** the predicate's four defects and the exact shape that survived them are written up on #146 — nested string literals, selector strings in test files, class-context-only template prefixes, and `;` handling in the CSS parser. **The two things still to decide before building, and they are decisions, not discoveries:** whether the guard is a ratchet (a count that may not rise) or an absolute zero — zero is defensible today and is the stronger promise, but it fails the first time somebody lands CSS a phase ahead of its markup, which is a normal way to work; and whether it runs over `admin-web` with the same threshold, where 33 defined classes against 55 rendered leaves almost no slack. **The scoped-`<style>` variant this row names as its own item is still its own item** and is unaffected. **DONE 2026-09-21 — `scripts/validate_stylesheet_anchors.py` (`6e191f1`..).** It asserts: every **anchor compound** in a package's `src/styles/` + `src/style.css` names at least one class that package's markup applies. The anchor compound is a selector's first compound — in `.memory-card .p-card-body` it is `.memory-card`, in `.memory-danger-action.p-button` it is both together. A rule whose anchor nothing applies cannot match in any state on any surface. **Green on arrival:** 634 anchor compounds across both packages, exit 0. **Red on this row's OWN occurrence:** run against `97afdca~1` — the tree before `cr-sidebar-favorites-show-their-cover` wired the classes — it exits 1 and names all three `.mobile-nav-favorite` rules and `.sidebar-favorite-cover` by `file:line`, among **1592** violations. That 1592-vs-0 pair is this row's own argument settled numerically: *no guard* was right in September because the corpus made it red at birth, and is wrong now because #146 removed the corpus. **The constraint neither this row nor #146 saw, and it decided the whole design:** the trust-chain job is **buildless** — `trust-chain.yml` runs `checkout` + a venv + `pip install pyyaml` and nothing else — so **there is no `node_modules` in CI**, and #146's predicate, which harvests the vendor class names from it, cannot run there at all. It would have been green locally and red in CI: the same *red on arrival* this row refused, displaced one step. Anchoring **dissolves** the vendor question instead of answering it, because a vendor override is always scoped by a project class — measured, **0 vendor-only anchors** out of 564 (web) and 70 (admin-web) against 2083 / 1951 names harvested from `node_modules` purely to check that claim. So the guard carries no vendor list and needs none. **The two decisions this row left open, both settled:** *ratchet vs absolute zero* **dissolved** — the property is per-rule, not a count, so there is no number to freeze and a ratchet would have had to write one down, which [changes/README](../changes/README.md) § *the locator, not the number* forbids; *admin-web's threshold* is **not a threshold** — the package set is derived (any directory with `src/styles/`), so admin-web is in scope automatically and a third package is in scope the day it lands. **Bitten before trusted:** six forged cases (snapshot-and-restore, never `git checkout`) and five corpus-stale cases. Three of them must stay GREEN and pin the design's core claim — a vendor reach-in and a vendor compound are not accused. **One bite found a defect in the guard itself:** emptying `src/styles/` while the `src/style.css` barrel remained came back green, because the barrel kept the file list non-empty and the guard silently validated one file instead of nine — the *going quiet when the subject is restructured away* failure `validate_shell_route_gate.py` warns about. Fixed, with the reason as a comment at the line. **Its limit (a) — it judges the anchor and nothing deeper — is pinned executable** as `validate_guard_gaps.py` case **16**, so a future tightening that closes it has to move the gap-map rather than land silently. |

| 142 | **The Safari Photos workaround protects the curator's picker and not the contributor's** | web, media, upload, sharing, safari, DONE | **DONE 2026-09-20 (`cr-share-picker-inherits-the-safari-rereads`, deleted).** The refine sharpened the finding and the implementation confirmed it was the load-bearing half: `ShareView` does not merely omit the re-read, it clears `input.value` **synchronously in the same tick**, so a retry ladder added without moving that clear re-reads an emptied input and loses the same files while looking correct. E3's parts (a) and (c) already reach both pickers through shared helpers — the gap is exactly (b) — and `uploadQueue.ts` already carries the section header the lift belongs under. **inbox (found 2026-09-20 while implementing `cr-upload-prepare-visibly-and-decode-once`, which named it as a trap and deliberately left it).** `MemoryDetail`'s picker re-reads `input.files` over a retry window and merges by signature, because Safari's Photos gallery populates the input **late** and with **empty MIME types** ([uc.memory.upload](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.upload.md) E3). `ShareView`'s contributor picker enqueues **synchronously, once** — no re-read, no merge — so a contributor uploading from Safari's Photos gallery silently loses whatever had not arrived yet. It was the live control that proved phase 1's immediate-enqueue shape works, which is how it was noticed. The question the row left open — does the re-read belong in a shared helper both call — was answered yes: `collectPickedFiles` in `uploadQueue.ts` owns the whole window (retry ladder, signature merge, deferred input release, teardown) and both pickers call it, knowing nothing about each other's `UploadTarget`. A control test proved the refine's point rather than assuming it: moving the clear back into the synchronous path reddens the late-file test as well as its own, so the ladder alone would not have fixed the contributor. E3's wording needed no change — it already attributed the workaround to *"the picker"*, and the lift is what made that true; both sharing use-cases now cross-reference it and each carries the outcome it can assert in its own right (a picked photo is uploaded, never silently dropped). |
| 143 | **Photo metadata and the detail panel — what a picture is allowed to tell you about itself** | feature, memory, media, metadata, exif, privacy, NEW | **built 2026-09-20 (`cr-media-detail-panel-and-place-name`, deleted).** The panel shows the file (name, plaintext size, format), camera + lens, the full exposure block including compensation, flash and the 35 mm equivalent, the provenance an editor wrote in (software, author, copyright, rating, title, description) and the geo block (altitude, view direction, accuracy) — plus, under the maps opt-in, a **resolved place name** above the coordinate and a mini map with a view-direction needle. Both tags are already read into the lazy tier, so the follow-up starts with data. (b) **MakerNote** — exifr returns it undecoded and per-vendor tag tables are exiftool-scale. (c) **Serial numbers and XMP face regions** — `BodySerialNumber` links a photographer's whole corpus to one camera and `mwg-rs:Regions` carries people's names; not extracted, and the reason is not privacy-by-omission (the original bytes carry them either way) but that *displaying* them normalises them, and putting names on faces is a product decision, not EXIF by-catch. (d) **Making rating and keywords act** (sort, filter, auto-cover) — this shows them; searching sealed data is a crypto question, not a query one. |

The row said three dedicated dialogs; there are **five** (`DeleteNodeDialog`, `DeviceTrustControl`, `MemoryDetail`'s trash purge, `KeyRotationPanel`, `MemoryBuckets`' type-the-name delete) and they share no contract — what is duplicated is ~12 lines of chrome, what differs is precisely the mutation ownership the shared dialog exists to refuse. | 146 | ~~**`web/src/styles/` is 71 % dead — the pre-E2EE design system outlived the implementation it was written for**~~ | web, admin-web, css, cleanup, debt, DONE | **inbox (split out of #140 on 2026-09-20, on that row's own measurement — see it there for the method and the counts).** 461 of 648 classes in `web/src/styles/` have no user anywhere in `web/src`; `admin-web/src/styles/` is 152 of 174. They are the stylesheets of `legacy/frontend-noe2ee/src/styles/`, carried across an import that did not carry the views — that directory is not in this repo at all. **#136 and #137 fold in here**; the V1 line's *"cost minutes"* is true of those two and false of the row they belong to. **The deletion is the work and the risk is visual regression**, so the predicate is a work-list, never an authority: delete in groups, check live, and treat any class that turns out to be alive as a finding about the predicate rather than an exception to wave through. **Hard sequencing constraint, not a preference:** this pass measures the markup, so it runs only when the markup has stopped moving — after the web CRs in flight, never beside them, or it deletes a class that became live an hour ago ([docs/ai/README](ai/README.md) § *a measurement is only as true as the tree it ran against*). **The larger question it opens, to be asked once rather than rediscovered:** CSS was only the layer that happened to be *measurable*. The five dead classes the scoped-CSS probe found are named `beitraege-picker`, `beitraege-chip`, `beitraege-chip-count` — German names in an English codebase, the same sediment. What else came across from the deleted implementation and was never used? **Do not start that search before this pass has run**, or it inherits this row's noise. **DONE 2026-09-21** (`cr-delete-the-inherited-stylesheets`, commits `3549ea4`..`3845648`). **Measured:** both packages' sheets **11209 → 3676 lines, 67 % gone**; re-running the predicate over what is left returns **0 candidates in web and 0 in admin-web**. web/src/styles + style.css: 656 defined classes → 216. admin-web: 307 → 33, against the **55** class names its templates actually render — that gap was the whole finding. #136 and #137 close with it, each with its own decision recorded on its row. **The predicate was wrong four times and every correction is the reusable part, so they are written down here rather than in a deleted CR:** (1) a nested string literal — `:class="{ 'is-open': x }"` and `:pt="{ panel: { class: 'y' } }"` carry the real class one quoting level down, and splitting on whitespace alone left `'is-open':` as a single non-class token; six live classes reached the delete list before this was fixed. (2) a CSS-selector string is a user (`querySelector('.x')`) **but not in a test file** — all three `wrapper.find('.invite-link-input')` calls assert `.exists()).toBe(false)`, i.e. evidence the class is NOT rendered; a test that asserts presence needs markup that the markup scan already sees. (3) template-literal prefixes must be harvested only from a **class context** (`:class`, `classList.*`, `.className`) — harvesting from every backtick in the tree let an i18n key beginning `w` shield all of `workspace-*`. (4) the CSS parser must handle `;`, or `@import` statements swallow the first rule after them — that is the one that hid #136's `.veyq-shell`. **What the gate actually was, since `npm run check` renders no pixel:** a computed-style fingerprint of every element on eight surfaces (home, gallery, open lightbox, chapters, posts, trash, settings, public share) plus sign-in and admin `/login`, in both colour schemes at 1440px and 390px, captured against the **untouched** tree and re-measured after every phase — so each phase re-proved the ones before it. Every phase: 0 elements changed. The measurement was bitten before it was trusted (delete one live rule via the CSSOM → 1 element flagged, `position: relative → static`) and re-bitten after the largest deletion. **Its one false positive is worth knowing:** PrimeVue registers `--p-select-*` / `--p-tabs-*` on `:root` only once such a component has mounted, and custom properties inherit — so probing `/` before visiting a Memory reports *every* element changed with nothing visible. Warm every surface once before measuring; excluding the `--p-` namespace instead would blind the gate, because `base.css` defines some of those names itself. **What this pass did NOT cover:** admin-web's tenant list and tenant detail, because signing in as Server Owner needs a password typed into a field; they rest on a set-intersection check (no class any admin template renders is on the delete list — 55 ∩ 250 = ∅, proven to bite) plus a declaration-level diff against the pre-CR tree. The same gap as #136's parked *"two eyes on the admin side"*, still open. **The follow-on question this row opened stays open and is now unblocked:** CSS was only the layer that happened to be measurable — what else came across from the deleted implementation. |
| 147 | **`shared/ui/` depends on a feature — `SecretKeyField` hard-imports the emergency-kit decoder and its scanner** | web, architecture, layering, debt, NEW | **settled 2026-09-23, not a defect to fix** (with the owner, in `cr-shared-imports-no-features`: the import stays, and [ui-web-styleguide](crosscutting/ui-web-styleguide.md) § *Where a component lives* names it as the one known inversion and says why — undoing it moves the kit import and its jsQR dependency, not a file). Closed 2026-09-28. **inbox (found 2026-09-20 while lifting the locale editor into `shared/ui/`; measured against `main`).** [`shared/ui/SecretKeyField.vue`](../web/src/shared/ui/SecretKeyField.vue) imports `importKitFromFile` from [`features/account/emergencyKit/emergencyKit.ts`](../web/src/features/account/emergencyKit/emergencyKit.ts) (its `parseKit`/`KitData` come from `@veyq/crypto/emergencyKit` since #169, which is not a feature) and mounts [`KitScanner.vue`](../web/src/features/account/emergencyKit/KitScanner.vue) — so the dependency runs `shared/` → `features/`, and every screen that reaches for the shared field drags a feature in behind it. **Measured: it is the only one.** A sweep of `web/src/shared/` for `features/` returns six files; four are comments naming a path (`handle401.ts`, `httpError.ts`, `naming.ts`, and the lifted `LocaleMarkdownEditor.vue`) and one is a test — `SecretKeyField.vue` holds the only runtime imports, both into the same feature directory. **The component is not the problem: it is correctly shared** — five screens drive it (`KeyRotationPanel`, `ChangePasswordPanel`, `LoginView`, `RecoverView`, `TenantJoinView`), which is why it was lifted in the first place. **What makes this worth a row is that the file already argues against itself:** its own header says the control stays *decoder-agnostic* — `accepts` is a caller-supplied predicate and an unrecognised decoded string is handed back via `scanned`, so that — in its own words, citing [ADR 0059](decisions/0059-device-linking.md) §1 — *"recognising a payload is the caller's job"* (that phrasing is the component's, not the ADR's; the ADR carries the one-scan-entry decision, not this sentence) — and then it hard-imports one specific payload's decoder. The inversion is the part of that argument that was never finished, not an oversight. **Two shapes, and the refine picks:** (a) the kit decoder + scanner move to `shared/` as well, since a shared component and one feature both use them; or (b) the component takes them the way it already takes `accepts` — injected decoder, scanner as a slot — which is the direction its own header points and costs every one of the five hosts a prop. **Note for whoever refines it:** `scripts/validate_locale_editor_single_home.py` records the neighbouring rule (*a feature component imported from another feature belongs in `shared/ui/`*) as **measured at zero violations**; this row is the other direction, which nothing guards — so a guard is a candidate here, but only after the shape is chosen, since (a) and (b) leave different trees. |
| 148 | **`vitest run` aborts the suite on an unhandled error while every test passes — PrimeVue's tablist timer fires after `MemoryDetail.test.ts` tears down** | web, tests, flake, trust-chain, DONE | **done 2026-10-01** (`cr-unit-tests-unmount-what-they-mount`, deleted). `web/src/test/setup.ts` calls `enableAutoUnmount(afterEach)`, so no test leaves a component mounted past its file's teardown. The mechanism: PrimeVue `TabList.mounted()` arms a **Node** timer it never clears, and when it lands after vitest has deleted the DOM globals, `getOuterWidth` throws `ReferenceError: HTMLElement is not defined` and the run aborts with every test green; contention is what widens that window. [`autoUnmount.test.ts`](../web/src/test/autoUnmount.test.ts) repeats vitest's teardown steps, so it reddens on demand without the setup line and pins it permanently. |
| 149 | ~~**The web test suite opens real sockets — 14 connection attempts to `localhost:3000` per run, from an `<img>` nobody meant to fetch**~~ | web, tests, hermetic, trust-chain, DONE | measured 2026-09-21 (while grounding #148) | **inbox.** A green `npx vitest run` (130 files, 1090 tests, rc 0) prints **14** `AggregateError`s, each `ECONNREFUSED ::1:3000` + `127.0.0.1:3000` — `npx vitest run 2>&1 \| grep -c ECONNREFUSED` → 70 lines. **Cause — and the first answer written here was WRONG, which is why the real one names its proof.** It is not an `<img>` and not a happy-dom resource setting: happy-dom 20.10.6 ships `enableImageFileLoading: false` by default, so it never fetches an image at all. The request is `GET /api/account/profile`, caught by listening on port 3000 during a run — user-agent `HappyDOM/20.10.6`, referer `http://localhost:3000/`, **14 of them, one per test**. `AppSidebar.vue:37` imports `getMyProfile` and `onMounted` calls it at `:249`; `session.apiBase` is `''` (same-origin by design), so the generated client issues a relative `/api/...` that happy-dom resolves against its own origin. `AppSidebar.test.ts` stubs the tenant list for exactly this reason — its comment says *"the tenant list is a network call (onMounted)"* — and missed the profile beside it. The component wraps the call in `try/catch` (the `~me` fallback), which is why the suite stayed green while the request left the process. Two earlier attributions were also wrong and are recorded so nobody repeats them: *"11 after AppSidebar, 3 after MemoryDetail.optimistic"* was stderr interleaving — running `AppSidebar.test.ts` **alone** produces all 14; and patching `globalThis.fetch` in `src/test/setup.ts` never fires, because the client's request does not pass through the binding visible there. **Why it is worth a row rather than a shrug — two reasons, and the second is the sharp one.** (1) Every rejection is caught today, so the run stays green; a rejection that lands *late* is exactly the shape of an **unhandled error with no failing test**, which is the symptom #148 records as costing a full diagnosis pass. So this is a second candidate source for that row's abort, independent of the PrimeVue timer — and it is the reason #148 no longer names that timer as settled. (2) **The tests are not hermetic.** Port 3000 is the single most common dev-server port there is; if anything happens to listen on it while the suite runs, these requests **succeed** and the suite silently reads a stranger's bytes. Today that is 14 refused connections; on a machine running some other project's dev server it is 14 answered ones, and nothing in the run would say so. **What NOT to do:** patch the two test files. The anchor is the asset import, not those files — any future component that renders an imported asset reopens it, which is [changes/README](../changes/README.md) § *a guard derives its anchor set from the code* applied to a fix. **DONE 2026-09-21** — `AppSidebar.test.ts` now stubs `getMyProfile` alongside the tenant list it already stubbed, returning an **empty** username on purpose (`personalScope('')` is `~me`, the hash these tests drive directly, so the stub restores today's behaviour instead of moving what the suite asserts). Measured after: that file 14 → **0** connection attempts with its 14 tests still green, and the **whole suite 0** (`npx vitest run 2>&1 \| grep -c ECONNREFUSED` → 0; 130 files / 1090 tests green). The three shapes this row first proposed were all answers to the wrong cause and are dropped. **The class is closed too, on the owner's call the same day — the net is built**, in `src/test/setup.ts` of **both** packages: happy-dom's `settings.fetch.interceptor` blocks every outbound request *before a socket opens*, records it, and an `afterEach` fails that test naming `METHOD URL`. **Bitten before trusted, four ways.** Green on the healthy tree (web 130 files / 1090 tests, admin-web 18 / 104, zero net messages, zero connection attempts). **Red on the real defect**: remove this fix's `getMyProfile` stub again and all 14 tests fail, each naming `GET http://localhost:3000/api/account/profile` — and that forged run shows **0** `ECONNREFUSED`, which is the proof the net *prevents* the socket rather than reporting it afterwards. Red in admin-web too, on a forged `/api/tenants`. And the **carve-out holds**: inside a scratch `*.e2e.test.ts` the request goes out for real and fails on the socket (`connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:59999`), not on the net. **Three limits, written at the code:** (a) `*.e2e.test.ts` is not armed — those drive a real backend through the same config, and the suffix is the discriminator because it is already what separates them, nothing is listed; (b) a **sync** request is recorded but not blocked (faking `ISyncResponse` is more machinery than the case deserves while both suites make zero); (c) an import-time request is attributed to the first test of its file. **admin-web was clean when the net went in** (measured: 0 connection attempts) and got it anyway, because `AdminConsoleView.vue`'s async fetching `onMounted` is the same shape that bit web, and because the ui-web-styleguide's rule about that copy says nothing is automatically true of both — it is carried over by hand or not at all. |

| 152 | **The Admin API is public — ADR 0065 moved only the admin UI behind localhost** | security, admin, backend, infra, contracts, DONE | **DONE 2026-09-24 (cr-admin-api-only-through-the-admin-container, deleted; `8f0c16e` contracts, `c6629d0` listener, `ae666e2` clients + deployment, `4fd058a` gate, `66d8cce` docs, `638357b` coverage).** Built as decided below. The backend's admin listener (`8090`, `veyq.admin-channel.port`) opens on `StartupEvent` — not on Quarkus' `HttpServerStart`, which fires asynchronously after the main bind and is not re-fired by a dev hot reload — so a bind failure fails startup (measured live). A post-match filter answers a marked operation off the admin channel with Vert.x's own unknown-route page; the first shape, an empty 404, was distinguishable, and the acceptance test that compares against a live unknown route caught it. The set is generated from the markers at build time; `AdminChannelPostureSweepTest` holds it to the Server-Owner gate both ways. The chart has a `veyq-app-admin` Service and a NetworkPolicy, held by `chart_meta_check.py` (xii)/(xiii). Web's `serverRole` probe and settings signpost are gone. **Measured on built images:** setup through Traefik → 404 (forged headers too), through the admin container → 201, `veyq-admin tenant list` → answers; live e2e 10/10 births through the listener. History below. **refined 2026-09-23** — on the V1 line. Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review: the `admin` nginx proxies `/api/` to the same backend the public origin routes with no path restriction, so every Server-Owner operation is reachable from the internet behind the owner's login — and `POST /api/setup/owner` behind nothing (`@PermitAll`, guarded only by `isSetupComplete()`): whoever calls it first after `up -d` becomes Server Owner. **Decided with the owner, and recorded where it binds** — [ADR 0065](decisions/0065-admin-ui-cli-separate-service.md) § Decision (the admin-channel bullet) and [uc.admin.admin-channel](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.admin-channel.md): `admin-web` stays a pure client; the backend answers the ten role-gated Server-Owner operations plus `createServerOwner` only on a second, internal **listener** the admin container is pointed at, classified by the socket (arrival port + a non-global peer) and never by a header, and 404s them on the public origin — so setup needs no bootstrap token. The operations are marked `x-veyq-channel: admin` per operation in `contracts/`, derived by the backend at build time and held to the Server-Owner gate by a sweep. Anonymous calls to role-gated operations keep their 401 (security runs before any post-match filter — measured); web's `serverRole` probe is deleted; `ADMIN_DOMAIN`-before-setup is documented, not enforced. **Measured in a refine spike** (throwaway `@QuarkusTest`, removed): a Vert.x server on `VertxHttpRecorder.getRootHandler()` runs the full JAX-RS + auth pipeline, and forged `Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-*` headers move `host()`/`remoteAddress()` but never `localAddress()`. Rejected, with reasons in the ADR: edge-only exclusion, a channel secret, an admin container holding state, a bootstrap token, Quarkus' own second listeners. |
| 153 | **A contract bound reaches the server only if someone also hand-writes its annotation — and almost none of the array/number bounds have one** | security, contracts, backend, trust-chain, NEW | **done 2026-09-24.** The rule — in a request body the schema bound and its annotation are one rule written twice, value for value, both ways, and a nested constrained object carries `@Valid` — is in [the styleguide](crosscutting/developer-styleguide.md) and enforced by `scripts/validate_request_bounds.py`, schema-scoped over everything reachable from a `requestBody`; its first run named 59 gaps across eight specs, all fixed.
