| 154 | **An upload must finish within the 600 s presign TTL — large videos fail on ordinary uplinks, and every chunk is presigned up front** | feature, media, backend, contracts, web, security, DONE | **done 2026-09-27** (`cr-upload-frame-size-contract`, then `cr-upload-windowed-presign`). The frame size is `chunk_frame_size` in the crypto vectors; `start` derives `chunkCount` from `byteSize` and signs each `PUT` for its chunk's exact length. `start` now signs only a blob's first window (`veyq.blob.presign-window`, 64) and `presignBlobUploadWindows` / `presignShareBlobUploadWindows` each next one, authorized again per request — a removed member or a revoked link stops an in-flight upload at its next window. A session is bound to its opener (account or share identity, `V51`); window, complete and cover commit resolve it only for that caller, closing the hole where any member or contributor identity could complete another's session by its id. The stale-session purge keys on the last issued window; `max-bytes` is a 20 GiB ceiling. The web client streams through one pump (a `file.slice` per frame, ≤4 `PUT`s in flight, the next window asked ahead, a `403` re-signed and retried once) for member, contributor and cover uploads. Folded in: review B9 (one chunk-key enumeration, one `PresignedRef`). Found on the way: the opt-in `BlobBatchE2ETest` had been `404`ing since `cr-scope-access-gate` (its seed lacked a tenant membership) — fixed, and a multi-window blob now moves through the real store there. **Not proven:** that a real tab stays within memory on a multi-GiB file — the unit tests bound what the pump reads and holds; a live upload that size is a manual check. The total bound is #155's. |
| 155 | **No storage quota exists — an anonymous contributor link can fill the server's disk** | security, sharing, media, admin, saas | **done 2026-09-27 — both halves built.** The first half (`cr-share-link-storage-limit`, deleted): every CONTRIBUTOR and UPLOADER link stores at most the tenant owner's per-link limit (`veyq_tenant.share_link_limit_bytes`, V55, 10 GiB), set in the web Storage rubric; a refused visitor tile reads "link full". The second half (`cr-tenant-storage-quota`, deleted): the Server Owner sets, changes or clears each tenant's quota (`veyq_tenant.storage_quota_bytes`, V56, none by default) with `setTenantStorageQuota` on the admin channel, in admin-web's tenant panel or `veyq-admin tenant quota`, and sees usage beside quota in the list; the tenant owner reads it read-only beside the usage. **Every** start — member, cover, link — now goes through one locked transaction (`MediaHandler.reserveAndOpen`): lock the tenant row, check the quota and, for a link, the per-link limit independently, insert or refuse whole `quota_exceeded`, commit, then presign. `StorageQuota` stays the only SUM, and `listTenants` asks it once, grouped. A refused member upload fails at once in the tray with a no-space reason and no retry — the queue no longer retries `quota_exceeded` for anyone, link visitors included — and a refused cover says the same. **Not proven:** the lock on MariaDB (both halves' races run on H2 only), and admin-web's suites, which no CI run has executed yet (ADR 0040; the coverage gap names what they will add). Decided with the owner into [ADR 0073](decisions/0073-storage-quota-belongs-to-whoever-pays-for-the-store.md), [uc.sharing.share-link-storage-limit](features/sharing/use-cases/uc.sharing.share-link-storage-limit.md) and [uc.admin.tenant-storage-quota](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.tenant-storage-quota.md); a per-member quota is #179, who writes the quota once a tenant can bring its own bucket is #41. |
| 156 | **Host → tenant is resolved in four places, and recovery trusts the raw Host — a tenant owner receives recovery links for other tenants' accounts** | security, account, backend, tenancy, NEW | **done 2026-09-27** — the request-tenant half landed 2026-09-24 (`cr-request-tenant-and-tenant-bound-recovery`, deleted): `RequestTenant` in `shared/tenant` is the one Host → tenant resolution and handlers take a tenant id; recovery mints and mails only for a member of the resolved tenant and answers before anything keyed by the address; `AccountStore.findByEmail` + `normalizeEmail` are the one lookup and the one email rule; `scripts/validate_lookup_single_home.py` keeps all three single. **The C8 side item landed 2026-09-27** (`cr-cold-credential-proves-the-secret-key`, deleted): the recovery tag is gone, and completing a reset needs a signature of the account's Secret-Key proof key, which neither a database dump nor a linked device's Master Key can produce. The C8 side item was first planned as storing the recovery proof hashed; the #159 refine the same day replaced the tag altogether with a Secret-Key proof signature, which also closes what hashing could not (a linked device computes the tag from the Master Key it holds), so that CR was withdrawn before it started. Refined 2026-09-23 into [ADR 0054](decisions/0054-password-recovery-two-factor-release.md) § Decision 1, §4 and §6, `uc.account.recover-access` and [scopes-and-tenancy](crosscutting/scopes-and-tenancy.md) § Tenant resolution. The refine added two things. (1) The timing half of §6 was false: the SMTP send ran inside the request, so a known address answered one mail transaction slower. The request now answers first. (2) A Server Owner outside any tenant has no recovery; that was accepted, not fixed. Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review. `TenantHostResolver` calls itself the single home for the header rule, yet `RecoveryResource` and `AccountProfileResource` parse `X-Forwarded-Host`/`Host` by hand, and `TenantStore` + `AccountProfileHandler` run their own `TenantEntity.find("domain", …)` (locator: `grep -rn 'X-Forwarded-Host\|find("domain"' backend/core/src/main/kotlin backend/apps/memories/src/main/kotlin`). **The bite:** `RecoveryHandler.request` picks the mailer by the request's host but looks the user up instance-wide. So the owner of tenant A, who controls A's SMTP relay, can request a recovery for any address with `Host: a.example` and read the token of an account in tenant B, or of the Server Owner. That routes a recovery token through a third party (ADR 0054 §4) and gives every tenant owner an instance-wide existence oracle (ADR 0054 §6). The second factor still holds, because the wrap needs the Secret Key. **Direction agreed:** one request-scoped `RequestTenant` (tenantId, domain) in `shared/tenant` replaces the copies, and handlers take a `tenantId` rather than a host. Recovery mails only an account that belongs to the resolved tenant and answers 202 either way. Suspension (review S6) is deliberately *not* here: it moves to the shared scope gate, which gets its own row. Settled in the same conversation: a tenant owner's own relay seeing recovery mail for their *own* members is already covered by ADR 0054's two factors, so no doc change. **Side item (review C8):** the recovery proof was a Master-Key hash stored as the raw bearer value, so a database read plus the victim's mailbox was enough for a reset — closed by the Secret-Key proof signature above. **Folded in (review B10):** email normalization is spread over six handlers and three lookups bypass `AccountStore.findByEmail` (`VeyqUser.find("email", …)` in `TenantStore`, `RecoveryStore`, `LoginHandler`); the recovery rework touches those lookups anyway. |
| 158 | **Nothing deliberately serves `web/dist` in production — no SPA fallback, the CSP is a build-time `<meta>`, no security headers** | security, infra, backend, web, helm, V1, NEW | **DONE 2026-09-24 (cr-app-shell-served-by-backend, deleted).** Built as decided below: the backend's app shell (`backend/…/shared/appshell/`) answers HTML navigations with `index.html`, sends the emitted policy as a header with the signer's store origin appended, sets `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy` and HSTS, revalidates everything but `/assets/*`, and refuses to boot a shell with no policy or a CSP `<meta>`. Measuring the CR's *"not measured"* suspension note found `/` loading on a suspended tenant's domain and deep links refused, so `TenantSuspensionFilter` now refuses `/api` only (owner's call; ADR 0066 updated). The HSTS switch is `VEYQ_HSTS_ENABLED` (compose) / `hsts.enabled` (Helm). Live-verified on a fast-jar in `store-origin` mode; Service-Worker video playback under the policy and a real cluster are **not** ([cc.security.csp § Verification](crosscutting/cc.security.csp.md#verification)). Refined 2026-09-23 into [ADR 0069](decisions/0069-backend-serves-the-web-app-shell.md) + [cc.security.csp](crosscutting/cc.security.csp.md) → `cr-app-shell-served-by-backend`. **Measured 2026-09-23** on a fast-jar built with `dist` in `META-INF/resources`: (a) holds, since `/recover`, `/tenant/accept`, `/s/<id>` and `/login` loaded as HTML all answer 404 `Resource not found`; (b) holds, since the chart signs for `https://<storeDomain>` in `store-origin` mode and a page-context fetch from the served shell to a store on another origin fires `securitypolicyviolation` on `connect-src`. The same run found `index.html` and `sw.js` served `public, immutable, max-age=86400`, so after an upgrade a browser runs the old shell against the new API for up to a day. On the owner's call it is folded into the same CR, together with fail-closed on a shell that has no policy. Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review. The `Dockerfile` copies `web/dist` into `META-INF/resources` and Quarkus serves those files as plain static resources. `grep -rln 'RoutingContext\|@Route' backend/core/src/main/kotlin backend/apps/memories/src/main/kotlin` finds only the bearer mechanism, and there is no Quinoa. Three consequences: (a) **SPA fallback** — the router uses `createWebHistory()` (`web/src/router.ts`), so a direct load of `/recover#…` (the recovery mail), an invite link or `/s/…` is expected to 404 in production. The e2e suites are API-level vitest runs that never load a page, so nothing would notice. (b) **Helm** — `web/src/shared/security/csp.ts` bakes `connect-src 'self' <pwned> [geocoder]` into a `<meta>` at build time, while the Helm target signs presigned URLs for the store's own origin (`store-origin` mode), so every chunk fetch is expected to fail on CSP. A header cannot loosen a `<meta>` policy (the two intersect), which makes `cc.security.csp.md`'s "add the store host at the serving layer" unbuildable. (c) **Headers** — the app origin sends no HSTS, `frame-ancestors`, `nosniff` or `Referrer-Policy`. `cc.security.csp.md` § Deferred parks them on "the future production web-serving component … There is no production web-serving component in the repo yet", a premise the Dockerfile has since made false. **Decided 2026-09-23:** a small backend piece serves the app shell on every target. It answers any HTML GET outside `/api`, `/__media` and real files with `index.html`. In production it sends the CSP as a **response header** and no `<meta>`, with `csp.ts` still the one source: the build emits the policy, and the backend appends only the configured store origin at runtime. It also sets `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy`, and HSTS (on by default, moderate `max-age`, switchable off). It lives in the backend rather than in Traefik or the Ingress for the same reason the inert-store fix lives in the signer: it is the one thing every deployment, and every self-hoster's own proxy, has in common. Ranked above #154/#155. |
| 159 | **A tenant or group owner's session — no keys — is enough to get the scope's plaintext: admission is unsigned and grant delivery is automatic** | security, crypto, spaces, tenancy, V1, NEW | **done 2026-09-27 — both doors closed.** The first door ((1) + (2)) closed 2026-09-27 (`cr-admission-is-a-key-holder-act`, deleted): every membership that is to receive a grant carries an admission tag under the scope's current epoch key, which every wrapping client verifies (a worker declines `admission_unverified`), a rotation re-tags or drops every open admission and the server refuses one that forgets any; every grant is signed by its granter, and a client opens a personal grant only self-signed and a shared one only under the granter's pinned key. On the operator row the flip was checked against the active operator and split it: *admits an account of its own* reads **Cannot**; *plants a scope key* reads **Cannot** in the personal scope and under a pinned granter, **Can** under a granter identity the operator fabricates (TOFU's first-use gap). Existing memberships carry no admission and existing grants no signature; nothing is migrated, and the dev instance is reset after this wave. The second door (3) closed 2026-09-27 (`cr-cold-credential-proves-the-secret-key`, deleted): rotation, change-password and recovery each carry a signature of the Secret-Key proof key, verified by the server, which answers `403 secret_key_proof_failed`. It binds every caller **but the operator**, who writes the directory itself, so [security-model](crosscutting/security-model.md#what-holds-today-on-a-client-the-operator-does-not-serve)'s row stays *Can* with that reason (owner's call, 2026-09-27). Existing accounts carry no proof key and can do none of the three until the dev instance is reset (batched after wave 9). Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review; refined the same day into [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-grant-distribution-invite-wrap.md) move 6, [ADR 0028](decisions/0028-identity-authenticity-tofu-verification.md) Decision 6 and [ADR 0060](decisions/0060-key-rotation-after-compromise.md) Decision 2 (+ [ADR 0054](decisions/0054-password-recovery-two-factor-release.md)). The refine changed the triage's mechanism and found a second door. **(1)** An owner signature checked "against the pinned owner key" needs a trust root, and the server supplies it — a malicious operator names an identity it generated as owner. So admission is an **HMAC tag under the scope's current epoch key**: only a key-holder computes it, every wrapping client checks it, a rotation re-tags and thereby strips a removed member of the power; role and identity key deliberately unbound (ADR 0020 move 6). **(2)** Grants are signed by their granter; the personal scope opens only a **self-signed** grant (chosen over deriving `K_SC` from the Master Key — one rule for every grant, the scope model unchanged). **(3)** `rotateAccountKeys` accepts any session, and ADR 0060's cold-credential gate was client-side only — which binds nothing against an attacker running their own client (a device-link holder; a password holder via `changePassword`). So a **Secret-Key proof key** (Ed25519 from the Secret Key alone, registered at birth) signs rotation, change-password and recovery, verified by the server, and replaces the old recovery proof (a Master-Key hash a linked device can compute and a DB dump contains). Residuals, named in ADR 0020 § Consequences: an operator redeeming a genuine tenant admission with its own key, and a departing member colluding at rotation (#161 (b)). |
| 160 | **Ciphertext is bound to its purpose, never to the object it belongs to — a server can swap or replay ciphertexts within one key** | security, crypto, contracts, V1 | **done 2026-09-27** (`cr-seal-binds-its-object`, [ADR 0072](decisions/0072-a-seal-binds-its-object.md)). Every seal goes through the typed `seal`/`open`/`sealTo`/`openAs` in `@veyq/crypto/v1`, whose context binds the purpose and the object (ids, epoch or version); the raw wraps left the package's exports, and `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json#seal` holds one vector per purpose, compared with the union both ways. Clients mint the ids of what they create (`id_taken` on a clash), a grant-delivery completion states its epoch (`stale_epoch` otherwise), and the four live swaps — the upload chain among them — are refused at the seal (`web/src/features/memory/sealBinding.test.ts`). Decided at implement time: a share id stays the 43-character token; item marks bind scope + node, not the marking member (the gap is #187); a public share's guest sees the scope id. **Every ciphertext written before it stops opening — a development instance needs a reset.** Freshness stays #161's. |
| 161 | **The trusted-tier guarantees the ADRs claim are not all built — correct the docs now, build the rest before V1** | security, crypto, docs, native, V1, NEW | **done 2026-09-28** → five CRs, all done — the client-chosen KDF profile (`cr-kdf-params-client-chosen`, 2026-09-24: `decodeKdfParams` refuses any served profile but the one the client writes, before deriving), and (a) the pairing authenticated by the QR (`cr-pairing-authenticated-by-the-qr`, 2026-09-27: the QR carries a 32-byte secret, the handle and a channel key derive from it, and the answer and the envelope each carry a MAC under that key — vectors in `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json#pairing_channel`; the three relay bites were red on the untouched tree; (g) folded in as `useGrantReceiver()`. **A pairing code shown by an older client no longer parses** — nothing persists across a ceremony, so no migration), and (b) the removal in one request (`cr-removal-rotates-in-one-request`, 2026-09-27: `rotateScope` names the member it removes and the server deletes the membership and commits the epoch in one transaction; the remover's client excludes that member from the directory it picked from; a scope whose grant is older than its current epoch opens as awaiting access, and a node create below the current epoch is `409 stale_epoch` — ADR 0024 Decision 4 and Decision 6's layer-1 half). and (d) the uploader's drop key (`cr-uploader-never-holds-the-memory-key`, 2026-09-28: a Memory keeps a hybrid drop keypair's private half under `K_node`, an UPLOADER link carries only its public half under `K_share` and no wrap, and each guest identity seals under a key of its own wrapped to it; every reader is served that chain — node, media page, bucket list, a viewer link's identities. The two web bites were red on the untouched tree; V57. **A stored-format change: an UPLOADER link made before it no longer resolves, so the owner's development instance needs a reset**). And (c) the trust state (`cr-trust-state-never-goes-backwards`, 2026-09-28: each device keeps local marks per account — the pin-store version, the device-register version, each scope's epoch — filed under a one-way derivation of the Master Key rather than any id the server names; an older store, a missing one after a mark and an unopenable one are refused as `trust_state_rolled_back` / `trust_state_unreadable`, before every read and write; the epoch floor reuses layer 1's closed *awaiting the current key* state (owner decision); the linker's marks ride the MAC-verified pairing envelope, and an account-key rotation carries them across. All six bites were red on the untouched tree. **A store that does not open now stays refused, with no way back yet** — new row #192). Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review; refined 2026-09-24. **The doc half is done:** [security-model § What holds today](crosscutting/security-model.md#what-holds-today-on-a-client-the-operator-does-not-serve) is now the one ledger of what holds against an active operator on a client it does not serve, per attack, with what closes each row; ADR 0026 gained that tier's row, and ten more ADRs, `CLAUDE.md` and eight use-case and enabler docs now state their guarantee and name what is not built; each points at the ledger or at its own *What is still open*. It also corrected `security-model.md`, which stated #159 and #160 as holding. **Every reviewer claim measured and holding, and the refine found more.** (a) The *add a device* direction is as open as the sign-in one: its five-digit check number is one hash over a bundle whose Ed25519 half the wrap never uses, so the relay grinds its own bundle in ~10⁵ hashes. Decided: a 32-byte secret in the QR, from which the handle and a channel key derive, and a MAC on both relayed messages. It was chosen over a PAKE, which covers only the sign-in direction and would be home-grown crypto. (b) Also bites on an honest server: a member with a stale grant writes under the old epoch and the server accepts it, and a tab closed between remove and rotate leaves the group unrotated. Decided: removal and rotation in one request, excluded by the remover's own choice, plus a device-local epoch floor. A withheld rotation (fork) and the queued rotations stay honest-operator only, named in ADR 0024; the owner chose that over designing a client-held roster. (c) Worse than read: a pin store that does not open counts as empty, and a rollback reinstates a retired key as a match. Decided: fail closed plus a local high-water mark per device (ADR 0028 Decision 7), which a linked device inherits through the authenticated envelope. (d) Also leaks every sibling share's key and password through `sealed_share_key`. Decided: a per-Memory drop key (ADR 0031 point 7), chosen over documenting write-only as access control. (e) Only a DoS: no downgrade path exists, and ADR 0017 rule 5 now says the server never chooses a cost parameter — built. (f) Documented in ADR 0068 and ADR 0024. (g) Folds into the pairing CR. **Adjacent:** #177's race is closed by `cr-rotation-states-its-epoch`, whose stated epoch is the expected-epoch check. New row #180. |
| 162 | **Runtime hardening on both targets — the internet-facing Traefik holds the Docker socket, and Helm has no NetworkPolicy** | security, infra, helm, NEW | **done 2026-09-28 — on the V1 line.** First half **done 2026-09-27** (`cr-traefik-file-provider-only`): Traefik runs the file provider only on both compose stacks and no container mounts the Docker socket — the ingress sink renders the services, the bootstrap and admin routers and the store middleware; the dev route is `infra/traefik-dev/blobs.yml`. Second half **built 2026-09-27** (`cr-runtime-hardening-both-targets`): every production compose service unprivileged with a read-only root on three networks (`validate_compose_posture.py`), and on Helm a read-only root plus a default-deny NetworkPolicy set naming `networkPolicy.ingressFrom` (`chart_meta_check.py` (v), (xiii), (xv)–(xviii)). **Live `up` passed 2026-09-28** (record in [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-runtime-hardening-on-both-targets.md) § Consequences); the CR is deleted. Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review, refined the same day into [ADR 0063](decisions/0063-database-driven-tenant-ingress.md) (the ingress sink renders all of compose's routing — services, bootstrap and admin routers included — because every static-file layout was measured unworkable on Traefik v3.6) and the new [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-runtime-hardening-on-both-targets.md) (posture and reachability on both targets: a compose ownership init because compose has no `fsGroup`, separate compose networks, a NetworkPolicy set whose ingress-controller peer the operator must name, `trusted-proxies` as an optional value because a pod-IP default would trust every pod). **Compose:** no service sets `user:`, `cap_drop`, `no-new-privileges` or `read_only`; Garage runs as uid 0 under compose but as 1000 in the chart. **Helm:** `grep -rn NetworkPolicy charts/` finds nothing, and neither target sets `quarkus.http.proxy.trusted-proxies`, so any pod in the cluster can reach the app directly with a forged `X-Forwarded-For`. The per-IP limiter's trust in that header is accepted in `cc.security.rate-limit.md`; the missing NetworkPolicy is what widens it on Helm. |
| 163 | **A self-hoster cannot verify where the image came from** | security, infra, release, V1, NEW | **DONE — runtime half 2026-09-25, build half 2026-09-28** (`cr-image-provenance-verifiable`): `infra/README.md` § Verifying a release verifies `veyq` and `veyq-admin` against `container.yml` on `main`/`release/<major>.x` of `raddatzk/veyq` and deploys both by the verified digest on compose and Helm; the publish no longer accepts `master`; every third-party image the recipe, the chart, the Dockerfiles and the backup tool name is `repo:tag@sha256:<index digest>`; held by `validate_release_identity.py` assertion 7, `validate_image_pins.py` and `chart_meta_check.py` (xiv); Dependabot's image ecosystems configured. Never run against a real release — none exists. **Build half BUILT 2026-09-27** (`cr-build-inputs-pinned`): `generate:api` in `web/` and `admin-web/` runs `scripts/verify_openapi_generator_jar.mjs` first, which downloads a missing generator JAR from Maven Central and checks it — present or not — against `scripts/openapi_generator_jar.sha256`; the backend generates with the same 7.23.0 (was 7.21.0); the wrapper carries `distributionSha256Sum`; `backend/gradle/verification-metadata.xml` verifies every dependency, the native path included; all held by `validate_build_input_pins.py`. **Build half DONE 2026-09-28:** three consumers the metadata write never ran broke and were fixed on main — `quarkusDev`'s bootstrap resolver (two POMs added), the IDE's Gradle `-src` zip (a third trust rule, scoped to `gradle:gradle`), and both image builds (`openapitools.json` copied before `npm ci`); the owner's IntelliJ *Sync All Gradle Projects* then passed. The CR is deleted. Raised 2026-09-23 by the security/reuse review, refined the same day into [ADR 0057](decisions/0057-release-identity-and-deployment-recipe.md) §3 (the identity pinned literally to `raddatzk/veyq`'s `container.yml` on `main`/`release/<major>.x`, deploy by the verified digest) and §6 (a release pins what it runs and what it is built from). The refine also found the upgrade runbook pulls and pins only `veyq`, never `veyq-admin`, and that the backend has no Gradle verification at all; the owner folded in the wrapper checksum, dependency verification and one generator version (backend 7.21.0 vs. clients 7.23.0). The only `cosign verify` is a comment in `.github/workflows/container.yml`, and its `--certificate-identity-regexp '^https://github.com/<repo>/'` accepts a signature from any workflow on any ref. `infra/README.md` has no verification step and extracts the recipe by a mutable tag. The signed recipe then pulls `mariadb:11.4`, `dxflrs/garage:v2.0.0` and `traefik:v3.6` by tag (the last one holds the Docker socket, #162), and the `Dockerfile`'s base images are tag-pinned too. **Decided 2026-09-23 — before V1**, because V1's audience pulls exactly these images: a documented verification step with the identity pinned to `container.yml` on `main`/`release/*`, and third-party images digest-pinned in the recipe and in the chart values. **Folded in 2026-09-23:** the same class at build time — `web/openapitools.json` pins openapi-generator-cli `7.23.0` by version only, and `npm ci` downloads that JAR with no pinned digest, while the generated client is untracked, so a poisoned generator's output would never be reviewed. |
| 164 | **The instance backup puts the config-secret key next to the database dump, unencrypted, and the dump keeps default permissions** | security, infra, backup | **done 2026-09-27** (`cr-backup-key-apart-from-dump`, deleted), as [ADR 0056](decisions/0056-instance-durable-state-and-cold-logical-backup.md) §1 decides: `scripts/instance_backup.py capture` writes leg (c) to `--key-into FILE` and refuses a destination inside the capture directory before anything is dumped (`--key-beside-dump` is the knowing exception), the manifest records `configSecret.placement` (`separate | beside | absent`, `backupFormat` 2), `restore` refuses a separately kept key that is not supplied unless told `--without-key`, the whole run is under `umask 077` with the capture root `0700`, and `verify` checks all of it. `infra/README.md` § Capture says to encrypt a capture before it leaves the host and keep the key on another medium; `scripts/run_web_e2e.py` captures that way, verifies every capture, and refuses a keyless restore before the keyed one succeeds. ADR 0050 is true again without amendment. |
| 165 | ~~**Client hygiene — five small fixes with no design question**~~ | security, web, admin, docs, DONE | **DONE 2026-09-23** (`7ccc504`, `291f7c0`, `509236d`, `99fc09c`) — `cr-client-hygiene`, refined and built in one session under the named mechanical exception (owner's call), then deleted. (a) admin-web's owner-invite link is always `https://`; web's identical `inviteLink.ts` had had no importer since ADR 0055 and is deleted. (b) `TenantJoinView` strips the secret once read, and it and `RecoverView` keep vue-router's `history.state` (the latter used to null it); a reload after the strip says to reopen the link. (d) admin-web's CSP lives in a tested module without `frame-ancestors`; nginx's `X-Frame-Options` is named as the whole guard. **What the row could not have predicted — the second-angle sweeps widened it:** (c) also reached admin-web's `mail`/`terms` catalog groups, which no admin-web source asks for (deleted, not fixed), two stale comments, and ADR 0048's own decision headline; (e) grew from three claims to about a dozen sites — the *"crypto wire-format spec"* three docs cite never existed, and the device-key overclaim sat in ADR 0005 §5 itself, the paragraph written to prevent overclaiming. **Left open:** admin-web's catalog still has other dead groups (e.g. `tenant.join`) — see #170, which already listed them. |
| 166 | **The breached-password check sends SHA-1 prefixes of partial passwords to a third party while the user types, and it is not opt-in** | security, privacy, web, admin, DONE | **done 2026-09-25** (`cr-breach-check-on-submit-opt-in`). All four password-choosing dialogs (change password, join a tenant, recover access, admin-web setup) carry an unticked box with a one-sentence note, and `useBreachCheck` (`features/account/passwordWarning.ts`, identical in web and admin-web) queries once on submit over the final password. A hit, or a check that could not run, stops the first submit; the same password then goes ahead with no second request. `pwnedCount` returns `null` for "could not run" instead of `0`. The rule and the tests that pin it: [security-model](crosscutting/security-model.md) § third-party boundary → *Verification*. Live pass (web, change password): typing and an unticked submit sent nothing; a ticked submit sent one `range/E6B6A`. |
| 167 | **`vue-i18n` 10 is deprecated — both web and admin-web depend on it** | web, admin, dependencies | **done 2026-09-24** (`cr-vue-i18n-v11`). Both packages are on vue-i18n 11.4.12; the bump needed no code change, as the refine measured. Each package's i18n test now compiles every message of every locale with `@intlify/message-compiler` and names any key that fails. The literal is now `{'@'}`, pinned by a mounted `GroupInvitationsView` test. admin-web's test was bitten by injection. A third assertion holds the test's compiler to vue-i18n's own `VERSION`, so a later bump that forgets the devDependency turns it red instead of testing an old compiler. |

| 174 | **Five hand-written visibility pollers, none guarded against overlapping ticks** | code-health, web, account, NEW | **DONE 2026-09-24 — both halves.** Web (`cr-visibility-poller`, deleted): all five pollers are instances of `createVisibilityPoller` (`web/src/shared/state/visibilityPoller.ts`) — skip-not-queue while a tick is in flight, an `AbortSignal` fenced on stop and restart, checked by every tick before it applies anything. Held-open-request tests were red on the old loops first (both pairing pollers, then the two worker pollers), and each property's test was bitten by removing it. **What the work order got wrong:** it asked for the five existing suites to stay green *unchanged*, while also — rightly — putting grant delivery and scope rotation under the in-flight guard; those two suites drove the clock synchronously, so a mocked worker's promise never settled and the guard skipped every later tick. Their clock calls became `advanceTimersByTimeAsync`, assertions untouched. Server (`cr-pairing-collect-single-use`, deleted): `markCollected` is a compare-and-set on `SEALED`, and only the collect that wins it gets the envelope. The loser answers as a spent channel does, which `PairingSliceTest.concurrentCollectsHandOverTheGrantOnce` pins. That test was red on the old code. **Refine record:** Refined 2026-09-23. **The overlap is a real bug for the pairing collect.** The collect poller ticks every 2 s with no in-flight guard, so any collect slower than that overlaps the next. `PairingStore.markCollected` flips the status unconditionally and hands out the envelope either way, so two concurrent collects both get the grant, and the onboarding tail runs twice, which `onboardTail.ts` forbids. When the collects are serialized, the loser's 404 flips the card to *gone* in the middle of the tail. **Decided:** the server's compare-and-set is a backend CR of its own; the contract already promises it. The web helper `createVisibilityPoller` is a factory, because the two pairing loops must not share state. It skips a tick rather than queueing it, and fences in-flight ticks on stop with an `AbortSignal`. A second compare-and-set gap found alongside is #177. |

A web bundle cached from before the deploy gets `400` on login until it reloads. |

Owner's call 2026-09-23. | 177 | **Scope rotation commits without an expected epoch — two concurrent rotations can link the chain to the wrong predecessor** | crypto, spaces, backend, web, contracts, V1, NEW | **done 2026-09-27** (`cr-rotation-states-its-epoch`). `rotateScope` states the epoch it creates, and `RotationPolicy.plan` commits it only as the scope's current epoch + 1; anything else is `409 stale_epoch` with nothing stored ([ADR 0024](decisions/0024-scope-key-rotation.md) §4, [en.spaces.scope-key-rotation](features/spaces/enablers/en.spaces.scope-key-rotation.md) E3). `ScopesStore.rotate` reads the scope row under `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE`, so two in-flight commits serialize into one `201` and one clean `409`; without the lock the loser was a `500` from `ux_scope_epoch` (3/3 runs). On web every rotation goes through `commitRotation` (removal, queued job, account re-key), which re-opens and rebuilds once from a fresh key, and the scope-rotation worker declines a job that stays stale. Proof: `ScopesSliceTest.aRotationBuiltOnASupersededEpochIs409AndLeavesTheChainIntact` (red before the fix: `201` where `409` is expected) and `…twoConcurrentRotationsStatingTheSameEpochCommitOnce`, `RotationPolicyTest` under PIT, criterion `rotation-commits-only-the-next-epoch`. The residual it makes visible stays open as #178. |
| 178 | **A removal can end without a rotation — the synchronous one has no job behind it, and a queued one can swallow a later removal** | crypto, spaces, backend, web, V1, DONE | **(1) done 2026-09-27; (2) DONE 2026-10-02** (`cr-queued-rotation-owners-only-no-leased-join`, deleted). An event joins a `scope-rotation` job only while it is `PENDING`, read under a row lock and on the scope row's lock (a tenant cut takes it, in ascending scope order, before it deletes), so a claim racing a join lands after the event; an event after a claim gets a job of its own, and a job completes only on its holder's own commit during the lease (`V65__job_rotated_epoch.sql`). The rejected shape (a commit closes older jobs) is recorded in [uc.spaces.complete-queued-rotation](features/spaces/use-cases/uc.spaces.complete-queued-rotation.md) E6. Pinned by `QueuedRotationSliceTest` (including the held-transaction race) and `RotationSliceTest`. |

(1) **Attribution:** covers record no uploader (`veyq_node.cover_blob_id` only), and media from before `created_by` existed carry `null`, whose fallback owner is the scope's oldest membership (`MediaStore.kt`, the `created_by` fallback). | 187 | **The server can relabel which of a node's item marks is the caller's own** | security, crypto, memory, DONE | **done 2026-10-01** (`cr-item-mark-self-tag`, deleted). An item mark's plaintext is `{"tag","ids"}`: the tag is an HMAC under `HKDF(masterKey, "veyq/v1/item-mark-self")` over the `item-mark-self` statement (scope, node, SHA-256 of the de-duplicated, sorted ids), and an entry is mine only when the server flags it `self` **and** the tag verifies — everything else counts as someone else's set ([ADR 0068](decisions/0068-favorites-two-confidentiality-levels.md) §9; vectors `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json#item_mark_self`). An account-key rotation re-tags the caller's own marks through the new `GET /api/item-favorites` (tag `own-item-favorites`), re-sealing only rows whose tag verifies under the old key. B1 (relabel) and B2 (the toggle laundering the other member's set into the caller's row) were red on the untouched tree. **A stored-format change: every existing item mark reads as someone else's until re-toggled** — the owner's development instance needs a reset or a re-toggle. |
| 188 | **A production restore places the config-secret key owned by whoever ran it, and the app no longer runs as root** | infra, backup, DONE | **done 2026-10-15** (cr-restore-places-the-key-as-the-runtime-user, closed; phase 3 proven in containers on Docker Desktop on the owner's go - the pre-fix tool left the key `0:0 600` and the app `ABSENT`, today's places it `10001:10001 600` and the app reads it, `OK`; the documented `sudo -E` host invocation itself is still unrehearsed, [infra/README.md](../infra/README.md) § Restore). Was **in-progress 2026-10-01**: the tool gives the key its directory's owner and refuses an unplaceable one before the first write (self-test, bitten); compose-production and Helm restore are written for leg (c). **Owed by the owner:** the live run on a production stack as root (phase 3), which closes the CR. **Refined 2026-09-28.** Re-derived by reading `faf9ef4`, not run. The row holds, but narrower and sharper than it was written. **Narrower:** a key that already exists at the target keeps its owner, because `O_TRUNC` does not chown. So only the `O_CREAT` branch bites. **Sharper:** that branch is the documented one. `ConfigSecretSeal` is constructed lazily, and nothing touches it at startup (measured: the startup observers never reference it). A fresh install's one start therefore generates no key, and the restore creates it root-owned. The state then reads `ABSENT`, not `MISMATCH`. Two findings beyond the row: placement runs *after* the database and object loads, so a `PermissionError` there half-restores the instance; and Helm has no written restore procedure for leg (c) at all. **Original row:** **inbox.** Found 2026-09-27 by `cr-backup-key-apart-from-dump`, *read, not run*: `scripts/instance_backup.py restore` writes the key `0600` as the invoking user, and on a production host the documented target is the `veyq_veyq-config-secret` volume's mountpoint, written as root. Since the runtime hardening the app runs as uid 10001 (Dockerfile `USER`), so it cannot read a root-owned `0600` key: `ConfigSecretSeal.readOrGenerate` catches the IO failure, sealing becomes unavailable, and the instance refuses invites and resets — the A2 symptom with the key actually present. The restore has never run against a production stack (none has been brought up), so this is unmeasured. Candidate: the restore takes the owner uid (or the runbook places the key through the app container, as `kubectl cp` would on Helm). |
| 189 | **Nothing checks the committed `gradle-wrapper.jar`** | security, backend, release, NEW | **done 2026-10-01** — the direct fix below landed as written: four re-pins, `scripts/gradle_wrapper_jar.sha256`, check 5 in `validate_build_input_pins.py`; all three bites went red and restored byte-identical (measured). **Refined 2026-09-28: a direct fix, no CR.** Re-derived: the row's premise was half wrong. Every workflow job that runs `./gradlew` uses `gradle/actions/setup-gradle` v4.4.3, and its `validate-wrappers` input defaults to `true` (measured: `setup-gradle/action.yml` at that release). So CI *would* validate the JAR against Gradle's published list. The container build `needs: test`, which is one of those jobs. But no workflow has ever run (there is no remote), and the pin `48b5f213…` is an **annotated tag object**, not a commit. It is the only such pin among the 13 distinct `uses:` SHAs (measured against the GitHub API: git/tags 200 and git/commits 404, while the other 12 are commits). Whether Actions resolves a tag-object SHA is remembered-doubtful. The JAR's hash `7d3a4ac4…6172` equals Gradle's published `gradle-8.14.3-wrapper.jar.sha256` (measured with curl; 9.5.1's is `497c8c2a…a9c7`, so the JAR predates the distribution it launches, which is harmless). **Exact change:** (1) re-pin all four `gradle/actions/setup-gradle@` uses to the commit `ed408507eac070d1f99cc633dbcf757c94c7933a # v4.4.3`. (2) Add check 5 to `scripts/validate_build_input_pins.py`: every tracked `gradle-wrapper.jar` hashes to a line in a new `scripts/gradle_wrapper_jar.sha256`, whose header names the services.gradle.org URL the value was cross-checked against (trust-on-first-write, like the generator pin). Also no workflow may set `validate-wrappers: false`. Anchors are derived by `git ls-files` and a workflow glob, never listed. **Bite:** append one byte to a scratch copy of the JAR → red; flip one hex digit of the pin → red; add `validate-wrappers: false` to one step → red. Use snapshot-and-restore (changes/README.md). The value of this change: a swapped binary now needs a *readable* one-line pin diff to go green. **Original row:** **inbox.** Left by `cr-build-inputs-pinned` (2026-09-27): the wrapper now verifies the distribution it downloads, but the JAR that does the verifying is a tracked binary no review can read and no job validates (no Gradle wrapper-validation step in `.github/workflows/`). Measured the same day: its SHA-256 `7d3a4ac4…6172` is Gradle's published wrapper checksum for 8.14.1–8.14.5, so today's JAR is genuine; a swapped one would pass every current check. Candidate: the guard holds it to Gradle's published checksum list, or a workflow runs the wrapper-validation action. |

| 191 | **The Dockerfile's `runtime-native` target does not build — `microdnf install curl` conflicts with the base's `curl-minimal`** | release, images, native, NEW | **done 2026-10-01** — steps (1)–(3) landed as written; the new last-stage assertion was red on the untouched tree, green after the move, and red again when the stage was moved back (measured). The base's curl is measured now: at the pinned digest `rpm -qf /usr/bin/curl` → `curl-minimal-7.76.1-31.el9`, and `curl --version` lists `http https`. **The done-when's `docker build --target runtime-native .` did NOT complete:** the Mandrel compile (`backend-build-native`) passed `[1/8] Initializing`, sized its heap at 5.85 GB (75.6 % of the VM), and was killed ~260 s later, before printing `[2/8]`, with exit 137 / `cannot allocate memory` on this Mac's 7.75 GB Docker VM (measured) — taking the dev MariaDB down with it for a crash-recovery restart, so do not re-run it beside a live dev stack. So whether the native target builds at all is still unshown — the load-bearing unknown below, left for its own row. **Refined 2026-09-28: a direct fix, no CR.** The healthcheck needs `curl -fsS http://…` (`infra/docker-compose.prod.yml` healthcheck). Helm uses `httpGet` probes and needs no binary (`charts/veyq/templates/app-deployment.yaml`). `curl-minimal` already provides `/usr/bin/curl`. This was measured from the CentOS Stream 9 `curl.spec` (release 43): `%files -n curl-minimal` ships `curl.minimal`, and `RemovePathPostfixes: .minimal` installs it as `curl`; `Provides: curl` and `Conflicts: curl` explain the error; HTTP is not among its `--disable-*`. That the pinned base's -31 build matches this spec is remembered, not measured. **Exact change:** (1) delete `Dockerfile`'s `RUN microdnf install -y curl && microdnf clean all`, and rewrite the stage comment to say that the base's `curl-minimal` is the healthcheck's curl. That also drops an install the `FROM` digest never pinned. (2) Move the `runtime-native` stage above `runtime-jvm`, so a bare `docker build .` builds what `container.yml` publishes. Every `runtime-*` guard iterates stages by name and ignores order (measured: the `STAGE` loops in the five Dockerfile guards). `validate_image_pins.py`'s earlier-stage rule still holds, because `backend-build-native` stays above. (3) So that (2) cannot drift, add to `validate_images_run_unprivileged.py`: each `(dockerfile, target)` in `container.yml`'s matrix is that file's **last** stage (`admin-web/Dockerfile`'s `runtime-nginx` already is). Bite it by moving the stage back. **Done when** (implement step, Docker): `docker build --target runtime-native .` completes, and `docker run --rm --entrypoint curl <img> --version` works. **Unmeasured and load-bearing:** BuildKit runs stages concurrently, so the failure at `:166` says nothing about whether the Mandrel compile (`backend-build-native`) has ever finished. If it fails, file that as its own row, and let the owner decide whether the native target is kept at all. **Original row:** **inbox.** Found 2026-09-28 by the coordinating session: a plain `docker build .` builds the last stage, `runtime-native`, and it failed at `RUN microdnf install -y curl && microdnf clean all` (`Dockerfile:166`): *package curl-minimal-7.76.1-31.el9 from @System conflicts with curl provided by curl-7.76.1-40.el9_8.7 from ubi-9-baseos-rpms* (measured on aarch64, `ubi9/ubi-minimal:9.5` at its pinned digest). The release workflow builds `--target runtime-jvm` (`.github/workflows/container.yml:160`), so no shipped image is affected, but the native target — kept so the two targets cannot drift — is broken, and a bare `docker build .` (as `cr-runtime-hardening-both-targets`' Open section writes it) builds it. Likely fix, *not measured*: `curl-minimal` already provides `/usr/bin/curl`, so the install can go (or `microdnf swap`); check what the healthcheck needs. Also decide whether a bare `docker build .` should default to the JVM target. |

| 196 | **Cutting a removed group member off an existing share link means re-issuing the link** | sharing, security, product | **done** → [ADR 0075](decisions/0075-a-link-a-departed-member-could-copy-is-reissued.md) (`cr-reissue-share-link`): `POST /api/shares/{id}/reissue` makes a new id + fresh `K_share` carrying the old settings and turns the old link off in one transaction; re-activation answers `409 reissue_required` when the share's owner has left its scope or its recovery copy predates the current epoch; a group removal can opt to turn off every link of the group (`revokeLinks`); the manage panel marks the epoch case and offers **renew**. Honest-server only — a dump holder still reads the Memory under `K_node` (ADR 0012). Measured at refine: the row understates it — shares exist in group and tenant scopes (ADR 0029 §6), every member's manage panel rebuilds and offers a copy of *every* link on a Memory (not only the ones they opened), a copied link works with **no dump** because `K_node` never re-keys, and re-activating a removed member's auto-revoked share hands them their own link back working (a live defect; the contract says the fragment is unchanged). The CR: a one-transaction **re-issue** op (new id + new `K_share`, settings carried, old one off), re-activation refused (`409 reissue_required`) for a link a departed member may hold, and an opt-in "turn off every link" on a group removal. Original finding: found 2026-09-28 by `cr-share-key-recovery-for-members-only` (the owner settled the residual precisely): a share's `K_share` never changes for the life of its link, so a member removed from a group scope who ever opened a share keeps its `K_share` — with a dump they keep reading that share, including later edits of its password, label or note, and can still use its link. Re-sealing the recovery copy under the new epoch on rotation would not help (they already hold `K_share`); shares minted after the removal are out of their reach. Stated in [ADR 0036](decisions/0036-editable-share-note-recoverable-share-key.md) point 4 and security-model § What stays open. The product question: should a removal offer — or perform — a **re-issue** of the Memory's existing links (a new `K_share`, a new link to redistribute, the old one revoked), and who is asked. Revocation stays independent of rotation today (security-model § Sharing). |

| 223 | **The setup, share-create and upload-complete losers get 500 instead of their named conflict** | backend, errors, DONE | **done 2026-10-02** (`cr-create-race-losers-answer-their-conflict`, deleted). The loser of a concurrent create now answers what a replay of its request gets, never a 500 ([developer-styleguide §12](crosscutting/developer-styleguide.md), the insert half). Nine of its ten sites go through `shared/persistence/createOrConflict`, which owns the transaction and, on a persistence failure, re-runs the handler's own "taken?" checks in their order in a fresh read: first-run setup, share and guest identity create, personal and group scope, node (slug, then id), sub-bucket, blob-upload start, and the username on rename, accept and register-and-accept. A failure those checks do not explain stays a 500 (`SetupServerSliceTest.aCommitFailureTheReplayQuestionDoesNotExplainStaysAServerError`). Upload complete spends its sessions first (`MediaStore.attach`), so a batch whose second item loses still answers its first. Race tests, 5 rounds each, red first with a 500 at every site, and bitten by an injected re-throw. Blob start in one tenant was already ordered by the quota's tenant lock (green before; 10 rounds), and the race it left was two tenants naming one blob id. Measured differences from the refine: a duplicate personal create's replay answers `personal_scope_exists`, and a duplicate node create answers `slug_taken`. **Original row:** Left by the #199 refine: a constraint violation kills the second writer, which surfaces as 500 rather than the contract's conflict answer. |
| 224 | **Entities that are both bulk-updated on `status` and saved as managed entities** | backend, correctness, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (`cr-managed-writes-flush-only-what-they-change`, deleted). Every entity carries `@DynamicUpdate`, so a managed write flushes only the columns it changed; `DynamicUpdateEverywhereTest` reads Hibernate's mapping and names any entity without it ([developer-styleguide §12](crosscutting/developer-styleguide.md)). Red before, 5 of 5, green after: a terms edit or a quota change lifted a tenant suspension, a Memory rename wiped the first UPLOADER link's drop key, a consent toggle rolled back a password change's auth key, a role change undid a group invite's accept, a rename rolled back a Memory's media revision, and a media meta edit overlapping a sub-bucket delete failed its commit on the foreign key (500). Already closed before the CR ran: the scope epoch (`ScopeEntity` was `@DynamicUpdate` since `cr-tenant-removal-share-links`) and a share edit overlapping its owner's removal (the share write's row lock, `cr-share-link-delete`). The same-column races: a rename or icon set checks the epoch in its own update (#275), a re-activation runs its checks under the scope lock every removal holds, and a move into a sub-bucket and the bucket's delete both lock the bucket first. |

| 227 | **A share id stored raw lets a database copy add uploads to contributor and uploader links** | security, sharing, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-02 (`cr-guest-write-proves-the-link-key`, deleted).** Naming yourself on a link proves you hold its key: the client derives `writeToken = HKDF(K_share, "veyq/v1/share-write:" + shareId)` (`v1.shareWriteToken`, vector `share_write_token`), every create and re-issue stores only `SHA-256(writeToken)` (`V64__share_write_verifier.sql`, every role), and `createShareIdentity` checks `X-Share-Write` after the password gate, before the role check — missing, malformed, wrong or a share with no verifier is the non-enumerable `404` (an id-only request answered `201`, measured red first). Owner accepted 2026-10-02: a malformed `X-Share-Write` value answers the bean-validation 400 (before any lookup, the same for every id) rather than 404. |

| 231 | **`io.quarkus:quarkus-junit5` is relocated to `quarkus-junit`** | backend, dependencies, NEW | **done 2026-10-01** — the exact change below, metadata pruned by hand (two components, 10 lines). Proof, measured: a cold `GRADLE_USER_HOME` `./gradlew help check quarkusBuild pitest` without `--write-verification-metadata` → `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 23m 18s` (PIT 77/81 killed, 95 %), and the cold cache holds no `quarkus-junit5` or `quarkus-relocations-parent` at all; then `quarkusDev` once in the worktree on that home → the dev and test app models resolved and the app launched, failing only on the deliberately unreachable throwaway database it was pointed at (no dev data touched). **Refined 2026-10-01: a direct fix, no CR.** Measured (`7a34c57`, local Gradle cache): `quarkus-junit5-3.36.0.pom` is a pure `<relocation>` to `io.quarkus:quarkus-junit:${project.version}` with the message *"Update the artifactId in your project build file"* (Migration Guide 3.31). It is the **only** relocation POM among the cached Quarkus 3.36.0 artifacts, and nothing but the two BOMs and `quarkus-relocations-parent` names it — no dependency pulls it in transitively. `quarkus-junit-3.36.0.jar` keeps the package: `io.quarkus.test.junit.QuarkusTest`, `TestProfile`, `QuarkusTestProfile` are all in it (`unzip -l`), so the 148 test imports of `io.quarkus.test.junit.*` stay untouched. **Exact change:** `backend/build.gradle.kts:46` `testImplementation("io.quarkus:quarkus-junit5")` → `("io.quarkus:quarkus-junit")`; the comment at `backend/src/test/resources/application-test.properties` names `quarkus-junit5` → `quarkus-junit` (`git grep -n "quarkus-junit5" -- ':!docs/inbox.md'` lists these two plus the metadata). The `pitest-junit5-plugin` is PIT's own and unrelated. **Verification metadata: no regeneration.** Gradle followed the relocation, so `quarkus-junit` 3.36.0's jar and POM are already verified (`grep -n '"quarkus-junit"' backend/gradle/verification-metadata.xml`); the change fetches nothing new and leaves two components unreferenced — `quarkus-junit5` (POM) and `quarkus-relocations-parent` (POM). Delete both by hand: a write does not prune unused entries (remembered from Gradle's docs, not measured here), and `validate_build_input_pins.py` has no unused-entry rule, so nothing else would. **Proof:** `GRADLE_USER_HOME=$(mktemp -d) ./gradlew help check quarkusBuild pitest` **without** `--write-verification-metadata` — green from a cold home under strict verification proves both that nothing removed is still fetched and that nothing unverified is; then start `quarkusDev` once against the edited metadata, since `backend/CLAUDE.md` says the four tasks do not cover its bootstrap resolver (whether that resolver reads test dependencies is remembered, not measured — so run it, do not reason it away); then the guard glob. **Original row:** Left by the #201/#213 refine: a Maven relocation — a small direct fix under the no-deprecated-dependencies rule. |

| 233 | **The granter lock: an account rotation breaks every grant the rotator signed** | security, crypto, rotation, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-02** (`cr-grant-verifies-under-its-signing-key`, deleted). Each grant row keeps its granter's identity key as of the write (`veyq_membership.granter_identity_public_key`, V63, backfilled from the granter's key at migration time) and both reads serve that copy, so an account rotation leaves every grant the rotator signed verifiable under each recipient's existing pin, and an owner it granted can complete the covering rotation; the recipient's pin still decides trust ([ADR 0028](decisions/0028-identity-authenticity-tofu-verification.md) § What is still open). The five grant writers are pinned by `scripts/validate_grant_writer_arity.py`, each with a slice leg. Accepting a changed key first runs one scope-rotation worker pass (`web/src/features/spaces/acceptChangedKey.ts`, both accept surfaces, uc.account.rotate-keys E9); what that pass cannot cover stays refused for the accepting member. Proven live by `scopeRotation.e2e`'s last leg, red against a jar that serves the current key. Grants whose granter rotated before V63 stay unverifiable (no key history exists). |

"Unused" means no configuration resolves the entry, and that is a fact about the resolved graph: the metadata holds 991 components (`grep -c '<component ' backend/gradle/verification-metadata.xml`) against ~24 coordinates the build scripts spell (two plugin ids plus 22 quoted coordinates, the rest BOM-managed or transitive), and the graph also runs through detached configurations (PIT, `quarkusDev`'s bootstrap resolver). Gradle's dependency-verification docs (fetched 2026-10-01) say the same of Gradle itself — it never removes an entry, since it cannot know upfront whether one will be used — and that their `--dry-run` cleanup misses configuration-time resolution. | 237 | **The published wiki claims what the product does not do, and some docs lag the code** | docs, wiki, honesty, NEW | **done 2026-10-01** — every claim measured against the code first, EN+DE corrected together. *is-my-data-safe*: contact keys **are** pinned on first use, a changed one is marked "Key changed" and blocks hand-over until reviewed (expected vs. current safety number); the proactive check (QR, "verified") is said to be unbuilt; the operator cannot take a single share offline (revoke is creator-only), only suspend a whole tenant; there is no report button; the server sees no file type (sealed MIME) and exact — not coarse — upload/change times, not the capture date; old data is not moved to newer locks. *account-keys*: one "Stay signed in" toggle (the password-only device wrap enrols by itself); no private-window detection; the keyless entry also offers the way back; no sessions list while locked; after a rotation every device, the rotating one too, needs password **and** Kit; mail is set up per space by its owner. *adding-device*: password **and** Kit (also `uc.account.onboard-device`), plus the real control names and the not-kept-signed-in case. *quantum*: content is AES-256-GCM, the hybrid KEM wraps keys; no per-item suite label; nothing upgrades old memories. *who-gets-in*: per-space mail, owner-only inviting. *running-your-instance*: the operator configures no SMTP (owns the key file), admin service + Helm named, rehearsal per CI run, whole-tenant suspend. | 238 | **The infra/README capture step reads the key (uid 10001, 0600) from the volume without naming root** | infra, backup, docs, DONE | **done 2026-10-01** (direct fix). § *An upgrade, step by step* step 2 now runs `capture` as `sudo -E` and `verify` as `sudo`, and says why (the key is uid 10001, `0600`, under Docker's data root) and what follows: what a root capture writes is root's, so `verify` and the encryption in § Capture run as root, or the operator `chown`s the capture and the key file to themselves first. | 252 | **Two concurrent re-issues of the same share both succeed** | security, sharing, backend | **done 2026-10-02** → [ADR 0075](decisions/0075-a-link-a-departed-member-could-copy-is-reissued.md) point 1, [uc.sharing.share-shared-scope](features/sharing/use-cases/uc.sharing.share-shared-scope.md) A4. A share is re-issued at most once: the re-issue spends the old share by a checked compare-and-set (its deletion where none is set yet, the transaction's first write; developer-styleguide §12), so the loser of two concurrent re-issues — and any later re-issue of a deleted share — is `409 share_deleted`, nothing written, a non-member still the `404`; the renew dialog re-reads the list. Measured before the fix: the share-link delete's row lock had already serialized the race (loser `404 share_not_found`, never two links). | 256 | **The admin nginx logs every request line (URI and query) to stdout, and no sweep reads it** | security, admin-web, wire-assertion, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix): **trimmed, not switched off and not swept.** Evidence for the choice: no admin flow puts a token in a URI — the SPA's three routes read no query, the Admin API's path parameters across the setup/auth/tenants/instance specs are opaque ids and its one query is the boolean `revokeLinks`, and credentials ride the Authorization header and the refresh cookie, neither in any log format. So the request log (the only record of who reached the highest-privilege origin) keeps its value, and only what nothing legitimate uses goes: `admin-web/nginx.conf.template` declares its own `log_format veyq` (method, path cut at the first `?` via a `map` over `$request_uri`, status, size, user agent, forwarded-for; no Referer) and a server-level `access_log /dev/stdout veyq` replacing the base image's `main`. Both deployment targets render that one template from the image (compose's tmpfs, the chart's emptyDir on `conf.d`), so one change answers both; no compose or chart file changes. **Proven by a container run, not by e2e** (the e2e stack has no admin container): a throwaway container from the local `veyq-admin:hsts` image (nginx 1.30.5) with each template bind-mounted, needles in a SPA query, an API query, a Referer and a path — HEAD's template: all four in the access log; this one: the path only. Residual, measured in the same run and written into the template and the wire-assertion concern: nginx's error log names the whole request line, query included, when the upstream fails (no nginx knob; that line is the container's one diagnostic), and a cluster ingress in front of `admin.domain` keeps its own log. |

| 262 | **Re-measure the purge-vs-refresh lock footprint on MariaDB with the family lock in place** | backend, performance, sessions, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03.** Substrate: throwaway `mariadb:11.4@sha256:70cc07…` container (`veyq-i8f-measure`, own port, anonymous volume, 2 GiB cap, removed afterwards), migrated V1–V67, seeded as `cr-session-purge-cost-on-mariadb` did (100 020 families, 4 016 000 refresh / 20 000 access / 8 000 challenge rows, 16 000 / 16 000 / 4 000 expired), driven by a scratch Python harness replaying SessionService's statements; REPEATABLE-READ, lock wait 50 s. **Footprint:** each step held open 2 s with one probe of each kind — only the access-token delete blocks a refresh (and a login); the refresh range delete (48 000 rows), the challenge delete (blocks only a challenge insert) and a family batch delete block no refresh; bite: a `FOR UPDATE` on the probed family row made the refresh wait the full 2 s. Live (#245's 8 runs, ~26 000 probes) no deadlock and no lock timeout; the slowest probes (98–187 ms, during the 4–9 s refresh delete and family pick) are contention, since the same steps held open block nothing. **`withDeadlockRetry`:** two refreshes of one token released together on near-empty tables, no retry — the pre-lock token-first order deadlocked 17 times in 1 600 requests (the probe sees the cycle), today's family-first order 0 in 1 600: the cycle it exists for is gone, so it can be retired. **But the same run found family-first revoking the family in 800 of 800 races:** rotate's `currentTokenOf` is a plain read, and under REPEATABLE READ it sees the snapshot the family-id query opened before the lock wait, so the loser never sees the winner's successor (H2 reads committed rows, so no test sees it). With that read made `FOR UPDATE`: 0 revokes, 0 deadlocks in 800. Retiring the retry is therefore proposed together with that fix (new row); the KDoc on `SessionRotationRetry` and a trap comment in `rotate` carry it. |
| 263 | **A share write's row lock (delete against a concurrent edit) has no test** | sharing, backend, tests, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix). `ShareDeleteRaceSliceTest` holds a delete's commit back (`whileUncommitted`) while an edit, and in a second test a re-activation, writes the same link: each must find no share, and the tombstone must keep a null `sealedNote` and `enabled = false`. Red first with `SharingStore.liveForUpdate`'s `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` removed: the edit answered `DONE` and the re-activation `true`, round 0 of each; green with it. Each test ends with a control on a link nobody deletes, which the write changes. `@DynamicUpdate` does not close this one, since the write's columns are exactly the ones the tombstone cleared; the store's KDoc now names the test. |
| 264 | **The bucketless media backfill in `listBuckets` may be dead code before V1** | media, backend, hygiene, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix) — proven dead, deleted. Read, not run against data: `MediaStore.attach` is the only `MediaEntity()` insert in `backend/src/main` and stamps `bucket_id` + `created_by`; media never change node; the one way a set `bucket_id` returns to null is the V35 foreign key's `ON DELETE SET NULL`, and both bucket deletes avoid it (`deleteBucketReparent` moves every medium, trashed ones included, before it drops the row; `NodeSubtreePurge` erases a node's media before its buckets). So only rows from before V35 could be bucketless, and V1 has none to carry (no instance's data was read for this). The loop is gone and `listBuckets` only reads. Two test fixtures had seeded guest media without a bucket and passed only through the backfill (`ShareGuestNamesSliceTest.theMembersStillReadBothNames`, `ShareUploaderSliceTest.everyReaderOfTheMemoryIsServedWhatOpensAnUpload`, both red on the deletion); they now seed the primary bucket as `attach` does. Bitten: with `attach`'s `bucketId` stamp removed, `MediaBucketsSliceTest.uploadStampsOwnerKeyAndMaterializesPrimary` goes red, which the backfill could have masked. `moveMedia`'s `ownerKeyOf` keeps its pre-bucket fallbacks (`shareIdentityId`, then the scope owner); with `created_by` always set they are dead too, left for their own row. |
| 265 | **A suite that drives `token/refresh` and pairing so the wire sweep sees token rotation** | e2e, security, wire-assertion, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03.** `web/src/features/account/pairing/pairing.e2e.test.ts` carries the refresh cookie by hand: a per-device jar around `fetch` (outside the wire recorder, so the `Cookie` it adds is recorded) keeps each `Set-Cookie` and replays it under its `Path`, so the product's own `refreshSession()` runs unchanged. Test 1: the owner's cookie rotates twice and each successor is the next credential. Test 2, *add a device* end to end: open → answer → collect + check number → delegate → seal → collect → `openGrant` (the trusted device's Master Key) → adopt (a cookie, no body token, a new family) → refresh; the grant token replayed is `401 refresh_rejected` and the adopted family is dead, while the granting family still rotates (the control). Stops before `runOnboardTail`'s unlock (IndexedDB). Canary: the device label, sealed only in the grant. Measured on its own run: 2 passed; 15 tokens (`Set-Cookie ×6`, `accessToken ×6`, `bootstrapSecret`, `handle`, `refreshToken` ×1 each) raw nowhere, **hash control met for all 15** — every rotated refresh token and the handle by its own SHA-256. Bitten: logging the rotated refresh token in `SessionService.refresh` and the handle in `PairingHandler.open` turned the log sweep red with 6 hits — 5 `token:Set-Cookie`, 1 `token:handle` (restored by snapshot, jar rebuilt). Cited as `e2e` under `uc.account.login` refresh-rotates / refresh-reuse-detection and `uc.account.onboard-device`'s two session criteria; the wire-assertion concern's "no suite drives…" limit now names this suite. |

| 272 | **Two co-owners demoting each other can leave a group ownerless** | security, spaces, correctness, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (`cr-owners-and-personal-scope-hold-under-overlap`, deleted). | 277 | **An UPLOADER share-create loser answers `drop_key_exists`, not `id_taken`** | sharing, backend, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix): **`id_taken`**, named by running the replay. Measured red first on H2: a double-submitted first UPLOADER link of a Memory answered `[201, 409 drop_key_exists]` in round 0 — the loser's guarded drop-key update waits for the winner's and finds the key set — while the same body sent again afterwards answers `409 id_taken`, because the store checks the id first. `drop_key_exists` was the wrong name: the web client answers it by re-sealing and retrying the id the winner holds. `SharingHandler.create` now re-asks the replay question on a `drop_key_exists` refusal, in a fresh transaction (MariaDB's REPEATABLE READ would keep the loser's own on its pre-winner snapshot); the contract's `409` says so. Two different links racing for the first drop key keep `drop_key_exists` (their replay answers it too) — `ShareCreateRaceSliceTest` pins both cases, 5 rounds each, and an over-corrected re-ask that always answers `id_taken` turns the second red. |
| 278 | **Two personal-scope creates with different ids can create two personal scopes** | security, spaces, correctness, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (same CR). V67 adds `veyq_scope.personal_owner` (null except on PERSONAL) and `ux_scope_personal_owner (tenant_id, personal_owner)`. `personalScopeExists` reads that key, so the loser's `createOrConflict` replay answers `409 personal_scope_exists`. The backfill copies each PERSONAL scope's OWNER. On an instance that already holds a duplicate, one scope per pair keeps the key and the others keep their rows with a null key, so the index builds and nothing is deleted. That was proven once on H2 in MariaDB mode with seeded duplicates; MariaDB itself was not run. |

| 284 | **The cover editor decides whether to delete the old uploaded cover from its stale tree copy** | memory, media, correctness, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix). Both Dockerfiles carry a comment at the line; no build instruction changed, so a static proof (no image build run). Measured: untouched tree green (2 covered `npm ci`); bitten on copies — `test` dropped from `build`'s `needs` → red naming both Dockerfiles, `if: false` on the gate job → same, `RUN npm install` (shell and exec form) → red, the lockfile dropped from the `COPY` → red. | 296 | **A node created or moved under a parent being trashed escapes the trash; a subtree restored during its purge is erased** | content, backend, correctness, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix). The scope row is the node tree's serializer (`NodesStore.lockScope`): a create re-checks its parent under it inside its `createOrConflict` transaction, `applyUpdate` takes it first and re-decides a move's parent and cycle under it (every update, so a rename's slug also never lands on a node a trash just stamped), and `deleteSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` take it before they enumerate. `NodeSubtreePurge` erases per scope, one transaction each (a failure logged and stepped over, `ScopePurge`'s shape), selecting what is due under the scope's lock. `NodeTreeRaceSliceTest` (create, move, restore-during-purge, restore-of-a-purged-subtree) was red first on H2 and on `mariadb:11.4` (`run_backend_races_on_mariadb.py`, 4/4 red unfixed, 4/4 green fixed); each of the five locks bitten alone on H2 reddens its named test(s). | 297 | **A sign-in, a new device wrap or a pairing can survive the password reset, change or key rotation it raced** | account, security, backend, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03.** The account row is the serializing parent (`AccountStore.lock`). Writers: `LoginHandler.verify` reads the account by email `FOR UPDATE` (so it verifies against the key a reset/change installed); `KeyMaterialHandler.addWrap` and `PairingHandler.delegate` lock it and re-read the caller's session under it (`SessionService.isLiveSessionOf`, a revoked one → `401 authentication_failed`, already in the contract; delegate now returns `DelegateOutcome`). Cleanups: `RecoveryStore.applyReset`, `PasswordChangeHandler.change` and `RotationHandler.rotate` take the lock first; change and rotation also re-read their own session there (closes two more members of the class: a change attested by a password another change just replaced, and a rotation by a session a change just revoked). New `CredentialResetRaceSliceTest` (5 methods, 3 writers × 3 cleanups held via `whileUncommitted`, both orders, plus change/rotation vs cleanup). Red first: H2 4 of 4 then-existing methods red (sign-in, wrap, delegation all survived a held reset; a reset overlapping a held sign-in left its session); MariaDB 11.4 3 of 4 red (the held-writer order is green there only because InnoDB's FK check S-locks the account row). Green after: H2 5/5, MariaDB 5/5. Bites (H2): login lock, wrap lock, delegate lock, change lock — all 5 methods red together; wrap re-check alone → wrap red (`201`, wrap outlived the reset); rotation lock alone → rotation by a revoked session landed (`200`). **The reset-side lock has no red case**: its floor update flushes its account-row write before it enumerates, which already queues it behind a writer's lock — kept to make that order explicit instead of resting on Hibernate's auto-flush. Coverage: `nothing-outlives-the-{reset,change,rotation}-it-raced`; styleguide §12 names the rule. |

At READ COMMITTED with the plain read: 26/26 green, so ADR 0078 alone closes it. Decided **yes** to reading `currentTokenOf` `FOR UPDATE` anyway: the decision then holds at any isolation instead of resting on one connection setting, and it takes no lock the path would not take (the grace writes that row) — bitten at REPEATABLE READ: 26/26 green with it. Measured on the final code: 400/400 held same-token races both 200, 0 revokes, 0 failed refreshes. `withDeadlockRetry` retired (`SessionRotationRetry` + its test deleted; `refresh` is `@Transactional`); the fork race now also asserts every answer is 200/401, and a reversed lock order (token row, then family) reds it on H2 (`[200, 500]` in round 4). Running H2 under REPEATABLE READ dropped as moot: production is READ COMMITTED now (ADR 0078). Coverage `refresh-deadlock-is-retried` replaced by `refresh-concurrency-decided-on-the-family`; ADR 0004 updated. |
| 303 | **A VIEWER link that becomes CONTRIBUTOR only shows "takes no new guests" afterwards** | web, sharing, ux, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (contract first; no new server state). The fact was missing: `newGuestsRefused` is false on every VIEWER row by design, so the client could not know. `ShareSummary.writeVerifierMissing` (`x-veyq-visibility: flag`, required) is `writeVerifier == null` for any role; ADR 0076 point 5 says why it exists beside `newGuestsRefused`. The edit dialog shows "made before guests had to prove …, renew it first" (EN+DE) with a **Renew link** button whenever the share lacks a verifier and the picked role is CONTRIBUTOR — on switching, or on opening an existing such CONTRIBUTOR link; the button hands over to the list's own renew dialog (remounted by key). Why a renew and not a verifier on the edit: ADR 0076 point 2 — an edit never takes one. The renew keeps the VIEWER role (the server takes it from the old row), so the switch is a second step on the new link, and the warning says so. Measured red first: `ShareWriteProofSliceTest.theManageListSaysWhichLinksHoldNoVerifierWhateverTheirRole` (every value `null`), three dialog tests red before the template change. Bites: the server serving `newGuestsRefused` in its place (VIEWER row false) → backend red; the dialog's role half dropped → warning shown before the switch, red; its verifier half dropped → the control red; `listSharesFor` not threading the flag → `share.test.ts` red; the parent's `@reissue` unwired → `ShareDialog.test.ts` red. Not bitten: the remount key (the child is stubbed there). |
| 304 | **A storage-quota update without `quotaBytes` clears the quota** | admin, contracts, backend, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (contract first). "No quota" is now its own operation: `DELETE /api/tenants/{tenantId}/storage-quota` (`clearTenantStorageQuota`, admin channel, Server-Owner, idempotent `204`/`404 tenant_not_found`), and `setTenantStorageQuota`'s `quotaBytes` is `[integer, "null"]` + `@NotNull @Min(0)`, read with `.validated()` — a missing or null quota is `400`. Why an operation and not an "unlimited" flag on the body: once Jackson bound it, absence and null are the same value, and a flag would need a cross-field rule in the handler that the spec cannot state. admin-web's facade keeps its `number \| null` signature and routes `null` to the clear call. `validate_request_bounds.py` rule 5 lost its exception mechanism (the one entry was this); backend/CLAUDE.md says where such a null goes instead. Measured red first: `TenantStorageQuotaSliceTest.anUpdateWithoutTheQuotaIs400AndKeepsIt` — `{}` answered **204** and cleared the quota; admin-web's facade test red (the clear call never made). Bites: dropping `@NotNull` from the contract → rule 5 red naming `TenantStorageQuotaUpdate.quotaBytes [presence]`, and the slice test red (`400` expected, `500` — the null reached `.validated()`). |
| 305 | **`moveMedia`'s owner-key fallbacks for pre-bucket media are dead** | media, backend, hygiene, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix) — deleted, with its sibling in the media listing. `moveMedia` takes the owner from `created_by` alone (a medium without it fails as a bug), `scopeOwnerUserId`/`ownerKeyOf` are gone, and `toRow` no longer falls back to the guest identity. | 306 | **The deprecation gate never sees arm64-only npm packages** | ci, deps, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix). `scripts/npm_ci_no_deprecated.py` gains a lockfile half after the install: every `package-lock.json` entry carrying the `deprecated` mark npm copies from the manifest at lock time is refused (offline, every platform), and every locked entry this install skipped (no directory at its path — another platform's bindings) is asked of the registry with `npm view <name>@<version> deprecated` (8 in parallel, an alias asked by its real name); an unanswered query is red, never "not deprecated". Measured on this arm64 Mac: 54 skipped entries (the x64/other-platform esbuild, rolldown, lightningcss, emnapi bindings), ~16 s of queries; the full gate green ("…nor among the 54 locked package(s) this platform skipped"). Bitten by forging the lockfile in memory against the installed tree: a skipped `glob@10.5.0` → named (live registry), the same as an alias → named, a `deprecated` mark on an installed entry → named offline, a registry that answers nothing → all 54 unanswered, and a forged skipped entry with a fake "deprecated" answer → named; the untouched lockfile is the control. `validate_npm_installs_gated.py` drops the limit this closes. |
| 307 | **The 200 examples of `resendTenantInvite`/`reassignTenantInvite` omit required fields** | contracts, admin, guard, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix, examples only — no generated delta). Measured: the guard against the untouched contracts named **16** such examples across three specs — besides these two, `createTenant` 201, both mail-settings 200s (`configSecretState`), `previewTenantInvite` 200 (`role`), `acceptTenantInvite`'s request (`username`), `createPersonalScope`/`createGroup` requests (grant signature, admission), `listMyScopes`/`listScopeMembers`/`listMyGroupInvitations` items, and the share upload/complete request items — all fixed, green over 499 object examples.
