| 308 | **`deleteCoverBlob` does not carry the expected `blobId`** | memory, contracts, backend, web, concurrency, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix). The delete takes a required `blobId` query parameter (contract-first) and `MediaStore.clearCover` clears the slot with a compare-and-set as its first write (`… where cover_blob_id = ?`, developer-styleguide §12); a slot holding another blob answers `409 cover_replaced` and writes nothing, an empty slot stays an idempotent `204`. Web passes `setMemoryCover`'s `replaced` upload and treats `cover_replaced` as nothing to delete (any other failure still rejects). ADR 0077 point 3 and `uc.memory.cover` say so. Measured red first: `NodeCoverSliceTest.aDeleteNamingAReplacedCoverIsRefusedAndKeepsThePeersUpload` (409 expected, 204 got — the peer's upload deleted) and `aDeleteOverlappingAPeersCommitDoesNotTakeItsUpload` (`whileUncommitted`: the old delete died on `StaleStateException`); web: 3 red in `media.test`/`useMemoryCover.test`. Bitten: dropping the compare reddens both backend cases by name; moving it into a read before the write reddens the overlap case only; swallowing every 409 on the web reddens the `scope_deleting` control. Both cover cases green on MariaDB 11.4 (`run_backend_races_on_mariadb.py --tests '*NodeCoverSliceTest'`, 13/13). |
| 309 | **admin-web's S6 selector misses relative feature mocks** | admin-web, tests, lint, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix). S6 is now `veyq-admin/feature-mocks-bare-fn` (`admin-web/eslint/featureMocks.js`), a copy of web's rule — admin-web keeps its own copies of web's code by design (ADR 0065), as it already did for S1–S7 — resolving a relative mock path against the test file; the selector form is gone. Measured: the rule turned on named 10 exports in 2 mocks (`./tenants` in `CreateTenantDialog.test.ts` and `TenantDetailPanel.test.ts`; the other relative feature mocks, `./release` and `./storeProvisioning`, were already bare), and typing them through `vi.mocked(fn)` showed what S6 is for: the stubbed `TenantCreated`/`TenantInviteState` lacked `status`/`inviteStatus`, `inviteExpiresAt` and `delivery`, now built as typed fixtures. RuleTester suite `admin-web/eslint/featureMocks.test.mjs` (14 cases); bitten: dropping the relative-path branch reddens 4 of them by name. `npm run check` in admin-web green. |
| 310 | **`commitCover` deletes the old blob row it read before locking the node** | memory, backend, concurrency, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix). `MediaStore.commitCover` now reads the node under `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` as the transaction's first load of it, so it sees the cover a peer's commit or a `clearCover` just reclaimed (developer-styleguide §12, ADR 0078). The sweep found the same shape once more and fixed it: `SharingStore.deleteContributedMedia` read the guest's media plainly and then deleted it, so a second delete of the same upload (retry, double tap) answered 500. It now locks on read, as `purgeMedia` does, and the second delete gets the replay's 404. Measured red first on H2 and on MariaDB 11.4 (`run_backend_races_on_mariadb.py`). Three cases went red, all with Hibernate `Unexpected row count`: `NodeCoverSliceTest.aCommitOverlappingAPeersCommitReclaimsThePeersCover`, `aCommitOverlappingADeleteFindsTheSlotEmpty` and `ShareUploaderSliceTest.aDeleteOwnOverlappingItselfAnswersAsAReplay`. All three are green after the fix on both engines (15/15 and 13/13). Bitten on both engines by adding a plain read of the node before the lock. The persistence context then hands the lock back the stale copy. The commit-vs-commit case goes red by name: the peer's blob leaks (no 500, because the blob lookup after the wait finds the old row gone). The delete case stays green under that bite for the same reason, so only the unlocked original breaks it. **Left open, read not run:** (a) `MediaStore.trashMedia` and `moveMedia` read media rows plainly and then write them as managed entities. A guest's delete-own that lands in between makes that update find 0 rows, and the member's request answers 500. The fix is the same lock-on-read. (b) `NodeSubtreePurge.eraseExpired` selects its media without a lock. If `MediaTrashPurge` or an expedited `purgeMedia` erases one of them first, the whole erase rolls back. The next tick retries it, so nothing is lost, but this belongs with #296's purge rework. |
| 311 | **`i18n.test.ts › uses every key it carries` runs ~4.6 s against a 5 s limit** | web, tests, flaky, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix, made cheaper — no timeout). Where the time went (measured, phases instrumented in a throwaway copy): the scan already read each source once; matching all ~230 files costs ≤ 3 ms and the catalog walk ≤ 3 ms, while the reads took 85–1100 ms alone and grew under the suite's own load, because each `readFileSync` blocked until the next could start. The scan now lists the files (sync, cheap) and issues every read at once (`Promise.all` of `readFile`), so their waits overlap; the per-key `[...heads]` spread is hoisted. Measured with `vitest --reporter=verbose`, old and new alternated on one machine: alone median 119 → 64 ms; inside the full `vitest run` (the case that timed out) median 2370 → 260 ms, max 3696 → 462 ms over 3 rounds each. Bitten: an unused `sessions.zzBiteNeverUsed` in EN turns it red naming that key; restored, green. |
| 312 | **The npm gate queries the registry for skipped platform packages in every CI job** | ci, deps, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** (direct fix). One job suffices: the registry half's answer depends only on the lockfile, and every gated job installs the same one at the same commit. `npm_ci_no_deprecated.py` takes `--skip-registry-check` (stripped before `npm ci`; the offline half — the lockfile's own `deprecated` marks — still runs); `ci.yml` `frontend`/`web-e2e` and `mutation.yml` pass it, `container.yml` `test` is the one that asks. Why an opt-out and not an opt-in: a run without the flag — every local run, a new CI caller that forgets it — asks, so forgetting costs time, never coverage. Why `container.yml` `test`: its triggers are a superset of the others' (`main` + `release/**`, push and PR, the lockfile not path-ignored) and every image build `needs:` it — the arm64 image is where the skipped bindings are actually installed. `validate_npm_installs_gated.py` assertion 3 pins it: exactly one non-skipping gate call; its workflow fires on every push/PR branch the other gating workflows fire on and admits `package-lock.json`; every `npm ci` image builder `needs:` it (limit: branch patterns compared by spelling, paths by `fnmatch` against the one lockfile name). Measured: the registry half 9.2 s for 54 skipped entries on this arm64 Mac, 0.00 s skipped; the install through the gate with the flag green (npm never saw it). Bitten in throwaway trees, each red with its own message: a second asking job (`ci.yml` drops the flag), none asking, the designation moved to `ci.yml` (branches + image `needs:`), `container.yml` no longer on PRs to `main`, the lockfile path-ignored, the image build needing a non-asking gating job; control green. |
| 313 | **`trashMedia` and `moveMedia` read the media row unlocked, then write it** | media, backend, concurrency, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix). Both read the media row under its lock, as every delete of it does: a trash of a guest's upload the guest deleted in between now answers `404` instead of a `500` at its flush, and a move answers `false` (`403`, its replay's answer) — measured, the plain-read move did not fail but answered success for a photo already gone (it was in its primary, so nothing was dirty). `moveMedia` takes its locks bucket → media (sorted) → node, and `deleteBucketReparent` now locks its media before the primary's node lock, so a reparent, a move, a trash and a guest delete agree on one order (#301's node lock had put the reparent's node before its media; read, not run — no deadlock test). `MediaRowLockRaceSliceTest` (trash, move) was red first on H2 and on `mariadb:11.4` (2/2), green fixed; each read's lock bitten alone on H2 reddens its named test. |
| 314 | **`NodeSubtreePurge` selects its media unlocked** | content, media, backend, concurrency, V1, DONE | **done 2026-10-03** (direct fix). Not closed by #296: the scope lock orders the purge against node writes, not against a media purge or a guest's delete, which lock only the media row. The purge now selects the subtree's media `FOR UPDATE` (in id order), so a concurrent erase of one finishes first and the row is no longer selected. `NodeTreeRaceSliceTest.aPhotoPurgedWhileItsSubtreeIsErasedDoesNotRollTheEraseBack` (a trashed photo's `purgeMedia` held uncommitted while `purge.run()` erases its Memory) was red first on H2 and on `mariadb:11.4` — the erase rolled back and the nodes survived — and is green fixed; the lock removed is that red run. |
| 315 | **The web pairing flow on the new `401` from delegate** | web, account, pairing, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03** → [uc.account.onboard-device](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.onboard-device.md) E6. Measured first: only `AddDevicePanel.vue` (both arms) calls delegate; the new device never does. On the trusted device the `401` went through `withRefresh`, whose refresh is refused too (the family is revoked), so the session ending loaded `/login` — and the panel's `grantFailed` vanished with the old document: the person landed on a sign-in page that said nothing. Now the grant (delegate **and** seal) runs under `explainEnding('grantInterrupted', …)` (`app/endingNotice.ts`, the one home of the one-word sign-in notice, moved out of `signOut.ts`); a refused refresh hands that explanation to `endSession`, which leaves it only when it actually starts an ending; `LoginView` shows `pairing.grantInterrupted` (EN+DE: signed out while giving a device access, no device let in, the four causes, sign in and start over). The new device hears nothing (no refusal to relay, as in E2) and reaches E1's `gone` when the channel expires. Red first: 3 tests, 3/3 red with the fix removed (`endingNotice.test.ts` notice null, `AddDevicePanel.test.ts` explanation `[null, null]`, `LoginView.test.ts` copy absent). Bites, each alone: refresh passing no explanation → 1 red; `endSession` dropping the notice → 1 red; explanation never reset → 3 red; notice left before the has-a-session check → 1 red (the no-session control); panel grant unwrapped → 1 red. Coverage: `a-grant-interrupted-by-the-session-ending-says-so`. |
| 316 | **Sweep account email change and account deletion for the credential race** | account, backend, security, concurrency, V1, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-03 — the row's premise did not hold; the sweep found the class's one other member.** Neither path exists: `VeyqUser.email` is written only in `AccountStore.createAccount`, and nothing deletes a `veyq_user` row (`grep -rn "\.email = \|VeyqUser\.\(delete\|update\)" backend/src/main` — the only `email` writers are the confirmation/recovery/challenge token rows). Re-derived as "which cleanup that ends an account's other devices does not take the lock": **"sign out everywhere else"** (`SessionService.revokeOtherFamilies`, uc.account.sessions). A device it signs out could delegate a pairing while it ran, and the delegated family outlived the sign-out (serially: revoked with the rest, or refused). Fixed by taking `AccountStore.lock` first in `revokeOtherFamilies` (change and rotation already hold it when they call it). Red first: `CredentialResetRaceSliceTest` +2 methods (sign-out held vs delegation over HTTP; delegation held as its store calls vs sign-out) — H2 2/2 red (`200`, delegated family alive; held delegation's family alive), MariaDB 11.4 1/2 red (the held-delegation order is green there, as #297's held-writer order was). Green after: H2 7/7, MariaDB 7/7. The red run is the bite (one line removed). **Checked and already safe:** revoke-one-session and logout (a delegation completed just before them also survives — revoking a device never revokes what it paired — and the delegation's locked session re-read linearizes the rest); sign-in vs sign-out-everywhere-else (linearizable as sign-out then sign-in; the password still signs in); refresh/adopt vs every cleanup (family row lock first, #302); address confirmation and a member-invite `confirmAddress` racing a reset/change (`VeyqUser` is `@DynamicUpdate`, held by `DynamicUpdateEverywhereTest`, so the stamp cannot write back a replaced auth key; the address never changes); wrap delete vs anything (deletes one row by id); pairing seal vs rotation (rotation supersedes by id, the seal is a status CAS). Coverage: `nothing-outlives-the-sign-out-everywhere-else-it-raced`; styleguide §12 names it. |

| 318 | ~~**Five server timestamps are written and never read**~~ | security, backend, privacy, DONE | **done 2026-10-09** — the five were nineteen. **`accepted_at` restored by owner decision 2026-10-09: audit evidence** of when a member accepted which terms version, should a violation or dispute arise — back in the core baseline (pin updated), written once per version from the server's clock (a re-acceptance of the recorded version keeps the first time), served by no API — since #402 (2026-10-10) one row per version, so an older version's time survives a newer acceptance; `EveryStoredTimeHasAReaderTest` names its reader as operators/support reading the database (ADR 0035 §4, uc.account.accept-terms + coverage, security model follow). So eighteen stay dropped. Re-derived over both baselines (51 time columns) against every reader in `backend/{core,apps/*}/src/main` (getters, JPQL/native strings, mappers, resources, purges, jobs): **dropped — written, never read** (baseline + entity + writers, pins in the same commit): `veyq_server_setup.completed_at`, `veyq_user.created_at`, `veyq_refresh_token.created_at`, `veyq_access_token.created_at`, `veyq_tenant.created_at`, `veyq_scope.created_at`, `veyq_upload_session.created_at`, `veyq_blob.created_at`, `veyq_tenant_profile.created_at`, `veyq_scope_epoch.created_at`, `veyq_identity_pins.updated_at`, `veyq_job_decline.declined_at`, `veyq_share_identity.created_at`, `veyq_node_attachment.created_at`, `veyq_tenant_terms_acceptance.accepted_at`, `veyq_device_register.updated_at`; Memories `veyq_scope_favorites.updated_at`, `veyq_scope_item_favorites.updated_at`, `veyq_media_derivative.created_at`. No contract served any of them; no doc named a support need; `accepted_at` was the one judgement call — ADR 0035 §4 only *listed* it, the version is what the gate reads and what says which text was accepted, so it went (ADR 0035, uc.account.accept-terms + coverage corrected) — reversed the same day, see above. **Kept, each with its reader**, now held by the root test `EveryStoredTimeHasAReaderTest` (Hibernate's mapping of every `Instant` column against a `table.column → reader` map, both ways; bite-tested 2026-10-09: an unregistered `veyq_scope_favorites.updated_at` and a stale entry each turned it red): expiries (login challenge, both tokens, invite, share, recovery, pairing, confirmation, job lease, reclaim not-before/abandoned), purge/order creation times (recovery, pairing, confirmation, invite, job, reclaim, membership, bucket), served ones (session family created/last-used, node created, share created), trash times (scope, node, media) and the media cursor. **Kept on a narrower read, flagged in the map:** `veyq_user.email_confirmed_at` (only its presence is read; ADR 0074 Decision 1 decides a time — an ADR question, not a cleanup), `veyq_share.deleted_at` (only presence — a tombstone flag would do) and `veyq_media.created_at` (read only as the fallback of a never-null `updated_at`) — #398. Security model's inventory now says which times are stored and which are not. Every existing database (dev ones too) is recreated: the baselines changed. Was: Found by #291 (2026-10-03, measured: `grep -rn` over `backend/core/src/main/kotlin backend/apps/memories/src/main/kotlin` — each is only ever assigned `Instant.now()`): `veyq_tenant_terms_acceptance.accepted_at`, `veyq_scope_favorites.updated_at`, `veyq_scope_item_favorites.updated_at`, `veyq_device_register.updated_at`, `veyq_identity_pins.updated_at`. Each tells a database reader, to the second, when a member accepted terms, re-sealed their favorites, renamed a device or pinned a contact — the favorites ones time a *preference* (ADR 0068) at rest, not only in passing. Nothing sorts, expires or serves on them, so dropping them (a migration + the entity fields) is a real data-minimisation gain at almost no cost; the request time the server observes live stays. Decide per column whether an operator would need it for support. |
| 319 | **No deadlock test pins the bucket → media → node lock order** | media, backend, concurrency, tests, DONE | **done 2026-10-09** (test only; the order already held). `MediaLockOrderRaceSliceTest` holds one writer *between* its two locks (first taken and flushed, second not yet) and runs the pinned production writer inside that window — deterministic, not likely: a trash's media → node against `deleteBucketReparent`, and the reparent's bucket → media against `moveMedia` into that bucket. Bite: each writer's pair swapped (the reparent's media lock after the primary's node lock — #301's order —, the move's media before its target bucket) → both red in round 0 on H2 (`Deadlock detected`) and on `mariadb:11.4` (`Deadlock found when trying to get lock`); restored green. `MediaStore.ensurePrimaryBucket`'s KDoc names the test. |
| 320 | **A concurrent rename onto the same slug may answer 500, not 409** | content, backend, concurrency, DONE | **done 2026-10-09 — the premise did not hold; pinned.** Every writer of `slug_token` (create, identity folder, `applyUpdate`, trash, restore) takes the scope lock first, so the second of two renames onto one name reads the first's committed token under it and answers `slug_taken` (409). No `createOrConflict`-style mapping was added: a unique violation there could only come from a writer that skipped the lock, and that should stay the 500 a bug looks like (`createOrConflict`'s own rule). `NodeTreeRaceCoreSliceTest.aRenameOntoANameAConcurrentRenameIsTakingAnswersSlugTaken` (rename held uncommitted, the other runs through `NodesHandler.update`). Bite: `applyUpdate`'s `lockScope` removed → red round 0 on H2 and `mariadb:11.4` with exactly the feared failure (`ConstraintViolationException` on `ux_node_parent_slug` / `Duplicate entry`); restored green. |
| 321 | **`delegate`'s claim does not re-check pairing expiry** | account, pairing, backend, concurrency, DONE | **done 2026-10-09** (direct fix, two halves). Re-derived: the claim's missing TTL check alone was not the hole — a claim past the TTL leaves a family the next sweep still revokes. The hole was the sweep's **unlocked read**: with a delegation's claim + bind uncommitted, `PairingStore.expireOverdue` read the channel without a family, waited at its update, flipped it EXPIRED and revoked nothing. Now (1) `expireOverdue` selects under `PESSIMISTIC_WRITE` (`order by id`), so it waits for the delegation and reads the family it bound; (2) `claimDelegation` also requires `expiresAt > now`, so a channel that expired between the handler's liveness read and the claim is refused like a flipped one. `PairingPurge.sweep(now)` split out of `run()` so a test can sweep at a clock just past the TTL. `PairingExpiryRaceSliceTest` (race: sweep overlapping a held delegation → family REVOKED; slice: claim past the TTL takes nothing, control inside it). Bite: each half removed → its test red on H2 and `mariadb:11.4` (family `ACTIVE`; claim `true`); restored green. |
| 322 | **Any other refused refresh lands on sign-in with no message** | web, account, ux, DONE | **done 2026-10-09** → [uc.account.sign-out](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.sign-out.md) E1/E3 + criterion `ending-says-why`. `app/endingNotice.ts` gained two words: `refused` — the default of a refused refresh's ending outside any `explainEnding` step (the server's plain 401 does not say why, so `login.sessionEnded` names the causes: expired, or a sign-out on another device, a password change or reset, replaced keys) — and `otherTab`, left by `sessionEnd.ts` when another tab's announcement ends this one (`login.sessionEndedOtherTab`); en+de, shown as the sign-in card's warning. The sign-out sequence runs its logout under `explainEnding(null, …)`, so its own refusal stays silent. Only a tab that held a session leaves either word: nothing about any account. Proof: `endingNotice.test.ts`, `signOut.refused.test.ts` (real refresh + ending), `sessionEnd.test.ts`, `LoginView.test.ts`; bites: default back to null → 2 red, no `null` around the logout → 1 red, no `otherTab` → 2 red, no LoginView mapping → 1 red. | 323 | **Short canaries collide with random base64 in the statement-log sweep** | e2e, wire-assertion, tests, flaky, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-10 — the floor sits in the recorder and refuses; the sweep is unchanged.** `recordCanaries` (`web/src/test/canaries.ts`) throws, recording nothing of the call, on a leaf shorter than `MIN_CANARY_LENGTH` = **10** that is spelled only in `[A-Za-z0-9+/=_-]` (a leaf with any other character — a space, `.`, an umlaut — cannot occur inside base64 or hex and is exempt). 10, not the proposed 12: at 64^-10 a chance hit is under 10^-6 per run even for 10^9 swept characters and a thousand canaries, and an ISO day (`2026-05-01`, user plaintext with no longer spelling) is exactly 10. The two observed hits were not suite literals but an envelope's **closed enum values** (`city` = `nameAnchor.level`, `auto` = `derivedMode`), which no test data can lengthen: they are now left out of the canary set as `ENVELOPE_VOCABULARY` (date modes, `nameSource`, `derivedMode`, the geocode levels, `MemoryCoverSource.kind`, `capturedAtSource`) by the argument that already keeps object keys out — fixed by source, never user plaintext; a new enum value is refused loudly until listed. Residual, stated in `cc.security.wire-assertion`: an enum value leaking *alone* is unsensed; a leaking envelope still carries its name and day. Measured by an Explore pass over all 21 call sites: only `memoryCover.e2e` and `memoryLocation.e2e` recorded a refused leaf — the enum values above plus the 9-letter `Teneriffa`, now `Teneriffa Süd` in both. Bite-tested: `canaries.test.ts` (5 tests) goes red with the refusal disabled (1 failed) and with the floor lowered to 9 (3 failed). Not run here: the e2e suites (they typecheck in `npm run check`); with `VEYQ_GEOCODER` set, `memoryLocation.e2e` would now be refused on a geocoded name under 10 letters (the orchestrator leaves it unset). |

| 334 | **Turn on the Gradle configuration cache for the backend?** | backend, build, NEW | **closed — cache left off, `org.gradle.parallel=true` ON in `backend/gradle.properties` (2026-10-11, owner approved a determinism run: `check --rerun-tasks` plain 457 s, parallel 378 s and 335 s on a loaded machine, the same 1406 tests and 0 failures in all three)**; the measurement below stands (measured 2026-10-11 on `main` at `c9a179e9`, Gradle 9.5.1, Quarkus 3.36.0; a loaded machine, load average 8–15). **The saving the 2026-10-05 measurement credited to the cache is not the cache's.** `./gradlew cleanTest check` (1400 tests: `:core` 894, `:apps:memories` 372 + 2 skipped, `:apps:files` 43, `:test` 91, all green in every run): plain **6m29s**, `--parallel` **4m14s**, `--configuration-cache` **4m13s** storing and **4m17s** reusing — every minute of it is parallel task execution, which the cache switches on implicitly and `org.gradle.parallel=true` gives without it. The cache's own saving is the configuration phase, warm daemon, 3 runs each: `help` 0.76–0.99s → 0.42–0.45s; an up-to-date `compileKotlin compileTestKotlin` 2.1s → 0.47–0.53s; `:core:test --tests '*TasksSliceTest' --rerun` 54–74s plain vs 49–61s reused, i.e. below the Quarkus boot's noise. **~0.4–1.6s an invocation does not pay for a second cache whose staleness has to be trusted**, and one task the owner runs daily does not take it: **`quarkusDev` is incompatible** — the Quarkus plugin marks it so (`QuarkusTask`'s `notCompatibleWithConfigurationCache`), so on a clean Gradle home `quarkusDev --dry-run --configuration-cache` discards the entry with 8 problems (`DefaultLegacyConfiguration`, `DefaultSourceSet` on every project's `quarkusDev`) and runs uncached; on this Mac's own Gradle home it **fails** instead (storing resolves `:quarkusDevRuntimeClasspathConfigurationDeployment` early, which needs a fetch the home's keystore breaks, `No target component for edge … quarkus-bom`), where the same dry run without the cache passes. **What is compatible, for whoever reopens this:** `check` (no problem stored), `quarkusBuild` (stored, then reused in 0.5s), `pitest` (on a clean home: stored and reused; on this Mac's home it fails to resolve `:core:pitest` with or without the cache, the keystore again), the `buildSrc` OpenAPI tasks, and `SpecSets`' configuration-time reads — bitten: a spec added in a stray `contracts/api/<dir>/` or nested in `contracts/api/memories/<dir>/` invalidated the entry (`directory '../contracts/api' has changed`) and the build refused it as it does uncached; an environment variable reaches a reused entry's test JVM (`VEYQ_TEST_DB_URL` at a dead port failed a reused `:core:test`), so `run_backend_races_on_mariadb.py` cannot silently run on H2. **What is left is a different question, not this one:** `org.gradle.parallel=true` (−35% on `check`) is open for its own decision; the module test JVMs already take free ports (`quarkus.http.test-port=0`), and the one shared fixed path is `:core`'s alone (`build/test-tenant-ingress-*` under the common working directory `backend/`), but one green parallel run is still no determinism proof. |

The cold-home metadata write added exactly the four POMs this row predicted (`apache-32`, `commons-codec-1.17.1`, `commons-parent-71`, `junit-bom-5.11.0-M2`, on `:core:quarkusProdRuntimeClasspathConfigurationDeployment`) — the mechanism still unexplained. One test added on purpose: `ScopeAccessWorldCoreSliceTest` (`:core`) builds the world with core alone. Was: `ScopeAccessWorld` (`:core`'s test fixtures) seeds tenants, users, memberships, proposals and the GROUP scopes in every gate state (`live`, `control`, `deleting`, `suspended`, `stranger`, `proposal`, `proposalOnly`) — core's — but also the content each probe's control needs: an upload session and a job (core's APIs) **and** a `MEMORY` node, a medium via `/media-batch`, a bucket and a share (Memories'; core alone refuses the kind). So the class compiles against `:core` yet only runs where Memories is part of the application; its four users are `ScopeAccessSliceTest`, `ClientMintedIdSliceTest`, `ReactivateShareCapabilitySliceTest` (`:apps:memories`) and the root's `OperatorBlindProbeTest` — none in `:core`. Shape: the core world keeps the tenant/user/scope/membership states and the two core-owned contents, and `Space` carries a `content` map beside them; a small `WorldContent` interface in the core fixtures (`seed(space, auth): Map<String, String>`), found as a CDI bean inside the application under test, lets an application contribute its own content; Memories adds `MemoriesWorldContent` (the `MEMORY` node, medium, bucket, share) with typed accessors (`space.mediaId`) so the probes stay readable. That needs `java-test-fixtures` on `:apps:memories`, consumed by the root's tests — measured to work 2026-10-05 (both fixtures jars are workspace modules of the root's and Memories' test models), but it changes `:core:quarkusGenerateAppModel`'s resolution so dependency verification then asked for four more POMs (`apache-32`, `commons-codec-1.17.1`, `commons-parent-71`, `junit-bom-5.11.0-M2`): the cold-home metadata write is part of the work, and the mechanism behind it is not understood. Gain: core's fixtures name no application, a second application contributes its content the same way and the gate probes cover its routes, and a `:core` test that expects Memories content fails to compile instead of at runtime. |

| 341 | **`veyq_node` still carries Memories columns, and the drop-key table is a core table** | backend, platform, migrations, DONE | **done 2026-10-10** (direct, both baselines changed with their pins — every existing database, dev ones included, is recreated: `docker compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml down -v`). Core's `veyq_node` names no Memories concept any more. **The cover** is the node's `COVER` attachment in core's `veyq_node_attachment`: `MediaStore.commitCover` takes the scope lock, reads the slot and hands it to `AttachmentStore.attach` as the expected blob (the contract's commit replaces unconditionally, so no conflict can occur under that lock), and `deleteCoverBlob` goes through the new `AttachmentStore.detach` (compare-and-delete on the expected blob under the scope lock; a non-live node answers empty, as before). No contract shape changed — only `media.openapi.yaml` descriptions; core's own cover erase (`COVER_BLOBS_PHASE`) and `SharingStore`'s cover arm are gone, `AttachmentNodeSubtreeEraser` and the attachment contributor serve it, and `validate_blob_reference_arity` pins three arms, all foreign-key backed (the FK-less cover column was the arm it was built for). `cover_byte_size` (write-only) is dropped. **The gallery counters** are Memories' `veyq_media_revision` (node_id PK, `fk_media_revision_node`, not cascading; erased by `MediaNodeSubtreeEraser` and `MediaScopeEraser`, both guards derived it); no row reads 0/0 and the first bump inserts under the node row's lock with the UPDATE asked again beneath it — **the lock order written down** at `MediaStore.bumpMediaRevision`: media → node (as before), and a bump now meets other gallery writers on the revision row instead of holding the node row to commit. **The drop key** is no table but the column `veyq_node.sealed_drop_private_key`, and it **stays core's**: core seals it in a `WRITE_ONLY` share (`SharingStore`), Files' drop box and Memories' uploader links both open through it; no core wording names Memories for it. Pinned by `NodeCoverSliceTest` (+ a guest reading the shared Memory's cover only), `MediaRevisionSliceTest` (first-bump race), `NodeTrashSliceTest`/`ScopeEraserOrderSliceTest` (rows gone, the FK refuses otherwise); seven bites red (commit without the slot read, detach without freeing, either eraser without the revision delete, the bump without its re-ask, the attachment contributor emptied, the listing reading 0/0), plus both erasure guards bitten. [ADR 0079](decisions/0079-veyq-is-a-platform-memories-is-its-first-application.md) Consequences holds the state. |

| 350 | **Slug function per node kind: containers keep today's slug, files get a stricter uniqueness; vectors for the folding** | web, core, applications, files, crypto, DONE | **done** (cr-files-application P1, 2026-10-07) → [ADR 0080](decisions/0080-every-node-carries-a-key-a-folders-key-behaves-like-a-memorys.md) § Decided with the owner: `KindDescriptor.slug` + `registry.slugFor`, `slugTokenFor` takes the node's kind at every write (member and guest), `fileNameSlug` beside `slugify`, both pinned by the `name_folding` vectors in `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json`; the guest pre-check folds each sibling by its own kind. The one part not built — a member-side sibling pre-check naming the colliding sibling — is split to #389. The original note: Today one `slugify` (`web/src/shared/naming.ts:21`) feeds both the URL path segment and the blinded uniqueness token. Add a registry hook per node kind: containers keep `slugify` (URL segment = uniqueness; a collision names the sibling), leaves use NFC plus simple lowercasing, punctuation kept (`Foto.JPG` = `foto.jpg`, `Day1.md` ≠ `Day1 md`; the stored name keeps its case; the folding is frozen into the token, so it gets conformance vectors); the ZIP export needs no case rule, only a sanitiser for the target system. Folders and files both collide case-insensitively. The client checks the decrypted siblings first and names the colliding sibling, the server's `slug_taken` is the race backstop. No slug library (the token freezes the folding; a table change would silently break uniqueness): keep our own function and add conformance vectors for the folding to `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json` (only the token has one today). Non-Latin names keep the hash fallback. Before Files, because the token freezes the folding. |

| 354 | **`ClaimRequest.capabilityTags` needs element bounds** | contracts, tasks, request-bounds, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-09.** Measured first: `claimTasks` with `["NOT-HEX…"]` answered 204 — any string was a tag. | 357 | **A restore brings descendants back without a valid slug token** | backend, trash, correctness, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-06 — a delete frees only its root's slug, and a restore leaves a descendant with a freed slug in the trash (owner decision 2026-10-06).** The corner: trash `C` alone (its name is freed under the live `F`, a sibling may take it), then trash `F` and restore it. `NodesStore.restoreSubtree` now skips every descendant holding a freed token (its own id) together with its subtree: it stays on `F`'s re-stamped marker, `listTrashRoots` lists it as a root again (its parent is live), it restores on its own through the ordinary client path (`slug_taken` while the name is held), and the one-marker purge still erases it whole. Pinned by `NodeTrashConsistencyCoreSliceTest.aDescendantWhoseNameWasFreedStaysInTheTrashWhenItsParentIsRestored` (with a wire- and DB-level "no live node holds token == id" check after every restore; red with the old clear-everything restore) and the restore halves of `restoringTheParentBringsBackTheWholeSubtreeOnItsOneMarker` and `NodeTrashSliceTest.deletingAroundAnAlreadyTrashedSubtreeMakesTheWholeThingAgeOutTogether`, changed deliberately; ADR 0061 Consequences, ADR 0080 Decision 8, `uc.memory.delete-node` step 6 and `en.memory.tree` follow. No contract change; the web (cr-node-key-hierarchy 5b.5): `SpaceTrashDialog` re-reads its list once the host's reloaded tree reaches it, so the left-behind descendant shows as a root (its name opens under the restored parent's key, which only the reloaded tree holds), pinned by `SpaceTrashDialog.test.ts` (red with the re-read before the tree reload). Earlier, the descendants: Re-verified at `NodesStore.deleteSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`. A delete now frees only its ROOT's slug (`root.slugToken = root.id`): every descendant's name lives under a trashed parent, and create, move and restore all refuse one (ADR 0080 Decision 8), so nothing can claim it and it keeps its token. Pinned by `NodeTrashConsistencyCoreSliceTest.aRestoredDescendantKeepsItsNameAndASiblingCannotTakeIt` (after the restore a sibling taking a descendant's name is `409 slug_taken`; red with the old free-every-node write: `201`); the leaf half of the former `aDescendantOfTwoDeletesKeepsItsTokenThroughBoth` now sits in the corner test above, which replaced it; ADR 0061 Decision 5 corrected in place, `uc.memory.delete-node` step 3 and `en.memory.tree` follow. Was: (medium; measured by the slice-4 agent of `cr-node-key-hierarchy`, 2026-10-06; predates ADR 0080, ADR 0061 Decision 5). `NodesStore.deleteSubtree` sets `slugToken = id` on EVERY node it trashes (to free the names), but `restoreSubtree` rewrites only the ROOT's token. Restored descendants therefore return with no valid slug: a same-named sibling can now exist, and a descendant whose name was taken meanwhile comes back as a duplicate. Fix options: release only the root's slug on delete (descendants keep theirs, nothing else can claim them while the root is trashed), or have restore re-derive every descendant's token under its live parent's key (needs the client). Decide, then pin with a test: delete a folder with a child, create a sibling with the child's name under the live parent, restore. |

| 359 | **A guest's own-media delete reads the share subtree without the scope lock** | backend, sharing, races, DONE | **done 2026-10-09 — took the scope lock (the safe side).** `MediaShareGuestWrites.deleteOwn` locks the scope row first, then the media row, then the node: the order a guest's complete (`ShareGuestGate.admittedInto`) and the trash purge already take, so no new lock order. A member's move of the medium's Memory out of the subtree has committed and is seen (`deleteOwn` → 404) or waits. Cost: a guest's delete waits for a tree write in its scope (one short transaction each, a guest delete is rare and single-row) — not measured, not expected to matter. `MediaRowLockRaceSliceTest.aGuestDeleteOfAnUploadMovedOutOfTheSubtreeMeanwhileAnswersNotFound` (move held uncommitted, delete inside; control: inside the subtree it deletes). Bite: the lock removed → red round 0 on H2 and `mariadb:11.4` (`Success(true)`: deleted after it left the subtree); restored green. Was: (low; slice 5a.5 of `cr-node-key-hierarchy`, 2026-10-07). `deleteOwn` now also requires the medium to lie in the share's subtree, read under the medium's row lock only, not the scope lock; a concurrent member move that takes the medium out of the subtree is neither guarded nor race-tested. Effect if it loses: the guest can delete its own medium for a moment after it left the subtree. Decide: take the scope lock like complete does (cost: serialises guest deletes with tree writes) or accept and pin the behaviour with a test. |
| 360 | **Every guest complete now takes the scope lock, also without a target node** | backend, sharing, performance, DONE | **done 2026-10-09 — left as is, by a structural measure (no timing run).** The many-guest case is guests of ONE link naming no node, so all complete into its root — and every such attach already locks that root's node row in the same transaction (`MediaStore.ensurePrimaryBucket` + `bumpMediaRevision`), so they run one after another with or without the scope lock: skipping it there buys them nothing. It would reorder nothing wrongly either (share → node, the pre-5a.5 shape, no inversion with a re-activation's scope → share), but what it would gain is only parallelism against tree writes and completes into other nodes of the scope; Files names its node on every complete, so takes the lock regardless. One lock order for every complete stays. The reasoning lives on `ShareGuestGate.admittedInto`'s KDoc. Was: (low until measured; slice 5a.5 of `cr-node-key-hierarchy`, 2026-10-07). `MediaStore.attach` takes scope lock -> `admitted()` -> target re-check for every guest `completeShareMedia`, so there is one lock order; guest completes and tree writes in the same scope now run one after another. Correct and simple, but it costs parallelism when many guests upload at once. Measure with a many-guest upload run before relaxing it (e.g. lock only when a `nodeId` is named). |

| 365 | **`slugify` turns a decomposed `ü` into `u`, not `ue`** | web, naming, crypto, DONE | **done 2026-10-09**: `slugify` normalises to NFC first and its hash fallback hashes that NFC form (a Hangul syllable and its jamo are one slug); `fileNameSlug` already did NFC before lowercasing and is unchanged. `name_folding` in `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json`: the one vector that pinned the bug now says `mueller`, six cases added (`MU`+U+0308`LLER`, `Ka`+U+0308`se`, `Scho`+U+0308`n`, composed and decomposed `한`, file `U`+U+0308`BER.txt`), every other vector byte-identical; all 34 re-derived by an independent Python (`unicodedata`) fold, 0 mismatches. Bite: dropping the NFC step reddens 6 cases (5 vectors + the unit test), hashing the raw name 2, dropping `fileNameSlug`'s NFC 3. Web mutation gate exit 0 (naming.ts 100 %). The original note: found by P1 of `cr-files-application`, 2026-10-07. `web/src/shared/naming.ts` `slugify` maps the German letters before it applies NFKD, so a name typed or pasted in decomposed form (`u` + U+0308, as macOS file names often are) folds to `u` while the composed form folds to `ue`; the vectors in `contracts/crypto/vectors/v1.json` (`name_folding`) pin the behaviour as built. Fix by normalising to NFC first (a stored-format change before the first release, ADR 0072: every folder slug token computed from decomposed input changes), re-generate the vectors from the independent Python implementation, and say in the vector note that the German table sees composed letters only. |
| 366 | **A member upload start accepts any role token and reserves quota for it** | backend, blobs, quota, DONE | **done 2026-10-08.** Core asks the applications through a new seam, `MemberBlobRoles` (`me.raddatz.veyq.core.blob`; Memories `MemoriesMemberBlobRoles` = its four roles, Files `FilesMemberBlobRoles` = `FILE_CONTENT`), and `BlobUploadHandler.start` refuses a request carrying a role none declares whole, `400 invalid_request`, past the access check and before any reservation. A **union, not a partition**: a member's start names no node, so core can only ask whether *some* application attaches the role; fails closed — core alone declares none and refuses every member start (`CoreAloneSliceTest`; `:core`'s own upload tests boot a test-only stand-in, `CoreTestMemberBlobRoles`, which `CoreAloneSliceTest` excludes). Left as accepted: a *known* role for an object that never takes it (a `FILE_CONTENT` session whose owner is no `FILE`) still opens and ages out — as any upload a member abandons, within the member's own tenant quota. → [ADR 0079](decisions/0079-veyq-is-a-platform-memories-is-its-first-application.md) Consequences (thirteen seams), [docs/apps/depot.md](apps/depot.md), `MemberBlobRoles` KDoc. |

| 368 | **`OperatorBlindProbeTest.API_PACKAGES` is a hand-written list of generated API packages** | backend, tests, operator-blind | **DONE 2026-10-08.** (Found by F1a of `cr-files-application`: the probe walked a fixed list — core, Memories, Files — so a new application's operations went unprobed until someone remembered it.) | 369 | **The 4-character canary `auto` (`memoryLocation.e2e.test.ts`) can match random ciphertext in the statement-log sweep** | web, e2e, wire-assertion | **superseded 2026-10-08 → #323** (a duplicate: same suite, same cause — #323 already names `auto` among the short canaries; this sighting and its fix proposal are carried there). Was: (low; seen once during the F3 merge of `cr-files-application`, 2026-10-08). The statement-log sweep reported 2 raw hits for `auto`, both inside base64 sealed bytes of an INSERT (`…WZWautoqvJLM…`); the identical suite passed on the immediate rerun (21/21). A canary that short collides with random base64 at a small but nonzero rate per run, so the gate is flaky by construction. Fix: use canaries of ≥ 12 characters (or add a minimum length to `recordCanaries`), bite-tested with a short one. |

| 387 | **Node id locality: `BINARY(16)` / UUIDv7 considered and rejected for ordering** | backend, database, ids, performance, MEASURED | **done** (2026-10-11, owner decision, pre-V1) — **every id column is now MariaDB's native `UUID`; outcome below, then the measurement and the recommendation it overrode.** Was: **inbox** (low; 2026-10-08, from #377(e)). Ordering siblings by a time-ordered client-minted id (UUIDv7) was rejected: a node id is client-minted (ADR 0072), so its timestamp is the client's clock — forgeable (a client picks its siblings' order) and leaking (every id would carry the client's own clock reading to whoever sees the id, a share's guests included, while `createdAt` is served to members only) — so siblings are ordered by the server-observed `createdAt` instead (ADR 0015). Ids stay random UUIDv4 in `VARCHAR(36)`. **Outcome 2026-10-11 (decision record).** The owner decided to take the pre-V1 window ('do it today'), against this row's own 'not worth it now': the move is cheap exactly once. **Why `UUID`, not `BINARY(16)`:** the two measured the same (above), but `BINARY(16)` needs an `AttributeConverter` on every id and FK attribute, `UNHEX` on native-query parameters, and makes ids unreadable in a SQL console and in logs; `UUID` keeps `String id` in every entity, prints the canonical lower-case form and takes it as a string. **What moved:** the 89 `VARCHAR(36)` columns in 33 tables (30 PKs, the FKs between them) in both baselines (edited in place with their pins in `scripts/applied_migrations.sha256`, `release: none`); **no entity changed** (`@Column(length = 36)` stays true of the canonical string). **Audited before the move** (writers traced in code and contracts): every column is written only from a server-minted `UUID.randomUUID()`, an authenticated principal (`identity.principal.name`, a user or a share-identity id), or a `format: uuid` contract field (client-minted ids, ADR 0072: node, blob, owner, media, bucket, scope, share identity, account, admission) — including the ones this row listed as untraced: `veyq_upload_session.owner_id` (the media or file-node id, a `uuid` field), `veyq_job.lease_holder` and `veyq_job_decline.worker_id` (the leasing user), `veyq_scope.personal_owner` (the owning user), `veyq_media.deleted_by`, `veyq_membership.added_by`/`granter_user_id`, `veyq_share.owner_account`, `veyq_device_pairing.delegated_family_id`. **Stayed `VARCHAR`:** `veyq_share.id` and the `share_id` that references it, `veyq_share_retired_id.id` (256-bit tokens, `VARCHAR(64)`), the synthetic keys `veyq_scope_favorites.id` (`userId|scopeId`, 73) and `veyq_scope_item_favorites.id` (`userId|scopeId|nodeId`, 110), `veyq_bucket.owner_key` (`user:{id}` / `share:{id}`), `slug_token`, every `token_hash`. **No column had to stay for a non-UUID value** — but three places held a non-UUID *sentinel* that the audit found and removed: `NodesStore.slugTaken` bound `''` as 'no exception' (`id <> ''`; now two statements), `StorageUsage.usageByTenant` read a native `SELECT t.id` as a `String` (a `byte[]` on H2, measured, not the `String` its callers read; now `CAST(… AS CHAR(36))`), and the tests' fixture ids (`login-test-user`, `tenant-owner-user`, `purge-fam-live` … — about 30 literals, all now UUIDs; `RemovalPolicyTest`'s never reached a database). **The boundary (fail closed, never a 500):** a non-UUID on `INSERT` is error 1292 and on `SELECT` a silent miss, so every id-named request input is `format: uuid` in the contract — the last three string-typed ones (`DELETE /api/sessions/{id}`, `DELETE /api/account/wraps/{id}`, `MasterKeyWrap.id`) were changed — which generates `java.util.UUID`: a bad body id is `400 invalid_request` (`MismatchedInputExceptionMapper`), a bad path segment is `404`, both before a handler or query. **Held by** `scripts/validate_id_contract_format.py` (every id-named path/query/header parameter and request-body property reachable from any operation is `format: uuid`, the two named exceptions `shareId`/`suiteId` with their reasons) and by `IdBoundarySliceTest` (a path id, a body id, a parent reference, a client-minted blob and owner id, an id in a list, the two formerly-204 operations). **Case:** the normalisation point is the generated `UUID`: it parses either case and every handler reads `toString()` (canonical lower-case), so an upper-case id is the same id — stored, compared and printed lower-case, and an upper-case duplicate is `409 id_taken` (`IdBoundarySliceTest.anUpperCaseIdIsTheSameIdInCanonicalForm`). **Test database:** H2 `MODE=MariaDB` takes the `UUID` DDL as it is (no compat form, one SQL for both engines) and is *stricter* than MariaDB — it fails the statement for a bad string where MariaDB warns on a `SELECT` — so a green H2 suite is the stronger boundary proof; the String-parameter path through Hibernate/JDBC on the real engine is `scripts/run_backend_races_on_mariadb.py`. **Order:** a `UUID` column orders by its own segments, not by its text — `order by id` is therefore an engine-internal order (fine for lock order and for a keyset cursor compared in SQL, e.g. `MediaStore`), never one a client may read as text-sorted: Files' two listings, documented 'ordered by node id', now sort the text in Kotlin. The same change exposed an arbitrary tie that the old column hid: `TasksStore.compromiseRotations` picked the 'newest' job by `created_at`, which is second-precision on MariaDB, so a dead-letter and its re-queue in one second tied (`RotationSliceTest`, 2 tests red on MariaDB only); the tie now goes to the live job, then the id. **Proof on the real engine:** the race runner with `--tests '*'` runs the **whole** suite (all four Gradle projects) on a throwaway `mariadb:11.4` — green after those two fixes, and it is what found them (H2 was green on both). **Dev-boot schema validation (follow-up 2026-10-11).** The first `quarkusDev` after the move logged `Failed to validate Schema: wrong column type … found [uuid (Types#OTHER)], but expecting [varchar(36)]` for all 89 columns: Quarkus' `%dev` validation (production is `none`) compared `@Column(length = 36)` — `varchar(36)` — with the native type. **Fix:** the 89 attributes are `@Column(columnDefinition = "uuid")` (the validator accepts a declared type name equal to the database's; `String id` stays, no converter; no DDL is generated, Flyway owns it, so the definition affects nothing else). Alternatives not taken: `@JdbcTypeCode(SqlTypes.UUID)` on a `String` (would make Hibernate bind/read a `java.util.UUID` for a `String` attribute — a conversion, the thing the owner ruled out). **Held by** `EntitySchemaValidationTest` (`:core`, `:apps:memories`): a `@TestProfile` with `schema-management.strategy=validate`, so the boot fails on any entity/column drift — on H2 in `./gradlew check` and on MariaDB in `scripts/run_backend_races_on_mariadb.py`'s default run (`schema_classes()`); bitten on both engines (one id back to `length = 36` → `wrong column type … [veyq_tenant]`). **Existing databases** cannot be migrated and are recreated (`docker compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml down -v`; stated in the baselines' header). The numbers above stand as the reference for the size/IO case (−32% size, +25…47% load, no point-read gain at today's scale). Measurement and the earlier recommendation follow. **Revisit only as a database-locality decision for all primary keys** (index locality and size of `BINARY(16)` and/or time-ordered keys). **Measured 2026-10-11** with [`scripts/bench/id_key_bench.py`](../scripts/bench/id_key_bench.py): the project's pinned `mariadb:11.4` (11.4.13, default `utf8mb4` / `utf8mb4_uca1400_ai_ci` — the baselines name no charset), `innodb_buffer_pool_size=128M` (MariaDB's default, and small enough that locality matters), a synthetic `veyq_node` + `veyq_node_attachment` with the baselines' columns, indexes and the attachment FK, random UUIDv4 keys inserted in random order, 1M nodes (+250k attachments) and 10M (+2.5M). Medians of three passes; the host was running a Gradle build (load average ~15), so read **±15%** as noise. **Inventory (both baselines):** 37 tables, 89 `VARCHAR(36)` id columns in 33 tables (30 of them a PK, 89 matching `@Column(length = 36)` in the entities), 36 of 37 FKs on them, 34 of 45 indexes contain one; not id-typed and untouched by any move: `veyq_share.id` (a 256-bit token), the synthetic PKs `userId|scopeId[|nodeId]` (`VARCHAR(73/110)`), `owner_key`, the blinded `slug_token`/`token_hash` values. **Numbers, `VARCHAR(36)` → native `UUID` (`BINARY(16)`, measured at 1M only, is within 1% on size and within 5% on load and 128M-pool reads — so one column; cached children 209 vs 150 µs, `UUID` ahead):** (1) *size* (node + attachment, data + indexes) 1M: 1 248 → 851 MB (−32%), 10M: 11 774 → 7 827 MB (−34%); the node table's secondary indexes alone 501 → 291 MB (−42%) at 1M and 4 589 → 2 467 MB (−46%) at 10M, the attachment table −47% / −50%; (2) *bulk load* (node + attachment rows/s, seven random-key B-trees) 1M: 3 168 → 4 646 (+47%), 10M: 1 923 → 2 397 (+25%); *insert into the loaded table* (1 000-row batches) 1M: 2 666 → 3 131 rows/s (+17%), 10M: 1 061 → 1 463 (+38%); (3) *reads*, µs per operation incl. ~9 µs loop overhead, pool 128M — 1M: point lookup 38.5 → 45.5 (**no gain**; cold after restart 49.4 → 44.2), the children of a parent (~50 rows via `ux_node_parent_slug` + clustered lookups) 1 880 → 1 627 (−13%), children + attachment join 250 → 158 (−37%); 10M: point 144 → 50 (−66%; cold 145 → 49), children 5 313 → 2 456 (−54%; cold 6 000 → 2 061), join 1 969 → 1 359 (−31%); 1M with everything cached (2G pool): point 11.9 → 8.5, children 330 → 150 (−55%), join 118 → 60 (−49%). **A third form:** `VARCHAR(36) CHARACTER SET ascii COLLATE ascii_bin` (no type change) is as large as `utf8mb4` (1 239 vs 1 248 MB) but loads at 4 458 rows/s and inserts at 2 954 rows/s (the `BINARY(16)` level) and reads the cached children in 226 µs (vs 330 / 150) — the comparison collation is the CPU half of the gap, the 36-byte width the I/O half; at the small pool it reads like `utf8mb4` (children 1 811, join 244). **Break-even:** the size saving is a constant third, the CPU saving is constant; the read saving that matters scales with data : pool — none on point lookups at 1M (data + indexes ≈ 1.2 GB per 1M nodes ≈ 10 : 1 to the 128M pool), −66% at 10M (≈ 90 : 1). Today's target (one person and their circle; no node count is recorded anywhere, I assume well under 1M) sits on the flat side: a 50-child listing costs 1.6 vs 1.9 ms. **Recommendation: not worth it now; if ever, only as native `UUID`, never `BINARY(16)`.** `BINARY(16)` measures the same as `UUID` but needs an `AttributeConverter` on every id and FK attribute of the 25 entities, `UNHEX` on the `:key` parameters of the three native queries (`StorageUsage`, `StorageQuota`, `MediaUploadAdmission`) and makes ids unreadable in a SQL console and in logs; `UUID` prints the canonical form and takes it as a string, so the entities stay `String id` (checked at the SQL level on the pinned image: canonical lowercase out, upper case accepted, a FK between two `UUID` columns works, `id = 'not-a-uuid'` matches nothing with warning 1292, an `INSERT` of one fails under strict mode with error 1292). Revisit when an instance is expected past ~2M nodes with a pool under a tenth of data + indexes (≈ 8M nodes at a 1G pool); the one reason to decide before then is the window: **before V1 the move is an edit of the two baselines** (89 columns, 37 FKs, two pin lines in `scripts/applied_migrations.sha256`, dev databases recreated with `docker compose down -v`), **after V1 an offline rebuild of 33 tables** (drop the FKs, `ALTER … MODIFY` every id column, re-add them; every row copied). **Cost of doing it now:** the baseline edit, an audit that every one of the 89 columns only ever holds a UUID (`owner_id`, `lease_holder`, `worker_id`, `personal_owner` were not traced), tests that mint non-UUID ids (found: `LoginSliceTest` `login-test-user`, `tenant-scope`; the search was a grep, not a proof), and a check of what the default H2 `MODE=MariaDB` test database does with a `UUID` column and what the MariaDB JDBC prepared-statement path does with a `String` parameter — neither was run here. **Not measured:** disk-cold reads (the Docker VM's page cache stays warm across a restart, so "cold" is buffer-pool-cold only), a mixed concurrent workload, replication and backup size, `BINARY(16)` at 10M, the 30 smaller tables, and anything through Hibernate. |

Audit of tokens + PrimeVue's Aura defaults, light and dark: **36 pairs below AA**, all in web's tokens or the preset, none in a component's own literal. Fix in `styles/base.css` only as tokens (`--app-primary`/`-hover`/`-active`, `--app-on-fill`, `--app-control-line`, both twins) plus a `--p-*` block routing PrimeVue's primary, danger/warn buttons, messages, toasts and form boundaries onto `--app-*` roles; ten fill sites moved from `--app-on-strong` to `--app-on-fill`. **Primary: emerald-700 under white, not dark text on emerald-500** — the primary colour is also text (text/outlined buttons, terms links) and a control fill (checkbox, switch), where `#10b981` fails on white whatever its label is; darkening it fixes all three and keeps the hue; dark mode keeps Aura's emerald-400. Measured with `getComputedStyle` on a throwaway Vite harness of the real PrimeVue + stylesheets (before → after): primary label 2.54 → 5.48 (hover 3.77 → 7.68, active 5.48 → 9.72), primary text/link 2.54 → 5.48, checkbox/switch fill 2.54 → 5.48, danger button 3.76 → 6.57, danger outlined/text 3.76 → 6.57, warn button 2.80 → 11.66 (now `--app-warning` yellow with dark text — the app's warning role, orange was Aura's), warn text 2.80 → 7.09, message error/warn/success 4.45/2.84/3.16 → 6.01/6.84/6.77, input/checkbox/switch boundary 1.48 → 3.31; dark: on-fill labels (accent badge, danger dialog button) 2.77 → 6.95, message error/info 3.90/3.82 → 5.47/9.06, input/switch boundary 2.22/1.64 → 3.55, primary 9.22 → 10.00. Left: PrimeVue's success/info **buttons** (2.28/2.77) — zero call sites, not routed; disabled controls (exempt); labels over media scrims (rendered content). `admin-web` is its own copy and fails the same 36 → #390. |

| 397 | **Sealed, immutable blob metadata in core (`blob-meta`)** | core, crypto, contracts, backend, web, files, memories, DONE | **done** (2026-10-09; owner-approved design). Built: P1 the purpose-only `blob-meta` seal under the blob's own `K_blob` ([ADR 0072](decisions/0072-a-seal-binds-its-object.md) Decision 2; `packages/crypto/src/blobMeta.ts` `sealBlobMeta`/`openBlobMeta`, vector `seal.sym['blob-meta']`): compact JSON `{v:1, type, name?, modifiedAt?}`, space-padded to the next 256-byte bucket (≤ 1024), no plaintext size and no upload time (the server holds both); P2 optional `wrappedBlobMeta` (base64, ≤ 2048) on `BlobUploadRequestItem` + `ShareBlobUploadRequestItem` and on every read ref that carries `wrappedBlobKey` (`FileBlobRef`, `MediaSummary`, `MediaDerivativeSummary`, the cover `BlobRef`, `ShareMedia`, `ShareMediaDerivative`); P3 `wrapped_meta TEXT NULL` on `veyq_upload_session` + `veyq_blob` in the core baseline (`updatable = false` on both entities; copied session → blob in `persistBlob`; no operation writes it after the start) — a baseline change, so every existing database, dev ones included, is recreated. P4 web core: `planBlob(…, meta?)` seals the meta under the fresh `K_blob` and `reserveAndPump` declares it only when present (member and guest transports alike; Memories passes none yet, so its B0 wire snapshots are byte-identical); `core/blob/meta.ts` `openBlobMetaOf` (null on absent/`OpenRefused`/`BlobMetaInvalid`, never a throw), `readBlobMeta` for a listed ref, the chunk provider's `meta()`, and the one download-name rule `downloadName` with `MIME_TO_EXT` moved there from Memories. P5 Files: a file's type, uploaded name and modified time are its blob's meta (`uploadFile.ts` `fileBlobMeta`, member, guest and drop box); the list reads size (`plaintextTotal`) and date from the blob, the download saves under `downloadName(node name, meta)`; deleted `fileMeta.ts`, the member's node-meta write after the attach (`metaWritten`), `ShareFileCompleteRequest.wrappedMeta` and the backend's `FilesUploadCompletion.META` + `FileContentWrite` — which closes the non-atomic member meta write `docs/apps/depot.md` listed as open. Nodes written before keep a stale `wrappedMeta` that nothing reads; #373's question whether `fileMeta.ts` joins the web mutation scope is moot (the file is gone). P6 Memories: every upload (member and guest alike — one path, `uploadMediaVia`) decides the original's type once (`resolveMediaType`: the file's own, or its extension's, else sniffed from its first 1 KiB, else `application/octet-stream`) and plans each blob with its meta — the ORIGINAL `{type, name, modifiedAt}`, THUMBNAIL/PREVIEW `{type: image/jpeg}`, a derived cover `{type: image/jpeg}` (else the source's type). Read: `summaryToItem`/`shareMediaToItem` carry `wrappedBlobMeta` on every ref; `originalBlobMeta`/`originalMediaType` open it synchronously from the listing under the media's key (null on absent, foreign or unopenable — never a throw); the lightbox saves under `originalDownloadName` = core's `downloadName(null, meta, Memory name + position with the meta type's extension, else `.bin`)`, and its `<video>` branch, its file name/format rows and the tiles' play badge (MemoryDetail, MemoryBuckets) read the blob meta type; the share viewer passes `blobMetaResolver` under each item's own `K_node`. Reconciled with #395 F10: the EXIF-tier `name` (`MediaExif.name`, `withOriginalName`, the drop step's never-shed rule) is gone — its only other reader, the details' file-name row, reads the blob meta — and with it the lightbox's `detailsLoading`/`downloadBlocked` wait. Also gone, dead with the blob meta (pre-release databases are recreated, so no row lacks it): the listed tier's `mediaType` (no contract change — the tier is client-side JSON; it is 32 base64 smaller), `backfillMediaType` and the lightbox's write-back, and `downloadOriginal`'s pre-open chunk-0 read and in-passing sniff (`sniffingFirst`, `replayingFirst`, `storedDownloadName`, `sniffExtension`, `derivedDownloadName`); `sniffMediaType` stays as the upload-time detector. The lightbox's "no details" note hung off the map's `v-if` and showed beside the rows of every photo without a coordinate; it now shows only when nothing is known. B0 wire snapshots re-blessed field by field (every declare item gains a 380-base64 `wrappedBlobMeta`; `wrappedExif` 80 → null; `wrappedMeta` 176 → 144; through the pinned stream the ORIGINAL's and PREVIEW's wraps and every PUT digest moved, the THUMBNAIL's and the cover's wraps are byte-identical; no path, header, order or count moved — stated in `uploadWire.characterisation.test.ts`). Proven by `media.test.ts`, `originalName.wire.test.ts` (at `fetch`, member and guest: the declare's meta opens under `K_blob`, no request carries the name or the type, base64 decoded), `shareUploader.test.ts` (a WRITE_ONLY guest's upload, opened through the drop chain), `MediaLightbox.test.ts` (the two-then-three repro from the blob meta, enabled while EXIF loads, foreign meta → fallback, an untyped upload plays and saves as `.mp4` with nothing read first), `MemoryDetail.test.ts` (badge) and `download.stream.test.ts`, eight bites each red; `media.e2e` records the blob meta as canaries (written, not run here). P7: ADR 0047 (amendment + addendum), ADR 0077 note, ADR 0079 §9, en.memory.media, uc.memory.gallery/upload/concurrent-curation, docs/apps/memories.md, the security model.
