| 403 | **28 `npm audit` findings, and npm cannot update the lockfile without a peer-resolution error** | web, admin-web, dependencies, security, DONE | **DONE 2026-10-11 — 28 findings → 9; the 9 left have no fix that is not a downgrade or a cross-major override, and none is reachable from shipped code.** Measured on **Node 22.23.3** (from the npx cache, the CI/Docker major; npm 10.9.2) — before: 28 (1 critical, 20 high, 7 moderate), after: **9 high, 0 critical, 0 moderate**. **The ERESOLVE, at its root:** `@vue/test-utils` (and its `js-beautify` chain) sat in the lockfile as one copy **nested per workspace** (`web/node_modules/…`, `admin-web/node_modules/…`) against the root `vue`, and npm could not move the root `vue` past that nested peer-dependent. Dropping the nested entries let npm re-place one hoisted copy, after which a resolution without `--force` exists. Two further traps en route, both now written into the root `package.json`'s `//lockfile` note: raising a shared range makes npm **nest** a new `vue` per workspace while keeping the old one at the root — where primevue, vue-i18n and `@vitejs/plugin-vue` still resolve, i.e. two Vue runtimes in one bundle (caught in the lockfile diff, fixed by dropping the vue family's entries so it is placed once); and npm **10.9.x** (Node 22's own, 10.9.9 included) crashes re-resolving vitest's peer set (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'edgesOut')`), so that one install ran with `npx -y npm@11`, followed by a plain npm 10 install so the lockfile keeps the `dev` flags CI's npm writes. **Bumped** (floors raised in the manifests, all within the major): `vue` ^3.5.35→^3.5.42 (locked 3.5.39→3.5.43, the whole `@vue/*` runtime family with it; closes the `@vue/server-renderer` XSS), `vitest` ^4.1.7→^4.1.11 in all three packages (4.1.9→4.1.11, `@vitest/*` with it; closes the mocker path traversal), `@openapitools/openapi-generator-cli` ^2.34.0→^2.41.0 (2.39.0→2.41.0 — **the generator JAR stays 7.23.0**, `openapitools.json` untouched; brings `concurrently` 10.0.6 → `shell-quote` 1.12.0, the critical, and `axios` 1.20.0), the root override `qs` ^6.15.2→^6.16.0 (6.15.3→6.16.0; its `//overrides` reason now names both new advisories and the real drop condition: Stryker 9.6.1 and 10.0.0 still pin `typed-rest-client` ~2.3.0). Transitive, within range: `dompurify` 3.4.11→3.4.16 (← jspdf, shipped), `postcss` 8.5.29, `nanoid` 3.3.20, `source-map-js` 1.2.2, `brace-expansion` 5.0.12, `browserslist` 4.29.3, `baseline-browser-mapping` 2.11.28, `postcss-selector-parser` 7.1.6, `fast-uri` 3.1.8, `ip-address` 10.7.3. **Left, with reasons (all 9 high, all dev/build-time):** (a) `braces` ← `micromatch` ← `fast-glob` ← `@vue/eslint-config-typescript` (+ the two direct-dep rows it raises): no fixed `braces` exists (3.0.3 is both latest and affected); npm's only offer is *downgrading* the eslint config to 14.0.1 — rejected; it globs repo-owned lint patterns, no untrusted input. (b) `basic-ftp` ≤6.2.0 ← `get-uri` ← `pac-proxy-agent` ← `proxy-agent` ← the generator CLI (+ the CLI's own row): `get-uri` 8.0.1, its latest, still requires `basic-ftp` ^5.3.1, so a fix is a cross-major override the checks would pass vacuously (`generate:api` never fetches a PAC file over ftp) — not taken; npm's other offer, CLI 2.14.0, is a downgrade. Reachable only with a `pac+ftp://` proxy configured for the JAR download. Re-check both when `get-uri`/`fast-glob` move. **Proofs:** `python3 scripts/npm_ci_no_deprecated.py` from a clean tree green (and **0 `EBADENGINE`** on Node 22.23.3 — #353's unmeasured half); web/admin-web `generate:api` from scratch + `npm run check` green (web 2363 tests, admin-web 148), packages/crypto `check` green (424); both mutation gates `npm run mutation -- --force` exit 0 (crypto 100 ≥ 100, web 100 ≥ 95) since vitest moved; all `scripts/validate_*.py` green. **Not measured:** the image builds (no Docker run); npm 11 was used only to write the lockfile, every install that proves it ran npm 10. |
| 404 | ~~**A stored map mode leaves an identity folder blank**~~ | web, core, files, ux, DONE | **done 2026-10-11.** The LEVEL decides which mode it shows: the stored preference where the union of what is on screen offers it (the shown sections' `views` and the folders' grid and list), else the grid (`core/node/levelViews.ts` `effectiveViewMode`), and it hands that mode to every section — **contract change:** `LevelSectionProps.viewMode` (ADR 0079 Consequences, web/CLAUDE.md registry paragraph, docs/apps/depot.md); Memories', Files' and the drop boxes' sections no longer read `useViewMode`. The clamp never rewrites the stored value, so the next level with Memories' section shows the map again; the toggle presses the mode shown (a stored map under a grid-and-list switch presses the grid — Memories' chapters toggle too). A stored map in an identity folder now shows Files' rows and the folders in the grid, with the folders' switch when there are folders. Evidence: f7e46993; `LevelView.test.ts` ("the mode it shows": DOM clicks, the stored value kept), `MemoryHomeView.test.ts` (real registry and level: identity folder in the grid, then the map again at its parent), `ViewModeToggle.test.ts`, `FilesSection.test.ts`, `MemoriesSection.test.ts`; bitten: no clamp, the clamp overwriting the stored mode, Files or Memories reading the stored mode, the toggle pressing the raw stored mode, the switch only from three modes — each red. Goldens and B0 wire snapshots untouched (byte-identical). Proofs: web `generate:api` from scratch + `npm run check` green (243 files, 2441 tests, 26 skipped), all 55 `scripts/validate_*.py` green. Unverified: not clicked in a real browser. Was: (low; found 2026-10-11 with #370). The view mode is one app-wide preference; in map mode Files' section and the folders step aside because Memories' section draws the map. In a guest's identity folder the level leaves empty sections out (#385 (b)), so Memories' is gone, the map with it, and Files' rows are hidden too: a member who last chose the map sees only the header card, and the toolbar offers no switch (nothing on screen has two presentations). Reasoned from `LevelView.vue`/`FilesSection.vue`, not clicked. A fix wants the level to hand its sections the mode it actually shows (a stored mode no shown section offers falls back to the grid) instead of each reading `useViewMode` — a contract change of `LevelSectionProps`. |
| 405 | ~~**The level view's live refresh re-reads the whole list; it should ask for a revision**~~ | backend, web, sharing, files, performance, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** ([ADR 0081](decisions/0081-a-scopes-tree-revision-is-the-level-views-change-signal.md), the media revision of ADR 0033 one level up; owner-approved 2026-10-12, file deletions explicitly covered). A per-scope counter `veyq_scope_tree_revision` (core baseline, pin line moved, `release: none`; dev DBs recreated) bumped by `NodesStore.bumpTree` — one atomic `UPDATE`, the write's last statement, under the scope lock every bumping writer already holds (a row of its own, not a `veyq_scope` column: that row is the tree's lock) — in node create, identity-folder create, `applyUpdate` (rename, move, meta, reorder; member and guest), trash (a deleted file is its trashed node), restore, the expired-trash purge, `AttachmentStore.attach`/`detach` (a file's upload, replace and removal, a cover, `completeShareFile`) and the first drop key a `WRITE_ONLY` link sets on its root. `listNodes` answers `ETag` = the revision and `304` to its echo (revision read before the rows, access gate first). **Guest decision:** `resolveShare`'s tag is a digest of what the response serves plus the attachments of the served nodes, NOT the scope revision — that would tell an anonymous guest when anything in the rest of the space changes; cost equals the reads the resolve already did, a server-side cache keyed by the revision is the recorded, unbuilt optimisation. A polling guest now notices a revoked/expired/deleted link (`404` → the unavailable state; a changed password → the gate; network, 429, 5xx only back off). Web: `fetchTree`/`pollTree` and `pollPublicShare` echo the tag, a `304` is no parse/decrypt/tick, `useLevelLive.read` resolves whether the scope MOVED (Files re-reads its blob listing then), the fingerprint stays the fallback. Tests: backend 22 + 3 (race, MariaDB-green) + 6 Files + 4 source-guard (`TreeRevisionBumpsTest`: red on an unlisted writer of node or attachment rows); web +4 live, +15 wire. Bitten: every bump site (all red, each path test), the 304 compare, the guest tag taking the scope revision, the guest tag without attachments, the web echo, the web tick on 304, the 404 mapping, the unavailable flip, the trashed-root flip — each red, restored byte-identical. Unverified: not clicked in a real browser; `run_web_e2e.py` not run. |
| 406 | **One header for every node, and one "New" menu for every creation** | web, core, files, memories, ux, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** (owner design, steps 1 and 2 of the unified level; step 3 is #407). (1) `NodeHeader` (`core/node/components/NodeHeader.vue`, `core/node/nodeHeader.ts`) is the header of a folder's level, a file, a drop box and a Memory: leading visual (the kind's icon or the application's cover slot), title, subtitle, meta slot, and the actions on the right — an application feeds only data and its capability-gated action list (`NodeHeaderAction`), core owns look, wrap, keyboard and dark mode; a share guest gets the same header with fewer actions (#401). Core's own actions (a future favorite heart on every node) plug in as `coreActions`, merged around the application's. `NodeOverviewCard` is retired. (2) The creation actions left the sections' headers and tiles for ONE "New" (DE "Neu") menu in the level toolbar, derived from the registry: a kind's `createEntry` (label key of its own namespace, icon, order, `allowed`/`enabled` capability functions; needs a `createDialog`) and `levelActions` (Files' "Upload files": the mounted section registers its handler with the level, `core/node/levelActions.ts`; greyed out while none is mounted, i.e. while another application's tab is shown). Core adds the folder entry; order Folder, Memory, Drop box, Upload. The empty-state tiles ("New Memory", "New folder") and the section header buttons are gone — an empty section says so in plain text (the files text points at "New" only where an upload is possible); drag and drop upload is unchanged. A READ guest has no menu. |
| 407 | **Unified level list (step 3 of #406)** | web, core, files, memories, ux, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** (owner design, sketch approved). ONE list (or tiles) per level, drawn by CORE: folders, Memories, files, drop boxes, every kind, no section headers; an application only supplies ROW DATA. **Decisions.** (1) *Row descriptor:* a kind names `list: { rows, tabLabel?, views?, emptyText? }` replacing `section`; `rows` is a factory the level calls once per view with the reactive `LevelRowsContext` (folder, the nodes of its kinds, epochs, access, tree, clamped view mode, live tick) and a `LevelRowsHost` (open, rename, move, registerAction) and returns `row(node) -> { icon, leading?, title, meta, primary, actions[] }` (or `null` = explicit unsupported row), `notices` and `presentation(mode)`. Actions are the header's `NodeHeaderAction` plus `first`/`loading`, each `shown` by the application's fail-closed capability function; at most two `first` actions are buttons, the rest sit in one `⋯` PrimeVue menu; core's own actions (#409's heart) plug in as `coreRowActions`, reserved and empty. (2) *Order:* folders first, then ALL other nodes mixed by name (natural, locale-aware, case-insensitive `Intl.Collator`, ties by `compareByCreation`) - pure function `core/node/levelList.ts`; a Memory's date no longer sorts. (3) *Tabs* are a pure filter on the kind's application; folders in every tab; offered where the kinds have a node here or the viewer may add one; stored preference unchanged and never rewritten. (4) *Primary click opens* for every kind (a file opens its page, `FileDetail`); Files' download is its one first-class action; replace/rename/move/delete are in the menu; Memories: the heart first-class, rename/move in the menu; a drop box: move/delete. (5) *Files' duties moved into the row factory* (`features/depot/levelRows.ts`): blob listing + live tick, upload/replace flows with confirmation and a built (detached) file picker, refusals/notices (drawn by core's `LevelNotices`), download, delete + undo; upload is the level action `files.upload` registered for the level's life (so the New menu entry is never greyed out by another tab) and takes dropped files (`acceptsDrop`: core hands them over only while the entry is allowed AND enabled). (6) *Memories' duties* (`features/memory/levelRows.ts`): covers, dates, the heart, and the MAP as the factory's `presentation('map')` (offered through `views`, drawn by core in place of the list). (7) *Empty states:* one plain level text composed from the New-menu entries (`node.levelEmptyNew`), or `node.levelEmpty`; an application tab with nothing shows its descriptor's text. **Not done / open:** a per-row *delete* and *share* for Memories and drop boxes need the host to mount core's delete/share dialogs for a child (today only on the opened node's page); the Memory meta line shows its date range, not a photo count (the tree holds none); chapters' grid in `MemoryDetail` still uses `MemoryCard`. Code: `core/node/LevelView.vue`, `LevelRowItem.vue`, `levelList.ts`, `levelRow.ts`. |
| 408 | **A level on screen stays current: live refresh, ~5 s, conditional on the scope's tree revision** | web, backend, core, files, memories, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** → [ADR 0081](decisions/0081-a-scopes-tree-revision-is-the-level-views-change-signal.md), [uc.memory.browse § live refresh](features/memory/use-cases/uc.memory.browse.md#live-refresh). Owner finding from a real-browser walkthrough: an owner tab did not show a file a share guest had just uploaded. Built in two steps: a visibility-gated, jittered, backed-off poll that re-reads the tree and compares a fingerprint (#408 first), then the per-scope tree revision with `ETag`/`304` on `listNodes` and `resolveShare` (#405; a guest's tag is a hash of what the guest may see, never the scope revision). Seen working in two real browsers on 2026-10-12: a guest's upload appeared in the owner's open level after 1 s, the idle ticks were `304`. A poll pauses while the tab is hidden and while a dialog is open, by design. Not covered: a cover change; a push or long-poll (ADR 0033's deferred upgrade). |
| 409 | **Favorites for every node (a pin in core)** | core, web, backend, contracts, ux, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** (owner 2026-10-12, after #406 and #407). Today the pin is Memories' (`veyq_*favorites` table in the Memories baseline, `generated/favorites`, the heart on a Memory card and detail only): a private set of node ids sealed under the master key ([ADR 0068](decisions/0068-favorites-two-confidentiality-levels.md)), so the data already fits any node. Move the pin to core (contract, backend table in the core baseline, web store) and make the heart a CORE action: `NodeHeader`'s `coreActions` (placement before/after the application's list) and the future row descriptors (#407) carry it for folder, file, Memory and drop box alike, gated by a core capability function, member-only (a guest sees no heart). The sidebar's Favorites section lists every kind with its descriptor icon and opens the node through the registry. A permanent delete removes the pin (check Memories' cleanup path moves with it). The per-item mark on a Memory's media stays in Memories. A drop box is a node (a container with per-guest identity folders), so it needs no special case. Changes the core baseline (pin line), so after the revision work and one at a time. **Decisions.** (1) *Contract and storage:* `contracts/api/favorites.openapi.yaml` is core's (`getFavorites`/`putFavorites`, same wire, same 65 536 cap); the Memories spec shrank to `memories/item-favorites.openapi.yaml`; `veyq_scope_favorites` moved from the Memories to the core baseline (both pinned, `release: none`, dev databases recreated); core's `favorites` package holds the entity, store, handler, resource and the phase-600 scope eraser. Seal purpose, plaintext shape and last-write-wins unchanged, so the crypto vectors and every stored set stay valid. (2) *The heart is a core action:* `favoriteCapabilities(access, node)` (member only, fail closed, no scope root, no unsupported kind, kept during a pending deletion as Memories always did) -> `favoriteHeaderActions` / `favoriteRowActions`, `first`, `placement: before`; `LevelView` contributes it to every row and the folder header, `FileDetail`, `DropBoxDetail` and `MemoryDetailHost` pass it to `NodeHeader`; Memories' own heart (row, header, card, the `favorite` capability) is gone. (3) *Sidebar:* core's `PinnedNodes` lists every kind with its registry icon and name, opened by `hashForNode`; the Memory cover the old rail showed returned with #411 (for every kind that has one). (4) *Cleanup:* the server cannot read the set, so a purged id stays inert and every view skips what does not resolve (no client prune: it cannot tell purged from not loaded); a trashed node's pin survives so a restore returns the heart; the scope eraser still takes the rows with a group. (5) *Guests:* no heart, no endpoint. (6) A drop box is a node: no special case. *Found, kept:* the server's put takes the WRITE gate, so the heart shown during a pending deletion is refused with `scope_deleting` and reverts. |
| 410 | **Core primitive: revisioned item collections inside a node (counter, conditional GET, keyset delta)** | core, backend, contracts, web, applications, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** (decision record at the end of this row; the text before it is the owner's brief). Owner 2026-10-12, after #409. Memories' gallery has it today in its own module ([ADR 0033](decisions/0033-live-media-updates-revision-polling.md)): a per-node `media_revision` + `delete_revision` (`veyq_media_revision`), `ETag`/`304`, a keyset cursor `(updatedAt, id)` for adds/edits and the id-set reconcile on a delete. Lift it into core as the platform's way for any application to publish a list of records that are not nodes (Memories' media now; Expenses' entries #325, Inventory's items #326 later): a core table of the two counters per node and a bump helper taken in the writer's transaction, a generic contract fragment for the conditional read and the delta, and a web poller on `shared/state/livePoller.ts`; Memories moves onto it with no behaviour change (its tests and race tests stay green, `veyq_media_revision` goes). The scope tree revision of #405 stays: it covers the node list, this covers the items inside a node. Changes the core and Memories baselines (pin lines); one baseline-touching job at a time. **Decision record (2026-10-12).** (1) *Core table:* `veyq_node_items_revision(node_id UUID PK, revision INT, delete_revision INT)` in the core baseline, a foreign key onto `veyq_node` that does not cascade; one list per node (a node's kind decides which application owns it), a `list` column in the key is not built until a kind needs two. No row reads 0/0. (2) *Helper:* `core/items/NodeItemsRevisions.bump(scopeId, nodeId, deleted)` - ONE atomic `UPDATE ... revision + 1`, insert on the first miss under the NODE row's lock with the UPDATE asked again beneath it. **Not under the scope lock**, which the brief suggested: the gallery writers lock a media row and never the scope, so a scope lock taken only here would be a new first lock after the media rows (the order `MediaLockOrderRaceSliceTest` pins is record rows -> node row -> revision row); the revision row stays a leaf. `read(nodeId, ifNoneMatch, sinceDelete, delta, ids)` returns `NotModified` or `Changed(revisions, delta, ids?)`; `ItemsETag` and `ItemsCursor` carry the wire forms. (3) *Erasure:* core's - `NodeItemsRevisionNodeSubtreeEraser` (phase 300) and `NodeScopeEraser` (ahead of the node delete); Memories' two erasers lost their counter line. (4) *Contract:* `_common.openapi.yaml` gains `parameters` `ItemsIfNoneMatch` / `ItemsSince` / `ItemsSinceDelete`, header `ItemsETag` and response `ItemsNotModified`, which `listNodeMedia` now `$ref`s; the response FIELDS stay Memories' (`mediaRevision`, `deleteRevision`, `mediaIds`) because a primitive is no generated type and renaming them would change the wire - **no wire change**: the regenerated `MediaApi.ts` differs from before in doc comments only. (5) *Memories:* `veyq_media_revision`, `MediaRevisionEntity`, `MediaStore.bumpMediaRevision` / `revisions` and `MediaCursor` are gone; the seven writer sites (attach, trash, restore, meta edit, the two re-bucketings, a guest's delete-own) call `items.bump`; the cover stays excluded. (6) *Web:* `core/items/itemsLive.ts` (`itemsPollTick`, `reconcileItems`, `newestItemsCursor`, `ITEMS_POLL`) on `shared/state/livePoller.ts`; the media poll keeps its 20 s interval and gains the jitter, the back-off on a failing server and the focus catch-up (strictly better, nothing lost), and a failed poll now rejects to the loop instead of being swallowed in the tick (`pollMedia` still swallows for a hand call). (7) *Guards:* `NodeItemsRevisionBumpsTest` (every Memories media writer bumps, no unlisted writer, nothing but core names the entity), `NodeItemsRevisionsCoreSliceTest`, `itemsLive.test.ts`; `NodeSubtreeEraserOrderSliceTest` / `CoreAloneSliceTest` / `ModuleCompositionTest` carry the new eraser. Both baselines changed with their pin lines: every existing database, dev ones included, is recreated (`docker compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml down -v`). |
| 411 | **A cover for any node, first for folders (and drop boxes): a core feature** | core, web, ux, files, memories, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** (decision record at the end of this row; the text below is the owner's brief and the design note it was built from). Owner 2026-10-12, planned after #409 (favorites in core; both hang on the `NodeHeader` and the row actions and touch the core baseline, so one at a time). Today a cover is Memories' (the `COVER` slot of core's `veyq_node_attachment`, chosen from the Memory's own photos, `useMemoryCover`). Make it core's: any node can carry a cover, the unified list's leading visual (all kinds already share one size, #407) and the `NodeHeader` show it, the icon stays the default; 'Set cover' becomes a CORE action next to the application's (`coreActions` / `coreRowActions`), Memories moves onto it. **Step 1 (decided 2026-10-12, option 1b): the owner uploads an image directly as the cover** of a folder or drop box (it serves as the cover only, it is not a file in the folder; the client derives the cover size with the existing derive pipeline and attaches it to the `COVER` slot as the node's own blob). Because the cover is the node's own blob, a move or delete of other files never touches it, and there is no dangling reference to handle; it goes to the trash and the quota with the node like any attachment. Choosing a cover from the folder's images (option 1a) is not built. **Not now: a cover or thumbnail for a FILE** — it needs the client to understand many file formats; separate idea, not mixed in. Open design points for the build: how the cover upload plugs into the upload queue and quota (a dedicated blob role under the existing open `BlobRole`, the cover seal bound to the node, ADR 0072) and Memories' cover (chosen from a photo of the Memory) moving onto the same core action as a second source, who may set it (writers only; a guest of a share sees it, never sets it unless the role allows it), and the guest-visible form.

**Design note (2026-10-12, before the build).** *What exists.* A Memory's uploaded cover is already core's `COVER` attachment (`veyq_node_attachment`, `AttachmentStore.attach`/`detach`), but the ONLY endpoints are Memories' (`commitCoverBlob`/`deleteCoverBlob`, MEMORY and CHAPTER only), the role `COVER` is declared by Memories (`MemoryBlobRoles`), and which image/crop a node shows lives in the node's sealed `wrappedMeta.cover` (`{source:{kind:'upload',ref}|{kind:'media',mediaId}, crop}`, seal purpose `node-meta` bound to scope+node, the blob's `blob-key` wrap bound to blob+node+`COVER`). `updateNode` takes `wrappedMeta` for any kind, a guest already reads any attachment of a shared subtree (`resolveShareBlob`) and gets every `ShareNode.wrappedMeta`, quota/trash/purge/erasure are slot-agnostic. So the server needs no schema change. *What moves.* (1) **Contract + backend:** two core operations in `content.openapi.yaml`, `setNodeCover` and `removeNodeCover`, each carrying the cover blob step AND the node's re-sealed `wrappedMeta` + `metaVersion`, applied in ONE transaction (attach/detach under the scope lock, then the version-checked meta write; a stale meta rolls the attach back, a stale slot answers `cover_stale` with the slot's current blob): the slot and the meta that names it can never part (no half state), and the compare-and-set of ADR 0077 is the only concurrency rule. Memories' two endpoints, `CoverBlobCommitRequest` and its handler/store/tests go; `COVER` becomes a role core declares (`CoreMemberBlobRoles`), the slot constant is core's. (2) **Opt-in seam (backend):** `NodeKindRules.takesCover(kind)` (default false), answered by the rule that already handles the kind - `FolderNodeRules` FOLDER, Files' rules `FILES_DROPBOX` (not `FILE`: no file covers), Memories' `MEMORY`/`CHAPTER`; core names no application kind. (3) **Web core:** `core/node/cover/` holds the cover model and sealed `cover` field (the same shape as Memories', so every stored Memory cover reads byte for byte; `nodeMeta.ts`/`nodeMetaWrite.ts` move from Memories to core with the pure envelope, unknown siblings `date`/`location` preserved), the derive+upload job (existing `createBrowserDeriver`, the `COVER_SPEC` {2048, 0.85} JPEG Memories' covers use, `planBlob`/`reserveAndPump` through the core upload queue so progress/failure show in the existing tray and toasts), the display store `coverStore` (moved from Memories; lazy decrypt, object URLs revoked on replace/remove/reset) and the guest loader. The action is `coverCapabilities(access, node)` (member, writable scope = not pending deletion, key ready, kind opted in through the descriptor flag `cover`, fail closed, no guest) -> `coverHeaderActions`/`coverRowActions` (`set`/`change`/`remove` as ONE `cover` action plus `cover-remove`, entries in the row's more-menu and the header), core's hidden `image/*` picker. The descriptor flag is `cover: true | { load? }`; `load` lets an application read a source core cannot (Memories: the `media` source = a thumbnail of one of its photos), so the sidebar, rows and header all use one store. A kind's own source UI: the node page may hand `coverHeaderActions` a `choose` handler (Memories' detail opens its existing dialog, which now commits through the core operations); everywhere else core's picker (image file, full-frame crop) runs. (4) **Display:** `LevelRowItem` leading (kind icon is the default; core supplies the cover for folder rows and any application row without its own `leading`), `NodeHeader` leading, `PinnedNodes` (restores the Memory cover for every kind). A failed or missing cover is the icon, silently. *Guests.* A guest sees covers of nodes in its shared subtree (the same blob route and the node's `wrappedMeta` it already gets) and gets no cover action (capability false for every guest role this step). Operator-blind and the guest read rules are unchanged: nothing new is served. *Removal is real.* Replace and remove free the old blob in the same transaction as the swap (row + a `veyq_store_reclaim` record per object, ADR 0062: the sweep deletes the objects after its interval); the client drops the old object URL from its store at once; nothing can reach copies already in other viewers' browsers or presigned URLs until they expire. **Decision record (built 2026-10-12).** *Contract:* `setNodeCover` (`PUT /nodes/{id}/cover`) and `removeNodeCover` (`POST /nodes/{id}/cover/removal`) in `content.openapi.yaml`, `409` bodies `NodeCoverConflict` (`cover_stale` with the slot's current blob, `stale_version` with the current meta); Memories' `commitCoverBlob`/`deleteCoverBlob` and `BlobRef` are gone, no schema change. *Backend:* `NodeCoverHandler`/`NodeCoverStore` in core (`AttachmentStore` attach/detach then `NodesStore.applyUpdate` in ONE transaction: a refused slot commits the loser's freed upload, a stale meta rolls the attach back), `NodeKindRules.takesCover` (FOLDER core, FILES_DROPBOX Files, MEMORY/CHAPTER Memories; not FILE), `CoreMemberBlobRoles` (core alone admits `COVER`; `validate_kernel_imports_no_slices` drops it from the application roles), two tree bumps per request. *Web:* `core/node/cover/` (model, writer, store, capabilities, `useNodeCover`, picker, `NodeCoverImage`), `nodeMeta.ts`/`nodeMetaWrite.ts` moved from Memories to core (stryker scope follows), `KindDescriptor.cover` (+ `load` for a source core cannot open), `LevelRowsHost.covers`/`ensureCovers`, the sidebar's Favorites show the cover again for every kind. *Memories:* its dialog saves through the same core write (`setMemoryCover`/`setMemoryCoverUpload`), its header's core action hands `choose` the dialog, its photo source is `coverLoad.ts`; `coverStore`/`guestCover` are deleted. *Guests:* see the covers (the node's `wrappedMeta` they already get, the share's gated blob route), no action for any role. *Golden/wire diffs:* the level golden gains each row's "more" menu button (cover action) and the folder header's "Set cover" (18 lines in 5 files); `uploadWire.uploadCoverBlob.json`: the blob declare and PUT digests are unchanged, only the commit `POST .../cover-blob` became `PUT .../cover` carrying the sealed meta. *Honest limits:* copies already loaded by other viewers and presigned URLs until they expire cannot be erased by us; an upload no write attached ages out through the stale-session purge. *Not done:* a cover for a FILE, choosing a cover from a folder's images (1a), a guest setting a cover, real-browser proof of the picker/derive of real photos (unit tests stub the pixel stage). **Follow-up 2026-10-12 (owner, after trying covers in a real browser): ONE cover dialog in core.** *Flaw seen:* a folder's cover had only a bare picker action 'Set cover' plus a second header button 'Remove cover', both with the same image icon, and no crop. *Decisions.* (1) The dialog Memories had is core's: `core/node/cover/components/CoverDialog.vue` (+ `CoverEditor.vue`, which every view mounts beside its content: `LevelView`, `DropBoxDetail`, `MemoryDetailHost`), strings `node.cover.*`; it offers choose an image, the shared 16:9 `ImageCropper`, re-crop of the current cover (stored crop restored), Remove (only where a cover exists), Save/Cancel, and rejects a non-image/empty/over-40-MB file inside the dialog. (2) ONE header action and ONE row-menu entry, `cover` ('Set cover' / 'Change cover', `pi-image`); the separate `cover-remove` action is deleted (removal lives in the dialog). (3) Save is `useNodeCover.save(node, intent)` for EVERY kind - the existing atomic writer: an `upload` intent is one upload-queue job + `setNodeCover` with the framed crop, `keep` a plain meta write of the stored cover value with only its crop replaced, `media` a meta write of the value the kind seals, `remove` `removeNodeCover`; same cover meta shape, so Memory covers re-open with their crop. Conflict rule: an upload and a removal re-apply over a peer's change (`surfaceConflict` false), `keep`/`media` surface it - the dialog re-opens on the server's current cover and says so (`node.cover.stale`). (4) **The seam for the Memory's extra source** is `KindCover.extraSource` in the registry descriptor `{ hintKey, seal(id, crop), idOf(cover) }` (Memories: `memoryPhotoSource` in `memoryCover.ts`): `hintKey` is an i18n key of the application's own namespace (`memory.cover.galleryHint`) that core resolves without reading by name, `seal` writes the stored shape (`{ source: { kind: 'media', mediaId }, crop }`, key order pinned), `idOf` tells which photo a stored cover already is so only that photo's crop is restored. The lightbox's 'Set as cover' emits `coverFromPhoto { mediaId, src }` up to `MemoryDetailHost`, which calls `cover.open(node, { source })` (no thumbnail yet: `cover.save` full-frame in one step, as before); the tile emits `editCover`. (5) **Deleted:** `MemoryCoverDialog.vue` (+ test), the Memory-only save path (`useMemoryCover`'s editor state and `onCoverSave`, the source's `cover` door and `CoverDoor`, `setMemoryCover`/`setMemoryCoverUpload`/`sameMemoryCover`/`CoverSaveIntent` in `memoryCover.ts`), core's programmatic `pickImageFile`, `memory.cover.{set,edit,choose,copy,copyNoMedia,upload,remove,save}` and `media.uploadNoSpace` (the neutral `node.cover.noSpace` replaces it - the trash hint of the photo wording is gone); `memory.cover.{setFromPhoto,isCover}` stay (the lightbox) and `memory.cover.galleryHint` is the one new Memory string (the old copy/copyNoMedia split on 'the Memory has photos' went with it: one line, shown always). (6) `sourceCapabilities.setCover` no longer rests on a source door (there is none): a guest's column of the matrix is false and core's composable is member-only. (7) Display: the crop was already one formula (`coverImageStyle`) drawn by `NodeCoverImage` (core) and `MemoryCoverImage` (Memory page/map); a parity test pins that both frame identically, a cover without a crop is the centred fit. *Golden/wire:* no golden snapshot changed (the row menu already carried the 'cover' entry; only the removed one left it - none was in the golden's state), `uploadWire.*.json` byte-identical (the scenario now calls the core pair `uploadCoverImage` + `writeNodeCover`, which is what the dialog runs). *Not proven (no real browser here):* the crop interaction (drag/zoom) and the OS file picker in a real browser, the dialog's focus/Escape under a real PrimeVue overlay (unit tests mount the real Dialog in happy-dom; Escape is asserted as the dialog's own close event). |
| 412 | **View a file when it is opened: text, images, video, audio and PDF in the file page (no preview blobs for Files)** | web, files, core, ux, DONE | **done 2026-10-12** (decision record at the end of this row; the text before it is the owner's brief). Owner 2026-10-12. Files does NOT generate thumbnails or preview blobs (a Memory keeps its derived tiers; a cover for a FILE stays out, #411). Instead the file page (`FileDetail`, member and share guest alike — a share looks like the member view) loads and decrypts the file itself when it is opened and shows it in the page or full screen: images (the type sniffed from the bytes, not the claimed MIME; SVG only as an `<img>` from a blob URL, never inline), plain text (size cap, shown as text, never as HTML), video and audio through the service worker's media route (ADR 0047, ranged, no whole-file decrypt in memory), PDF through the browser's own viewer from a blob URL if the CSP allows (else pdf.js, a lazily loaded optional dependency, Apache-2.0, or the download only). A size cap per kind (a file over it offers the download instead), a clear fallback when the browser cannot render a format (HEIC off Safari, an unknown codec), no new dependency for the first cut. Reuses the chunked reader and streaming download of #371 and the media loaders Memories already has. Open design points: CSP (`img-src`/`media-src`/`frame-src blob:`), whether a PDF may sit in a sandboxed frame, mobile Safari behaviour, text encodings and line limits, keyboard/full-screen controls shared with the Memories lightbox. **Decision record (built, web only - no contract or backend change).** The file page (`FileDetail`, member and share guest alike) mounts `FileViewer`, which reads the file when the page opens through the source's new `chunks()` (the download's chunk provider; a guest's presign is the link's) and shows it above the facts. **The bytes decide** (`core/blob/sniff.ts`, magic numbers; the sealed type and name only narrow): images JPEG/PNG/GIF/WebP/AVIF/BMP (HEIC tried, `img.onerror` falls back) and SVG (markup first element `<svg` AND an svg claim; otherwise it is text) as `<img>` on a `useObjectUrl` blob URL typed with the sniffed type, cap 64 MiB; text = valid UTF-8 (BOM dropped) or UTF-16 with a BOM, no NUL/control byte, AND a text-ish type or extension, as `textContent` in a `<pre>`, cap 1 MiB (cut on a character boundary, a note says so), other 8-bit encodings are not guessed (not valid UTF-8, so not shown); video/audio (mp4/mov/webm/mkv/ogg, mp3/aac/wav/flac/m4a) via the service worker's route with the page-side provider, uncapped, `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 3 -> 4 because the worker now accepts `audio/*` besides `video/*` (a stale worker falls back, as designed), no worker = message + download; reused: `registerPlayback`/`makeChunkProvider` (already core-level, `sw/` and `core/blob`), the EBML reader moved from `features/memory/download.ts` into `core/blob/sniff.ts`. States: loading (progress, Stop) / ready / too-large / unsupported (format, pdf, empty, codec, no-sw) / error (Try again) / cancelled; unmount or a new file aborts the read (AbortSignal into the stream pump) and revokes the object URL and the playback handle. Full screen: the Fullscreen API on the viewer container, a fixed overlay where it is missing/refused. **PDF decision (first cut):** the shipped policy (`web/src/shared/security/csp.ts`, `dist/content-security-policy.txt`, served by the backend app shell) is `object-src 'none'; frame-src 'none'`, so the browser's viewer in a blob frame is impossible without widening it - NOT done (owner's call): the first cut showed a message + the download. **PDF inline - done 2026-10-12 (owner: 'I want PDFs shown inline on the file page'), policy UNCHANGED.** Drawn by **pdf.js** (`pdfjs-dist` 6.4.299, Apache-2.0, published 2026-10-03, not deprecated; the *legacy* build) onto `<canvas>` elements inside the page. `pdf/pdfEngine.ts` is the seam (`openPdf` -> page count, page size, `render`, `destroy`), reaching pdf.js through a dynamic `import()` of `pdfjsRuntime.ts`, so the library (~0.5 MB) and its worker (~1.3 MB, a hashed same-origin asset emitted as `assets/pdf.worker.min-<hash>.js` for `worker-src 'self'`) load on the first PDF only; `npm run verify:pdf-worker` (in `check`) pins the chunk, the worker, the dynamic-only import, no `eval`/`new Function`, no `.wasm` and the runtime data. Options (`pdf/pdfOptions.ts`): the document is `{data}` (never a URL), `useWasm: false`, `enableXfa: false`, no scripting (pdf.js 6 has none without the viewer's scripting manager), no annotation layer (a text layer was added later the same day: see the follow-ups), `verbosity: 0`; CMaps, standard fonts and ICC profiles are same-origin copies under `assets/pdfjs-<version>/` (`connect-src 'self'`), no origin added. `PdfPages.vue`: vertical scroll, pages drawn only near the viewport (IntersectionObserver, one-viewport margin, canvas freed when far), "Page n of N", zoom out/in/fit-width (50-400 %), `files.viewer.pdf.*` DE+EN; caps 64 MiB, 1000 pages, 32 M pixels per image, 16 M per canvas; a protected PDF -> first cut 'password protected' + download, since then a password prompt (follow-ups), an unreadable one or one over the caps -> message + download; the document is destroyed on replace, navigation and unmount; member and guest mount the same component. Sniff: `%PDF-` at byte 0 or, for a non-text head, within the first KiB. Proven: 30 viewer unit tests (engine mocked, bitten: eager drawing, no free, no destroy, no cap, redraw-after-zoom ...), 7 real-library node tests (page count, size, password-protected, broken, too many pages, the option set), `verify:pdf-worker` bitten 10 ways on the built dist, and once in a real browser (the built chunk + worker under the built CSP header from a local server: 3 pages drawn, a standard font fetched same-origin, a broken PDF refused, no `securitypolicyviolation`). **Not proven:** the Vue viewer on a real file page (scroll, zoom, full screen, mobile), the dev server's serving of the runtime data. **Follow-ups done 2026-10-12 (owner: 'do the follow-ups too - it applies to normal text as well'), policy still UNCHANGED:** (a) **PDF text layer** - pdf.js 6's `TextLayer` (`streamTextContent` + `new TextLayer({textContentSource, container, viewport})`, viewport at the CSS scale) over each drawn canvas: selectable and copyable text (mouse, keyboard, Ctrl/Cmd+C), built with the page, emptied with the bitmap, rebuilt on zoom, sized as the raw page and turned into place for `/Rotate` (a real browser found that bug), spans only so links stay NOT clickable, a scan simply has an empty layer; its few rules are `styles/pdf.css` (tokens). (b) **Password prompt** - `openPdf(bytes, signal, onPassword)` keeps one loading task and answers pdf.js' `onPassword`; an in-page form (labelled `type=password` field, `autocomplete="off"`, focus, Enter/Escape), a wrong password is the inline error 'Wrong password, try again', Cancel keeps the download offer; the password is only a promise value on its way to pdf.js - never state, log, storage or network. (e) **Plain text** - selectable (a real-DOM check against the stylesheets proves no `user-select: none` reaches it), a Copy button (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`, then the selection through `execCommand('copy')`, then the text selected with a Ctrl/Cmd+C note; a truncated text copies and says it copied only the shown part) and a Wrap lines toggle (on by default). Tests: text layer one-per-drawn-page, torn down, rebuilt on zoom; password flow (prompt, wrong, right, cancel, nothing left in DOM/state/storage/console/network); the real library on an RC4-encrypted fixture (right password opens, `getTextContent()` returns the text of an encrypted page); Copy / truncated / fallback; all bitten. Real browser (the Vite dev server with the real header, the real `FileViewer`): mouse-drag selection, alignment at 100 % / 200 % / rotated, link text does not navigate, password flow, Escape -> download, Copy fallback; the clipboard API's success path was NOT observable there (the pane refuses it). **Still open, both need an owner decision:** (c) an annotation layer (live links = a navigation and phishing vector: a PDF must not navigate the app - needs a policy for where a click may go; form fields); (d) **the WASM decoders (JBIG2, JPEG 2000, ICC, PostScript) need `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` - a policy decision** (until then a scanned page may show gaps; pdf.js ships asm.js fallbacks that could be served same-origin without the policy change - not tried); (f) unproven in a real browser: the viewer on a real file page behind the app, SW video/audio for a FILE, fullscreen, mobile Safari, HEIC, a phone. Spec: [uc.depot.view-file](features/depot/use-cases/uc.depot.view-file.md), [files.md](apps/depot.md) § The viewer. |

Verified by tests, **not in a real browser**. Decided in the build: the prompt shows only when neither a name nor a picture is set; the avatar also shows in the members list and the user menu. |

Relates to #325 (Expenses) and #326 (Inventory), which ask the same for other household data. |
| 417 | **A map view for a level: an application offers its nodes as a map (Memories first)** | core, web, memories, people, ux, DONE (web 2026-10-14) | **done 2026-10-14** (owner 2026-10-13; owner decision 2026-10-14; web only, no contract or backend change). | 418 | **Richer level rows: application details under the name, taller rows with a larger cover, and the same list with a rows/tiles switch on the profile page** | core, web, memories, files, people, ux, DONE (web 2026-10-13) | **done 2026-10-13** (owner 2026-10-13; web only, no contract or backend change). A row or tile now shows, under its name, the kind and the **details** its application supplies through the row (`LevelRow.details`, an ordered `{ id, text }` list; core joins, wraps and styles it, the application owns the facts and leaves out what a node does not have - [apps/README](apps/README.md#how-an-application-shows-facts-in-a-levels-rows-and-tiles)): Memories - date and place from the unsealed node meta (`features/memory/memoryRowDetails.ts`, one builder for the level and the profile); Files - size and the sealed modified time (the contract has no server-observed `updatedAt` on a file, so there is none to show); a drop box - its people count. A share guest sees the same details (rights remove functions, not facts). List rows are taller with a larger visual (4.5 x 3 rem, 6.5 x 4 rem from 521 px, one flush column for every kind), tiles are 16:9 covers in wider columns (280-340 px), cover images are `loading=lazy decoding=async`. The profile page's "Memories with this person" uses these very items and offers the folder view's rows/tiles switch over the same per-user stored preference (a stored map mode shows tiles, untouched). **Decisions:** the hook is the `details` of the row the existing `list.rows` source answers (not a second descriptor callback: the data a detail needs - Files' blob listing - lives in the factory); no ADR (no core extension rule beyond ADR 0079/0080 changed). **Not built:** the map view (#417, deferred); the visual proof in a real browser. |
| 419 | **An upgrade is proven, not promised: a release candidate boots on a database of the previous release** | infra, backend, ci, release | **refined 2026-10-14 → cr-upgrade-proof** ([ADR 0057](decisions/0057-release-identity-and-deployment-recipe.md) Decision 7, criterion `a-predecessors-backup-upgrades-with-its-content-intact` of [uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance](features/admin/use-cases/uc.admin.back-up-and-restore-instance.md); owner: build and run it now against the previous main commit, the predecessor a configured ref that becomes the last release by one default; in CI a job `publish` needs, **disabled until the first release**; red before the release informs, never gates; a backup of the predecessor restored onto the candidate, whose suites assert the content). The "Blocked until" below is lifted by that. The Helm leg is #428. Was: **inbox** (owner 2026-10-14, from the release-pipeline refinement). Nothing today shows that a new release leaves an instance of the previous one intact: Flyway migrates at boot with no down path ([ADR 0056](decisions/0056-instance-durable-state-and-cold-logical-backup.md) §6), and of [infra/README](../infra/README.md) § An upgrade, step by step only the backup step has ever run. backend/CLAUDE.md § Migrations already names the moment this becomes possible - the first release of a major >= 1 freezes the migration set. Shape to refine: before a release publishes, start the previous release by digest, seed it (`scripts/run_web_e2e.py` already captures, restores and runs a suite against an instance), switch to the candidate, and run the suite; a red run is a release that does not publish. Open: which data the seed must hold to mean something (sealed content with media, every application), whether the Helm path (`helm upgrade`, immutable fields) is a second leg, and its runtime cost on a manual dispatch. **Blocked until** the first `1.x` release exists - there is no previous release to start. |
| 420 | **The chart's two-part version is not SemVer to Helm: decide whether that is the trade we want** | infra, chart, release, DONE | **done 2026-10-14 — decided: the two-part version stays** (owner 2026-10-14; the trade and the rejected `.0` are in [ADR 0064](decisions/0064-kubernetes-helm-deployment-target.md)). Measured while implementing `cr-release-pipeline-and-chart-oci`. The chart is published with the release version, `<major>.<minor>` (ADR 0064). Measured with Helm 4.0.1 against a local OCI registry: Helm packages, pushes and installs it by exact version or by digest, but `helm lint` warns *"not a valid SemVerV2"*, and with no `--version` or a range Helm finds no tag at all (*"unable to locate any tags"*), because its tag listing skips every tag that is not three-part SemVer. The documented install is by verified digest, so nothing an operator is told to do breaks; what is lost is "the latest chart" and version ranges (and with them any tool that resolves charts that way). The alternative is a chart `version` of `<major>.<minor>.0` (`-SNAPSHOT` kept) beside an `appVersion` of `<major>.<minor>`, at the cost of the chart and the release no longer sharing one name. Owner's call; nothing is blocked on it. |

| 423 | **Copy a node to another place ("Copy here")** | core, web | **refined 2026-10-14 → cr-copy-node** ([uc.platform.copy-node](features/platform/use-cases/uc.platform.copy-node.md), [ADR 0082](decisions/0082-a-node-and-everything-under-it-exports-as-a-plaintext-zip-and-imports-under-the-importer.md) decision 11; owner: the client re-uploads - no server copy, no dedup; the same space only; members only; every kind through the export/import hooks with the losses named in the dialog; `Name (2)`; "Copy to…" beside "Move to…" with the folder browser's "Copy here" and in the selection bar of cr-select-nodes). Was: **inbox** (owner 2026-10-14, raised beside the move browser). A move re-wraps ONE key (ADR 0080 decision 1); a copy is a new node, so it needs a new content key for the copy and for **every node below it**, and every blob below either copied in the store (a server-side copy plus a client re-wrap of each `K_blob`) or downloaded and uploaded again. Across scopes (a personal folder into a shared space) the epoch keys differ too. It counts against the tenant's quota (ADR 0082), and the reclaim work list (ADR 0062) assumes one owner per stored object, which a deduplicated copy would break. A small item (one file) is cheap; a folder of Memories is a client-side re-encryption of everything in it. Questions: files only first, or every kind; server-side object copy vs re-upload; the same browser as the move (a second button) - yes, if it comes. |

| 426 | **uc.memory.upload says a hard failure cancels the rest of its batch; core's queue has no such rule** | web, core, memories, docs, DONE | **done 2026-10-14** (owner: strike the sentence; the queue lets the rest run, now pinned by `queue.test.ts`) (measured 2026-10-14 while building `cr-upload-tray-one-list-per-destination`, whose context named the rule as one the queue keeps). Not a tray question: the tray groups batches and never changes what the queue runs. |

| 435 | **A share someone sent me can be kept in my account — and opened from every client** | sharing, account, native, applications, future, NEW | **inbox** (owner 2026-10-15, raised while discussing separate native apps per application; explicitly *for the future*, not V1). Questions to decide fresh: (1) **what is stored** — the link plus its `#k=` key, sealed under my account key, so the operator still sees only ciphertext; (2) **what the operator learns** — today a guest is anonymous, a kept share links *my account* to *that share* on the server, which is new metadata (must the record carry the share id in the clear at all?); (3) **a share from another instance** — the record is then a pointer to a foreign origin, and a client that talks to several instances opens it there as a guest; (4) it is the clean way into a native app: the browser's "open in the app" banner otherwise hands the key over a custom URL scheme any app can claim, and a share kept in the account needs no such handover. |

| 445 | **The store setting's cache can put a stale resolution back after an invalidate** | backend, blobstore, admin, concurrency, DONE | **done** (2026-10-15). `StoreSettings` (`backend/core/src/main/kotlin/me/raddatz/veyq/core/blobstore/StoreSetting.kt`) now tags its cached resolution with the generation it was resolved under and serves it only while that generation is current; `invalidate()` advances the generation instead of clearing the field — the guard `StoredCapabilitySwitches` already had. Bite: `StoreSettingsCacheSliceTest` parks a reader inside `find()` after it read the old row, commits a new row and invalidates while it is parked, then releases it; on the old code the next `current()` answered the old endpoint (red: `expected http://new.invalid:9000 but was http://old.invalid:9000`), green with the fix. The two memos downstream need no generation: `SwitchingBlobStore.built` and `AppShellRoutes.emitted` are checked against the current resolution (by identity) and the current store origin on every call, so a racing stale write costs the next call a rebuild, never a stale store or header; each says so at its field. |
| 446 | ~~**A member who lost the Emergency Kit cannot get a new one while signed in**~~ | account, crypto, emergency-kit, security, docs, DONE | **done 2026-10-15** (cr-use-area-content phase 8): the still-signed-in window is said in the Use pages [Your Emergency Kit](../site/src/content/docs/getting-started/emergency-kit.mdx) and [Lost Emergency Kit](../site/src/content/docs/help/lost-emergency-kit.mdx), EN and DE; security-model lists no lost-kit cost, so it needed no line. **Decided 2026-10-15: option (c)** - the gap is accepted and the user docs say so; nothing is built. What is left is the doc sentence on the still-signed-in window in the Use page *Your Emergency Kit* (`site/src/content/docs/getting-started/emergency-kit.mdx`, with its `docs/` sources named), owned by cr-use-area-content, and a line in [security-model](crosscutting/security-model.md) if it lists what a lost kit costs. Option (a) is not rejected, only not now: it reopens as its own refine (an ADR before any CR) if the owner wants a repair path. The row stays until the doc sentence has landed. Origin: (found 2026-10-15 writing the Use page *Your Emergency Kit*; the owner asked whether a signed-in member can issue a new kit - they cannot). **Measured 2026-10-15 on `main`.** The kit PDF has exactly three writers. Two are in `web/src`: account birth, `EmergencyKitSave` in `views/TenantJoinView.vue:329`, and after a sign-in address change, `EmergencyKitSave` in `views/ConfirmEmailChangeView.vue:139`; the third is `admin-web/src/features/setup/EmergencyKitSave.vue` for the Server Owner at first-run setup. `renderKitPdf` has no other caller (`grep -rn "renderKitPdf\|saveKitPdf" web/src admin-web/src`). The email-change kit is a **re-print of the same Secret Key**, not a new one: it is shown only after `checkColdCredential` passes with the password and the Secret Key typed, pasted or imported from the old kit (`ConfirmEmailChangeView.vue:7-12`), so it needs the very thing that was lost. A **new** Secret Key exists nowhere: `secretKeyProofPublicKey` is a field only of the two birth requests (`contracts/api/tenants.openapi.yaml:2642`, `contracts/api/setup.openapi.yaml:274`), and `buildAccount` mints a Secret Key only at birth (`packages/crypto/src/accountBirth.ts:58`). **Why a signed-in member is stuck.** The Secret Key is never at rest on a device ([ADR 0005](decisions/0005-unlock-methods-and-device-trust.md) §3: it "lives only in the Emergency Kit"), and the Master Key a device holds does not yield it ([ADR 0054](decisions/0054-password-recovery-two-factor-release.md) Context fact 3: "The Master Key is not the Secret Key"; a linked device holds the former without ever seeing the latter, [ADR 0059](decisions/0059-device-linking.md) §10). Key replacement needs it too ([uc.account.rotate-keys](features/account/use-cases/uc.account.rotate-keys.md) § E2: no kit, rotation is impossible). Consequence: the member keeps working through any unlocked device, and **loses everything the day they forget the password** - the `RECOVERY` wrap opens by the Secret Key alone ([en.account.key-model](features/account/enablers/en.account.key-model.md) § Recovery), and there is no escrow by design (ADR 0054 Decision 2). The Use page states only the front half today: *If it is lost* in `site/src/content/docs/getting-started/emergency-kit.mdx:31-36` covers losing password **and** kit, not the still-signed-in member's window. **The design tension.** Anything that mints a new Secret Key from a signed-in session lets whoever holds only an unlocked device make their own recovery factor: a new Secret Key re-derives the `PASSWORD` floor, the `RECOVERY` wrap and the proof key, so a thief with a phished device-link grant ([ADR 0059](decisions/0059-device-linking.md)) could replace the Secret Key the owner holds and lock the owner out. That is exactly what rotate-keys' kit requirement guards ("the Secret Key is precisely what the attacker does not hold, so it is what the gate asks for", uc.account.rotate-keys E1; "the gate is the feature"). A re-issue path must not reopen it. **Options and costs.** **(a) A new Secret Key gated by password plus something else.** Needs a new contract and endpoint: replace the proof public key, the `PASSWORD` wrap and the `RECOVERY` wrap in one transaction (a partial apply is a lockout, ADR 0054 Decision 3), probably signed under something the thief lacks. The password alone is not enough: it is the one factor a phisher who also holds an unlocked device can have. The candidates for the second factor are address control (the mailbox, which ADR 0054 itself calls weaker than the account it protects, so it cannot be the only extra) or a delay with notice to every other session and the old address (a time-lock the owner can cancel). The old Secret Key stays valid until the swap, and every printed old kit dies at it, which must be said. Cost: a new recovery surface in the most sensitive place, a new ADR, server enforcement of the extra factor, and a threat-model row for the delay window. **(b) A re-issued kit from the unlocked Master Key. Not possible**: the Secret Key is an independent random 32 bytes, not derived from the Master Key or the password (`v1.randomBytes(32)`), so nothing a device holds can reproduce it, and wrapping the Secret Key under the Master Key to store it server-side would be escrow of the one secret the design keeps off every device (ADR 0005 §3) and would turn the `RECOVERY` wrap into a password-less path for any unlocked device. Ruled out by the invariants, listed so it is not proposed again. **(c) Accept the gap and say so in the user docs.** No code. Cost: a member who lost the kit while signed in has no repair and one bad day away from total loss; the docs must say it before they lose the kit, not after, and point at the one real mitigation that exists - copy the kit while you still hold it. This also makes the gap part of the honest *What holds today* story (no doc may claim more than is built). **Next:** the owner picks (a), (c) or a staged (c) now, (a) later; (a) is a refine with the owner into an ADR before any CR (pipeline step 2). The Use page *Your Emergency Kit* can ship under (c) either way, with the sentence on the still-signed-in window added. |

| 449 | **admin-web fails silently once its access token has expired** | admin-web, auth, ux, NEW | **inbox** (found 2026-10-15 in the browser pass of #447). Left open about a quarter of an hour, Server → Capabilities' switch answered `401` twice (`PUT /api/instance/capabilities/core.remember-device`) and the panel showed nothing: no message, no change. `handle401` pushes `login`, and `admin-web/src/router.ts:48` sends `login` back to `console` while `session.accessToken` is still set, so the redirect never lands; admin-web has no refresh on a `401`. Every panel uses the same `handle401`, so this is the console's, not the capability panel's. Wanted: refresh once and retry, or clear the session before the redirect; the panel tests pin only the push. |
| 450 | **A sign-in made while a device switch is off** | web, account, capabilities, ux, NEW | **inbox, decide** (found 2026-10-15 in the browser pass of #447). Three things on the sign-in screen of a device whose wrap is withheld (ADR 0088 decision 5). (1) **The remembered device's cookie.** The toggle is hidden while `core.remember-device.passwordless` is off, so the sign-in asks for a `SESSION` cookie (`LoginView.vue`: `remember` picks the scope) and replaces the device's persistent one. Switched back on, the device opens directly only until the browser closes; after a restart it lands on the sign-in with the first-sign-in hint, and a sign-in there with the toggle left off makes it a trusted device and drops its DEVICE_KEY wrap. Read from the code: the browser pane cannot restart its browser. Decide: keep the persistent scope for a device that keeps its DEVICE_KEY record, or accept it and say so in `uc.admin.switch-capabilities` A2, which says switching back on restores the device. (2) **The hint before the email.** The lookup is keyed on the entered email and the field starts empty even on a restored session, so both tiers read "first time on this device" until the email is typed; prefilling it from the device's one local record would fix that. (3) **A second session.** Typing the email drops the restored session (`watch(email)`), so the unlock is a new login and the old family stays listed until it expires (seven after the pass). (3) is not the switch's: it holds for any device that reaches the sign-in with a live session. |
